# BrainEngine map

This map lists every member of the `BrainEngine` interface in gbrain (TypeScript), grouped by family. For each member it gives the signature, what it does, the SQL it runs, where `PGLiteEngine` and `PostgresEngine` differ, and the tests that cover it.

- Reference: `garrytan/gbrain` at commit `8bbfd0b5e8` (master, 2026-11-16), facts refreshed to master `116d737413` (2026-11-24). Every `path:line` citation is relative to the gbrain repo root. Line numbers on facts that did not change are at `8bbfd0b5e8`; new or changed facts cite `116d737413`.
- Interface: `src/core/engine.ts:920-3421` at `116d737413`. Engines: `src/core/pglite-engine.ts` (3,374 lines), `src/core/postgres-engine.ts` (3,504 lines), plus `src/core/pglite-engine/*` and `src/core/postgres-engine/*`. Shared SQL: `src/core/engine-sql/*.ts` (about 19,500 lines with the engines).
- Method: each family was read by a separate research pass. Counts are per declaration. Line numbers were read at the commit above. Test coverage was found by grep, not by running the suite. Items a pass could not verify say so in place.

Confirmed build direction: the first build target is a Rust storage engine that implements `BrainEngine` as a third engine inside gbrain, next to PGLite and Postgres (option B, confirmed by Garry). The binding is still open; a napi-rs native module is the leading option. At `116d737413` a `RustEngine` class implements the interface (`src/core/rust-engine.ts:93`, `kind` `'rust'`). `src/core/rust-engine/members.ts` classifies all 360 members, and a member the store does not serve rejects with `engine_capability_missing` before any work. The last two sections of this map serve that target:

- [What a Rust engine must do to pass engine-parity](#what-a-rust-engine-must-do-to-pass-engine-parity) has the per-method parity notes for `test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts`, and a short account of the harness suite in `test/engine-parity/` that covers most members.
- [Adding a third engine and binding options](#adding-a-third-engine-and-binding-options) covers engine selection and napi-rs vs sidecar vs WASM vs `bun:ffi` for Bun.

| Section | Interface lines | Members |
|---|---|---|
| [Lifecycle, Pages and Search](#lifecycle-pages-and-search) | 920-1426 | 92 (incl. the `kind` and `capabilities` properties) |

| [Tags, Timeline, Chronicle, Ontology, Raw data, Files, Takes, Dream and Contradictions](#tags-timeline-chronicle-ontology-raw-data-files-takes-dream-and-contradictions) | 2070-2450 | 50 |
| [Facts, Segmented storage, Versions, Stats, Sync, Config and Migration support](#facts-segmented-storage-versions-stats-sync-config-and-migration-support) | 2451-3421 | 164 |

| [Adding a third engine and binding options](#adding-a-third-engine-and-binding-options) | engine selection | n/a |

The four family sections count 360 declarations (`src/core/engine.ts:920-3421`). The parity section counts 358 callable members; the difference is the two properties, `kind` and `capabilities`.

## Headline findings

- The SQL is mostly shared. Migrated storage domains keep their SQL once in `src/core/engine-sql/` and both engines delegate to it. Engine differences are in the wrappers: Postgres adds statement timeouts, JIT-off reads, reserved connections, read-only transactions, real cancellation and an exact-scan vector fallback. PGLite adds planner-stat refreshes and a single-process lock.
- Some engine differences are visible to callers. `searchKeywordChunks` ignores `type`, `types`, `where` and `exclude_slugs` on PGLite. The `stale` flag on keyword results is computed on PGLite and a constant `false` on Postgres. A Rust engine has to pick one behavior per method; the Postgres behavior is the safer reference.
- Several interface doc comments disagree with the code (`addLinksBatch`, `getEffectiveDates`, `createVersion`, `setPageAliases`, `updatePageContextualRetrievalState`, `insertFacts`). A Rust engine should follow the code and fix the doc first.
- `test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts` covers only 60 of 358 members and needs `DATABASE_URL`. 46 of its calls are raw Postgres SQL through `executeRaw`. A Rust engine cannot pass it unchanged; see the parity section.

---

## Lifecycle, Pages and Search

Scope: `BrainEngine` interface members in `src/core/engine.ts:920-1426` at `116d737413` (interface opens at :920, the `kind` discriminator is at :922, `capabilities` at :923, and the range ends before the `// Chunks` block at :1428; `searchKeywordChunks` is declared later and is not in this range). Engines: `src/core/pglite-engine.ts` (PGLite, 3374 lines) and `src/core/postgres-engine.ts` (Postgres, 3504 lines). Domain SQL lives in `src/core/engine-sql/*.ts` and is shared by both engines.

Member count: 92 interface members in scope (2 properties, 90 methods). Rows for 40 of these members keep `engine.ts` lines at `8bbfd0b5e8` in the tables below, and rows for the other 52 members cite `116d737413`. Optional members: `holdSchemaMigrationLock?` (engine.ts:939 at `116d737413`), `findDuplicatePage?` (:1013), `findDuplicatePages?` (:1017). Every other member is required. Note `initSchema()` is declared with no arguments (:937 at `116d737413`), but Postgres takes an optional `{ embedding? }` (postgres-engine.ts:576).

### Plumbing used by these methods

| File | Role | Key lines |
|---|---|---|
| `src/core/engine-factory.ts` | `createEngine(config)` calls `constructEngine`, registers the chat usage sink for the engine, and wraps `engine.disconnect` so the sink is deregistered before the original disconnect. When shadow mode is on for the brain (`shadowStateFor(config)`), it returns the engine wrapped by `attachShadow` (`engine-factory.ts:33-36`). Every production engine passes through here. | :10-31 (wrap at :24-28) |
| `src/core/engine-sql/executor.ts` | `SqlExecutor` contract: `dialect`, `capabilities`, `query(sql, params)`, `run(fragment)`, `executeRaw` (rows only), `unsafe` (raw driver, no gauge), `transaction(fn)`. Results are `{ rows, affectedRows }` (EO18). Errors pass through unchanged. `DialectCapabilities`: `maxBindParamsPerStatement` (PGLite 30,000, Postgres 65,533, `dialect-pglite.ts:26`, `dialect-postgres.ts:54`), `transactionAdvisoryLocks` (Postgres true), `probesEmbeddingCast` (Postgres true). | interface :110-123; capabilities :87-108; `jsonbParam` :133 |
| `src/core/engine-sql/dialect-pglite.ts` | Wraps one PGLite handle. `pgliteQuery` pre-checks the abort signal, then races the statement against abort. The statement itself is not cancelled (WASM has no cancel). `PGLITE_CAPABILITIES` at :25-29. `transaction` calls `db.transaction` and returns a new executor over the tx handle. | :42-56 query; :58-76 executor; :71-74 transaction |
| `src/core/engine-sql/dialect-postgres.ts` | Wraps one postgres.js handle (pool, reserved conn or tx). `query`/`run` go through `runUnsafe` with `prepare: true, simple: false` (named prepared statements; PgBouncer `prepare:false` still honored). `executeRaw` bumps the `raw` checkout gauge. `unsafe` is a bare `conn.unsafe`. `transaction` uses `readWriteTransactions(conn).begin`. `POSTGRES_CAPABILITIES` at :129-133. `jsonbParam` binds via `conn.json` (:137). | :144-170; query :145-146; executeRaw :155-162; unsafe :163-165; transaction :166-168 |
| `src/core/engine-sql/fragment.ts` | `sqlFragment` tagged template builds `{strings, values}`. Nested fragments splice, `trustedSql(text)` splices constant identifier or ORDER BY text, anything else becomes a positional parameter. `renderFragment` numbers `$1..$n` only at render time. `joinFragments` for lists. No identifier escaping: identifiers come from constant allowlists. | `sqlFragment` :46; `trustedSql` :75; `joinFragments` :80; `renderFragment` :94 |
| `src/core/engine-sql/brands.ts` | Read brands. `ScopedRead` (:174) is an executor branded as running inside `withScopedReadTransaction` on Postgres (RLS `app.scopes` bound when enabled); `scopedRead()` (:184) is an identity cast used only on the handle that helper yields (PGLite brands its own executor). `LegacyUnscopedRead` (:177) marks reads that ran unscoped on master (EO4 inventory), created by `unscopedExecutor(exec, reason)` (:193). Brand keys are checked by `scripts/check-engine-sql-brands.ts`. | :174-196 |
| `src/core/engine-sql/normalize.ts` | `compileRowNormalizer(kinds)` converts only declared columns. Kinds: `jsonb` (parse text), `bigint` (Number), `date` (Date), `vector` (`parseEmbedding`), `text[]`. Used e.g. by the domain-bank row mapper (`last_retrieved_at: 'date'`) in `engine-sql/pages.ts`. | ColumnKind :212; compile :227-236 |

Engine-side wiring for all methods below: each engine exposes an `engineSql` getter that builds a fresh executor over the current handle on every access (never cached), so `transaction()` clones (created via `Object.create(this)`) run domain SQL inside the transaction. Postgres: `postgres-engine.ts:317-325`. PGLite: `pglite-engine.ts:794` `engineSql` getter.

### Lifecycle

| Member (engine.ts) | Signature | What it does | PGLite (`pglite-engine.ts`) | Postgres (`postgres-engine.ts`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kind` (:832) | `readonly kind: 'postgres' \| 'pglite'` | Discriminator for migrations and consumers. The interface also admits `'rust'` (`engine.ts:922`). | `kind = 'pglite' as const` (:739) | `kind = 'postgres' as const` (:226) |
| `capabilities` (`engine.ts:923`) | `readonly capabilities: EngineCapabilities` | The set of capability names the engine reports (`engine-capabilities.ts`), so a caller asks for a capability instead of branching on `kind`. | `capabilities = capabilitiesForEngineKind('pglite')` (`pglite-engine.ts:777`) | `capabilities = capabilitiesForEngineKind('postgres')` (`postgres-engine.ts:268`) |
| `connect` (:835) | `connect(config: EngineConfig): Promise<void>` | Opens the datastore. | `connect` :799 → `_connectWithRootRegistration` (registers managed filesystem engine) → `_connectInternal` (:887). Takes a kernel file lock (`acquireLock(dataDir)`, pglite-lock.ts) for file-backed DBs, then `PGlite.create({dataDir})`, configures codecs. In-memory: optional `GBRAIN_PGLITE_SNAPSHOT` restore (skips schema replay). Wasm-abort on a persistent dir triggers `attemptWalRepairAndRetry` (in-place WAL repair, with consent-gated refusal). Stores `_savedConfig` for `reconnect`. | `connect` :433. Two modes: (a) instance pool when `config.poolSize` is set: own `postgres(url)` with `max` from `resolvePoolSize`, `idle_timeout 20`, session timeouts (`statement_timeout`, `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`), prepare per URL, `ConnectionManager` wrapper; (b) module singleton via `db.connect` (db.ts), shared with other engines. `types: { bigint: postgres.BigInt }` on instance pools. |
| `disconnect` (:836) | `disconnect(): Promise<void>` | Tears the datastore down. Idempotent (memoized call). | `disconnect` :1050. Sets requested flag, awaits pending connect, then `_closeInternal` (:1088): runs the `_beforeDisconnect` barrier, sets `_db = null`, `stopAndDrain` on DB work, drain background work, checkpoint (failure warns and retains ownership), `db.close()`, release kernel lock. Close watchdog arms SIGTERM/SIGKILL timers. A failed close poisons the engine (`_closePoison`) and retains the lock. | `disconnect` :516 → `disconnectInternal` (:526): runs `_beforeDisconnect` stops only when a pool, instance handle or module singleton is open (`postgres-engine.ts:589-590`), logs disconnect audit, drains background work, closes `ConnectionManager` (direct pool), `db.endPoolBounded(this._sql)` (bounded wait, #1972). Module-singleton teardown only by the engine that created it (`_ownsModuleSingleton`). |
| `reconnect` (:845) | `reconnect(ctx?: { error?: unknown }): Promise<void>` | Recovers a dropped connection from the config captured at `connect()`. Callers must use it instead of disconnect + bare connect (#2034). | `reconnect` :1139: no-op if never connected or in-memory (`database_path` unset, to preserve state). File-backed: `disconnect()` then `connect(savedConfig)`. | `reconnect` :2859 (far from the other lifecycle methods). Instance pool: build-then-swap (new pool proven live before old is ended); `_reconnecting` guard; `ctx.error` classified by `isConnectionEndedError` to audit `reap_detected` vs `reconnect_other` (`logPoolRecovery`). Module singleton: `db.connect(savedConfig)` then refresh `ConnectionManager` read pool. |
| `initSchema` (:846) | `initSchema(): Promise<void>` (Postgres: `opts?: {embedding?}`) | Creates or replays the schema and applies migrations. | `initSchema` :1147. When a snapshot was loaded, schema and migrations are already applied, and it only records the schema version through `noteSchemaVersion` (`pglite-engine.ts:1188`). Dims/model from embedded settings, gateway, or defaults; stored embedding identity wins. Runs under `withSchemaMigrationLock` (in-process serialization, since no `holdSchemaMigrationLock`): `assertSchemaNotAhead` (#7776), `applyForwardReferenceBootstrap`, `db.exec(getPGLiteSchema(dims, model))`, `runMigrations(this)`, then `registerManagedFilesystemEngine`. No `verifySchema` or zombie-index sweep. | `initSchema` :576. Pool = `connectionManager.ddl()` when dual-pool, else `this.sql`. Dims/model as PGLite. Runs under `withSchemaMigrationLock` (:619): `assertSchemaNotAhead` (:620), `applyForwardReferenceBootstrap(conn)`, `retrySchemaReplayOnDeadlock(withSchemaLockTimeout(conn, conn.unsafe(schemaSql)))` (:642; session `lock_timeout` on a reserved backend, #5227), `runMigrations`, `verifySchema(this)` (self-heals missing columns), `dropZombieIndexes(this)` (best-effort, invalid HNSW indexes). Clears shared types if anything changed. |
| `holdSchemaMigrationLock?` (:848) | `holdSchemaMigrationLock?<T>(fn: (assertHeld) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>` | GBRA-467 cross-process lock (key 42). Optional: when absent, `schema-migration-lock.ts:22-30` serializes in-process only. | Not implemented. The `schema-migration-lock.ts` wrapper serializes within the process (PGLite datastore lock admits one process). | `holdSchemaMigrationLock` :687. Uses the DDL pool if `max>1`, else the statement pool if `max>1`, else a session lock (`holdSchemaMigrationSessionLock`). Reserved-connection form (`holdSchemaMigrationTransactionLock`) polls `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock(42)` (init-schema-lock.ts:189-203). Session form uses `pg_advisory_lock(42)` (init-schema-lock.ts:130-137) and `pg_advisory_unlock` (:237). |
| `transaction` (:849) | `transaction<T>(fn: (engine) => Promise<T>, opts?: TransactionOptions): Promise<T>` | Runs `fn` on a clone engine bound to a transaction; nested calls become savepoints. | `transaction` :1209. Checks only `opts.signal` (abort before start). Uses `db.transaction`, runs under `_dbWork.admit` + `PgliteCheckpointGuard.runOutermost` (checkpoint blocked during tx), composable tx handle, `withHeldPageKeys`. Ignores `readOnly` and `pipelineEarly`. A nested call on a tx clone runs inside the same tx. | `transaction` :716 → `transactionOn` (:726). `readOnly` opens `BEGIN READ ONLY` (not wrapped by `readWriteTransactions`). `checkoutGauge.acquire('tx')` (top-level only). Options `pipelineEarly`, `pipelineFirst`, `signal` (abort via `withTransactionAbort`), commit checks (`withCommitChecks`), held page keys, `holdSearchConnection`. Clone sets `sql` to the tx handle; nested call uses `conn.begin(run)` (savepoint). |
| `transactionDirect` (:851) | `transactionDirect<T>(fn, opts?)` | Short control transaction on the direct route. Honors nesting. | `transactionDirect` :1225: `return this.transaction(fn, opts)` (no direct route exists). | `transactionDirect` :720: when dual-pool and top-level, uses `connectionManager.ddl()` (direct pool), else `this.sql`; then `transactionOn`. |
| `registerBeforeDisconnect` (:853) | `registerBeforeDisconnect(stop: () => Promise<void>): () => void` | Registers a mandatory resident-consumer stop barrier that runs before datastore or pool teardown. Returns an unregister function. | `registerBeforeDisconnect` :753. Adds to `_beforeDisconnect` Set; barrier runs at the start of `_closeInternal`, before `_db = null`. | `registerBeforeDisconnect` :230. Same Set. Barrier runs first in `disconnectInternal` (:524). |
| `withReservedConnection` (:859) | `withReservedConnection<T>(fn: (conn: ReservedConnection) => Promise<T>, opts?: { route?: 'ordinary'; selfContained?: boolean }): Promise<T>` | Runs `fn` on one dedicated backend. Release automatic. `route: 'ordinary'` skips the direct pool. `selfContained` lets a one-connection pool lend the connection. | `withReservedConnection` :1196 (`opts` not accepted). No pool: passes the single `db` through. `conn.executeRaw` only; no `transaction` member. | `withReservedConnection` :763. Picks the direct DDL pool when dual-pool and not `route:'ordinary'`, guarded by `tryAcquirePoolLongHold` (permit; throws `PoolCapacityError` if none). Gauge `reserved` acquired before `pool.reserve()`. `conn.executeRaw` uses `reserved.unsafe` (AbortSignal ignored). `conn.transaction` = `transactionOn(reservedTransactions(reserved))`. Release in `finally` (double-release tolerant). |

### Pages CRUD

Shared SQL for most methods is in `src/core/engine-sql/pages.ts` (both engines call `pagesImpl.*` with `this.engineSql`). Differences are in how the read/write is scoped and wrapped (noted per row).

| Member (engine.ts) | Signature (compact) | What it does and SQL | Engine differences |
|---|---|---|---|
| `getPage` (:869) | `getPage(slug, opts?: GetPageOpts): Promise<Page \| null>` | Returns `readPageSnapshot(...).page`, or null. Soft-deleted rows return null unless `includeDeleted`. | PGLite :1230 calls `readPageSnapshot` directly. Postgres :850 same contract. |
| `readPageSnapshot` (:870) | `readPageSnapshot(slug, opts?: PageSnapshotOptions): Promise<PageSnapshot \| null>` | Canonical page snapshot (`src/core/page-state/snapshot.ts:66-120`): one page row by slug. With `resolveAlias`, falls through `slug_aliases` to the canonical page. Ordering `(p.slug = $1) DESC, (p.source_id = $n) DESC, p.source_id ASC LIMIT 1`. Filters: `deleted_at` unless `includeDeleted`; `requireLiveSource` (sources not archived); `requireUnambiguous` (`count(*) OVER ()`). Joins `sources.incarnation`, tags (`jsonb_agg`), fact-withdrawal and fact-purge aggregates (`fact_purges`). | PGLite :1234 `executeRaw` directly, no scope. Postgres :854 runs inside `withScopedReadTransaction(sourceIds, sourceId)` with `prepare: true`; it uses JIT off and an alwaysTransaction wrap only when `resolveAlias && excludePrivate` (#6276). |
| `readPageSnapshotsBatch` (:875) | `readPageSnapshotsBatch(refs: {slug, sourceId}[], opts?: {maxBytes?, includeDeleted?, absentIsNull?}): Promise<PageSnapshotBatch>` | Exact-ref batch (`src/core/page-snapshot-batch.ts:42-60`). `VALUES`-style wanted list with cumulative bytes (`SUM ... ROWS UNBOUNDED PRECEDING AND 1 PRECEDING`) and returns the longest prefix whose bodies fit `maxBytes`. Same snapshot joins as `readPageSnapshot`. | PGLite :1238 `executeRaw`. Postgres :861 binds the batch's distinct `sourceIds` for RLS through `withScopedReadTransaction`. |
| `lockPageKeys` (:877) | `lockPageKeys(keys: readonly PageKey[]): Promise<void>` | Holds exact page identities (including absent rows) through commit. Throws unless called on a transaction engine. Takes only keys not already held (sorted acquisition via `lockUnheldPageKeys`, `page-state/guards.ts:114-119`). | Both engines identical in shape: PGLite :1242 (`lockUnheldPageKeys`), Postgres :867 (`lockUnheldPageKeys`). |
| `putPage` (:891) | `putPage(slug, page: PageInput, opts?: PageWriteOptions): Promise<Page>` | Validates slug, runs in `transaction(write)` (or inline when already in a page tx and `opts.inline`). In order: `lockPageKeys`; revision precondition check when `expectedRevision`/`force` set (`readPageSnapshot includeDeleted`); `assertOriginUnclaimed(revive:true)` (`persistence/origin-guard.ts:112`); then `pages.ts:103-204` `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (source_id, slug) DO UPDATE` with `deleted_at = NULL` (revival), inside a `WITH ins AS (INSERT ... RETURNING ...), bound AS (UPDATE write_gate_holds ...)` statement so a page created at a slug takes over the unbound quarantine holds waiting for that slug (`HOLD_BIND_CTE`, `engine-sql/hold-binding.ts`, used at `pages.ts:201`), `COALESCE` preservation for `effective_date`, `import_filename`, `source_path`, `source_kind`, `source_uri`, `ingested_via`, `ingested_at`, contextual retrieval columns when provided, `chunker_version` via `bodyWriteChunkVersion`, `RETURNING ... `. Blank-body guard: if the incoming body is blank and not `allowEmptyOverwrite`, reads the prior row and refuses to blank a non-empty or segmented page (pages.ts:116-133). | PGLite :1262 passes a `reread` callback to `pagesImpl.putPage` for the PGLite edge case where `RETURNING` yields zero rows. Postgres :887 passes none, and maps origin unique violations through `classifyOriginUniqueViolation`. |
| `findDuplicatePage?` (:922) | `findDuplicatePage?(sourceId, opts: {hash, frontmatterId?, excludeSlug?}): Promise<{slug,id} \| null>` | Import dedup pre-check (#1309). `pages.ts:57-80`: `WHERE source_id = $ AND deleted_at IS NULL AND (content_hash = $hash OR (frontmatter->>'id' = $fmId AND $fmId IS NOT NULL))`, excluding `excludeSlug`. Orders frontmatter-id match first, then `id`. `LIMIT 1`. | PGLite :1252 uses `scopedRead(this.engineSql)` with no scope transaction. Postgres :877 runs in `withScopedReadTransaction(undefined, sourceId)`. |
| `findDuplicatePages?` (:926) | `findDuplicatePages?(sourceId, inputs[]): Promise<Array<{slug,id}\|null>>` | Batched version (GBRA-75 w10). `pages.ts:82-101`: `jsonb_to_recordset` of inputs joined to pages, `DISTINCT ON (k.n)`, same predicate and ordering. Returns one entry per input, in order. | PGLite :1258; Postgres :883. Same SQL. |
| `deletePage` (:952) | `deletePage(slug, opts?: {sourceId?}): Promise<void>` | Hard delete. `pages.ts:206-208`: `DELETE FROM pages WHERE slug = $ AND source_id = $` (source defaults to `'default'`). Cascades via FKs only. No scrub of name/feedback stores. Used by purge, sync per-slug fallback, and test teardown. | PGLite :1276 and Postgres :901 both call `pagesImpl.deletePage(this.engineSql, ...)`. |
| `deletePages` (:989) | `deletePages(slugs: string[], opts: {sourceId}): Promise<string[]>` | Batch hard delete, one transaction. `pages.ts:221-247`: `DELETE ... WHERE slug = ANY($::text[]) AND source_id = $ RETURNING slug`, then scrubs by slug: `scrubPurgedSlugs`, `scrubPurgedNameStores`, `scrubPurgedSlugHolds`, then `scrubPurgedSlugStores`. The holds go first because the slug-store scrub removes the rename record the hold sweep reads. The delete returns `slug, id`, and the secret occurrences of the deleted page ids (`pageSecretOccurrences`) are passed to the slug-store scrub (`pages.ts:228-250`, at `116d737413`). Throws above `DELETE_BATCH_SIZE` (500, `engine-constants.ts:25`). Empty input returns `[]`. | Same SQL on both engines via `this.engineSql` (PGLite :1286, Postgres :911). |
| `resolveSlugsByPaths` (:1007) | `resolveSlugsByPaths(paths: string[], opts: {sourceId}): Promise<Map<string,string>>` | Path to slug map. `pages.ts:249-274`: `SELECT slug, source_path, deleted_at IS NULL AS live ... WHERE source_path = ANY($) AND source_id = $`. Live rows win over tombstones; several live rows resolve via `pickCanonicalOrigin`; ambiguous paths are omitted. Throws above 500 items. | Both use `unscopedExecutor(...)` (LegacyUnscopedRead, "unscoped on master"): PGLite :1294, Postgres :919. |
| `softDeletePage` (:1017) | `softDeletePage(slug, opts?: {sourceId?}): Promise<{slug} \| null>` | `pages.ts:276-295`: `UPDATE pages SET deleted_at = now() WHERE slug = $ AND deleted_at IS NULL [AND source_id = $] RETURNING slug`. Null if nothing flipped. No cascade. Note: `sourceId` has no `'default'` fallback; when omitted, the predicate matches the slug in every source. | PGLite :1301, Postgres :926 (both `pagesImpl.softDeletePage(this.engineSql, ...)`). |
| `softDeletePages` (:1050) | `softDeletePages(slugs, opts: {sourceId}): Promise<string[]>` | Batch soft delete (#4587). `pages.ts:297-310`: `UPDATE ... SET deleted_at = now() WHERE slug = ANY(...) AND source_id = $ AND deleted_at IS NULL RETURNING slug`. Empty returns `[]`; over 500 throws. Re-runs never refresh `deleted_at` (72h clock). | PGLite :1314, Postgres :937. Same SQL. |
| `restorePage` (:1056) | `restorePage(slug, opts?: {sourceId?}): Promise<boolean>` | `pages.ts:312-327`: one CTE statement. `UPDATE pages SET deleted_at = NULL, updated_at = now() WHERE slug = $ AND deleted_at IS NOT NULL [AND source_id]`, plus `DELETE FROM op_checkpoints` for the owed-purge record (GST-2766). Runs inside `transaction` after `assertRevivalUnclaimed` (`persistence/origin-guard.ts:156`). | PGLite :1318, Postgres :941 (same wrapping). |
| `purgeDeletedPages` (:1071) | `purgeDeletedPages(olderThanHours, opts?: {dryRun?, excludeIds?, onlyIds?}): Promise<{slugs, count, pages?}>` | Hard purge after the soft-delete window (`pages.ts:340-425` at `116d737413`). `assertPurgeAge` refuses an age that is not a finite number of 0 or more (`engine-errors.ts:48`, called at `pages.ts:349`), before any statement runs. Hours are floored. Dry run: same WHERE predicate as a `SELECT`, returns `pages`. `onlyIds` limits both the preview and the delete to those page ids under the same holds. Real run, one transaction: collects shas from `page_body_manifest`; `DELETE FROM pages WHERE deleted_at IS NOT NULL AND deleted_at < now() - interval` excluding rows under holds (identity-downgrade checkpoint, `atomRetirementPurgeHold`, `derivedRetirementPurgeHold`, `derivedOriginPurgeHold`) and `excludeIds`; then `expireGonePageFacts`, deletes `page_body_stagings` rows, deletes orphaned `page_segment_texts`, and scrubs per source (purged slugs, name stores, slug holds, then slug stores with the purged pages' secret occurrences). The real run returns `pages` (slug, source_id, deleted_at) as well as `slugs` and `count`. | PGLite :1322 and Postgres :945 both use `this.engineSql`. Same SQL; PG runs it on the pool or a tx clone. |
| `listPages` (:1079) | `listPages(filters?: PageFilters): Promise<Page[]>` | `pages.ts:484-561`. Filters: `type`, `tag` (JOIN tags), `updated_after` or keyset `(updated_at, slug)`, `slugPrefix` (LIKE with escaped metachars, with `slugPrefixAlso` extra literal prefixes matched through `LIKE ANY`, `pages.ts:530-531`), `excludeSlugPrefixes` (`starts_with`), `slugAfter`, `sourceIds`/`sourceId`, `includeDeleted` (default hides), `excludePrivate` (`privatePagesFilterForCaller`), `effective_after`/`effective_before`, frontmatter `where`, `sort` via `PAGE_SORT_SQL` allowlist, `listColumnsOnly`. `LIMIT` default 100. Projects `updated_at_iso` with microseconds for keyset resume. | PGLite :1377 wraps in `plannerRead` (runs `beforePlannerRead` stats settle). Postgres :1009 wraps in `withScopedReadTransaction(filters.sourceIds, filters.sourceId)`. |
| `findPages` (:1081) | `findPages(query: FindPagesQuery): Promise<FoundPages>` | Live pages whose slug, source_path or title matches a find pattern (`engine-sql/page-lookup.ts:43`). | PGLite :1380 via `plannerRead`; Postgres :1012 via `withScopedReadTransaction`. |
| `countPagesBy` (:1083) | `countPagesBy(query: PageCountQuery, groupTypes?: TypeGrouper): Promise<PageCounts>` | Live page counts per (source, type). With `groupTypes`, distinct-slug totals per type group (`page-lookup.ts:72`). | PGLite :1383 via `plannerRead`; Postgres :1015 via `withScopedReadTransaction`. |
| `resolveSlugs` (:1097) | `resolveSlugs(partial: string, opts?: PageReadScope): Promise<string[]>` | Fuzzy slug resolver. `pages.ts:752-~800`: exact slug match first (`deleted_at IS NULL` + scope + privacy). Otherwise up to 5 rows: `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL AND (title % $partial OR slug ILIKE '%partial%')`, `ORDER BY similarity(title, $partial) DESC LIMIT 5` (pg_trgm). Scope: `sourceIds` array wins over `sourceId`; neither = unscoped (back-compat). | Both use `unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql, ...)`: PGLite :1415, Postgres :1056 (direct, no scope tx). Needs pg_trgm on both (PGLite ships its own extension set). |
| `getAllSlugs` (:1108) | `getAllSlugs(opts?: {sourceId?}): Promise<Set<string>>` | `pages.ts:563-572`: `SELECT slug FROM pages` or `... WHERE source_id = $`. No `deleted_at` filter, so soft-deleted slugs are included. | PGLite :1387 `scopedRead(this.engineSql)` direct; Postgres :1019 via `withScopedReadTransaction(undefined, opts.sourceId)`. |
| `listAllPageRefs` (:1127) | `listAllPageRefs(): Promise<Array<{slug, source_id, updated_at: Date}>>` | `pages.ts:574-589`: `SELECT slug, source_id, updated_at FROM pages WHERE deleted_at IS NULL ORDER BY source_id, slug`. Cross-source, unscoped. | Both `unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql, ...)`: PGLite :1391, Postgres :1023. |
| `listAllSources` (:1145) | `listAllSources(opts?: {includeArchived?, localPathOnly?}): Promise<SourceRow[]>` | `engine-sql/sources.ts:14-41`: `SELECT id, name, local_path, last_sync_at, config FROM sources WHERE (includeArchived OR archived IS NOT TRUE) AND (!localPathOnly OR (local_path IS NOT NULL AND config->>'role' IS DISTINCT FROM 'storage_manifest')) ORDER BY (id='default') DESC, id`. Config parsed if text. | Both `unscopedExecutor(...)`: PGLite :1396, Postgres :1028 (`sources` table is not RLS-scoped in master). |
| `updateSourceConfig` (:1165) | `updateSourceConfig(sourceId, patch: Record<string,unknown>): Promise<boolean>` | Atomic JSONB merge (`engine-sql/sources.ts:43-81`): `UPDATE sources SET config = <SOURCE_CONFIG_OBJECT_SQL> || $patch::jsonb WHERE id = $`. The coercion expression normalizes legacy double-encoded or array-shaped config (`gbrain_try_jsonb`, NULL on unparseable). Patch bound via `jsonbParam` (postgres.js `sql.json`); returns `affectedRows > 0`. | PGLite :1400, Postgres :1032. Same SQL via `this.engineSql`. No scope transaction on either. |
| `listPrefixSampledPages` (:1184) | `listPrefixSampledPages(opts: DomainBankSampleOpts): Promise<DomainBankRow[]>` | `pages.ts:619-~700`. CTE `prefix_pages`: `prefix = substring(slug from '^[^/]+/[^/]+')`, `connection_count = COUNT(page_links.to_page_id)`, `last_retrieved_at = GREATEST(p.last_retrieved_at, page_retrievals.last_retrieved_at)`. Filters: `deleted_at IS NULL`, `body_storage = 'inline'`, `excludeSlugs`, source scope. `ROW_NUMBER() ... PARTITION BY prefix` ordered by stale score (if `staleBias`, threshold default 90 days), then `connection_count DESC`, then `slug`. One page per requested prefix. Representative chunk = lowest `chunk_index` with `embedding IS NOT NULL`. | PGLite :1407 `scopedRead(this.engineSql)` direct. Postgres :1046 wraps reads in `withScopedReadTransaction(opts.sourceIds, opts.sourceId)`. |
| `listCorpusSample` (:1195) | `listCorpusSample(opts: CorpusSampleOpts): Promise<DomainBankRow[]>` | `pages.ts:704-750`: optional `SELECT setseed(clamped float8)` on the same executor when `opts.seed` is a number, then `ORDER BY RANDOM() LIMIT n` over the same filters (inline bodies, not deleted, exclusions, source scope). Same representative-chunk subquery. | PGLite :1411 direct. Postgres :1052 passes `alwaysTransaction: typeof opts.seed === 'number'`, so the seed and the select share one transaction and connection. |

### Search

Keyword search shares one builder family in `src/core/search/*` (keyword-chunk-match.ts, keyword-statement.ts, keyword-or-rarity.ts, title-statement.ts, vector-statement.ts, cjk-keyword-sql.ts). Engine code wires them to the executor.

| Member (engine.ts) | Signature (compact) | What it does | PGLite (`pglite-engine.ts`) | Postgres (`postgres-engine.ts`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `searchKeyword` (:1198) | `searchKeyword(query, opts?: SearchOpts): Promise<SearchResult[]>` | Page-grain keyword search, best chunk per page. Shape: `content_chunks.search_vector @@ websearch_to_tsquery(<ftsLang>, $1)`, `ts_rank(...) * keyword phrase factor * sourceFactorCase` (source boosts, `detail` gate), `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY page_id ...)` per page, `innerLimit = min(limit*3, cap*3)`, visibility clause (soft-delete, archived source), hard-exclude prefixes, `cc.modality = 'text'`. OR fallback (`orFallback`) reruns with OR-of-terms through the same path. CJK queries go to `_searchKeywordCJK`. | `searchKeyword` :1433. Plain `db.query` with `beforePlannerRead` settle (`planner-stats.ts:389`). No statement timeout, no JIT setting, no work_mem, no candidate LIMIT (`KEYWORD_RANK_CANDIDATES` not applied). Emits `stale` per row via `timeline_entries` MAX(created_at) comparison. | `searchKeyword` :1066 (builder :1078-1262). Uses `withScopedReadTransaction` with `alwaysTransaction: true, jitOff: true` and settings: `statement_timeout` from `keywordStatementTimeoutSetting()` (default 8000 ms, range 1000-60000; `keyword-statement-timeout.ts`), `enable_seqscan: 'off'` on relaxed or candidate-join attempts, `KEYWORD_STATEMENT_SETTINGS` (`work_mem 32MB`, keyword-chunk-match.ts:40). Candidate rows capped at `KEYWORD_RANK_CANDIDATES` = 50,000 (keyword-chunk-match.ts:30). Timeouts routed through `keywordTimeouts` negative cache (#6495; :222, :1231). Emits `false AS stale`. |
| `countKeywordPages` (:1200) | `countKeywordPages(query, opts?: SearchOpts & {timeoutMs?}): Promise<number>` | Cat 40 Hard F2 strict count only (never OR retry): `buildKeywordCountStatement` (search/keyword-statement.ts), capped at `KEYWORD_COUNT_CAP + 1` = 10,001 (keyword-statement.ts:35). Via `engine-sql/keyword-pages.ts:24-33`. | `countKeywordPages` :1571 via `keywordPagesImpl.countKeywordPages(read => read(scopedRead(this.engineSql)), ...)` with empty dialect (no timeout). | `countKeywordPages` :1266. Scoped read with `alwaysTransaction`, `jitOff`, settings; `statement_timeout` = `timeoutMs` override when given, else keyword timeout; plus `KEYWORD_STATEMENT_SETTINGS`. |
| `searchKeywordPages` (:1202) | `searchKeywordPages(query, opts: SearchOpts \| undefined, page: KeywordPageWindow): Promise<SearchResult[]>` | One window of matching pages (best chunk each) ordered `(score DESC, page_id ASC)`, no global chunk cap. Statement from `buildKeywordPagesStatement` (keyword-statement.ts). `engine-sql/keyword-pages.ts:36-40`. Also `searchHandlePages` (keyword-pages.ts:43-47) for slug-handle hits (GBRA-108). | `searchKeywordPages` :1574 (no timeout, no settings). | `searchKeywordPages` :1269. Same scoped transaction as `countKeywordPages`, with the keyword timeout and `KEYWORD_STATEMENT_SETTINGS`. |
| `searchTitles` (:1223) | `searchTitles(query, opts?: SearchOpts): Promise<SearchResult[]>` | Page-grain title arm (fix/title-retrieval-arm D1). `search/title-statement.ts`: pages whose title matches `websearch_to_tsquery`, `ORDER BY exact_title DESC, score DESC, p.id ASC` (exact-title key #5889), `LIMIT/OFFSET`, then `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` one representative chunk (`compiled_truth` preferred, then lowest `chunk_index`). Same page-grain filters and visibility as keyword. AND then OR fallback (`engine-sql/titles.ts:76`). No query-length gate. Each attempt is capped at `titleWholeVectorCandidates()` matching pages (`search/title-statement.ts:55`). A set that does not fit returns a marked row (`candidates_cut`). The strict attempt then reports `keyword_candidates_incomplete` through `opts.onKeywordIncomplete('candidate_budget')` and puts the title-only statement's rows first (`titles.ts:111-115`). The any-word retry matches the whole search vector while every match fits the cap, and titles only when it does not, or for a remote caller (`titles.ts:122-126`). | `searchTitles` :1578 via `titlesImpl.searchTitles` with `relaxedPrefersIndex: false, staleProbe: true` (so `stale` is computed) and no timeout. | `searchTitles` :1273 via `titlesImpl.searchTitles` with `statementTimeout` from keyword timeout, `statementSettings: KEYWORD_STATEMENT_SETTINGS`, `relaxedPrefersIndex: true`, `staleProbe: false`, and chunk-manifest reads. |
| `searchVector` (:1224) | `searchVector(embedding: Float32Array, opts?: SearchOpts): Promise<SearchResult[]>` | ANN search. `search/vector-statement.ts:310` `buildVectorSearchStatement`: two-stage CTE. The candidate stage keeps a pure distance `ORDER BY` so HNSW is usable; outer stages re-rank by source factor. Freshness placement (#5824), scope binding and index-walk or scope-scan variants are in the same builder. Iterative HNSW scan used when `supportsHnswIterativeScan(extversion)` says pgvector is 0.8+ (`vector-index.ts:141-144`). Pool attempts with `remainingMs` budget. | `searchVector` :1726. Same statement builder with `dialect: 'pglite'` (adds timeline `stale`). `this.vectorIterativeScan` probe cached on the engine. Each attempt in its own `db.transaction` with `withVectorSettings`. No exact-scan fallback (comment at :1727). Calls `beforePlannerRead` settle. | `searchVector` :1450. `vectorScope(opts)` (scope binding), then `searchIndexWalk` / `searchVectorPool` with attempts via `runVectorAttempt` (scoped read, `jitOff`, `vectorScanSettings` incl. iterative scan and per-attempt `statement_timeout`). Falls back to an exact scan (`stmt.exactSql`) when the pool is short. `explainVectorSearch` (EXPLAIN only) shares the same path. |
| `getEmbeddingsByChunkIds` (:1238) | `getEmbeddingsByChunkIds(ids: number[], column = 'embedding'): Promise<Map<number, Float32Array>>` | Rehydrates embeddings for rescoring. `engine-sql/chunks.ts:1085-1119`: `column` must match `COLUMN_NAME_REGEX` (else `EmbeddingColumnNotRegisteredError`) and is identifier-quoted. `SELECT cc.id, vector_send(cc.<col>::vector) AS embedding FROM content_chunks cc JOIN pages p ... WHERE cc.id = ANY($::int[]) AND cc.<col> IS NOT NULL AND chunkVisibleFilter`. Binary `vector_send` decoded with `decodeVectorSend`. Empty input returns an empty Map. | `getEmbeddingsByChunkIds` :1762 via `chunksImpl` with `unscopedExecutor` (LegacyUnscopedRead). | `getEmbeddingsByChunkIds` :1541, same brand and SQL. No scope transaction (unscoped on master), though the visibility filter stays in SQL. |

Engine-only search members (not on the interface, same range): `searchKeywordChunks` (Postgres :1291; PGLite :1638) is the chunk-grain anchor primitive with no page dedup. Postgres also has `_searchKeywordCJK` (postgres-engine.ts:1435) and `explainVectorSearch`.

CJK keyword fallback (`engine-sql/cjk-search.ts`): both engines route CJK queries (`hasCJK`) away from `websearch_to_tsquery` to an ILIKE term-AND scan (`search/cjk-keyword-sql.ts`). `CJK_CANDIDATE_CAP` = 2000 (cjk-search.ts:55). PGLite picks a capped vs uncapped variant from the planner chunk estimate versus `PGLITE_CJK_CAPPED_CHUNKS` = 50,000 (cjk-search.ts:56, :171). Postgres runs it under the keyword statement timeout and scoped read (postgres-engine.ts `_searchKeywordCJK`).

### Duplicate finder and identity decisions

These four members hold the duplicate finder's read and the owner's "same person" and "different people" decisions. Their SQL lives in `src/core/engine-sql/entity-keys.ts` and `src/core/engine-sql/identity-decisions.ts`, shared by both engines. `src/core/rust-engine/members.ts` classes all four under capability `pages` (:72-75), and `test/engine-parity/coverage.tsv` marks the Rust arm as `refuses:pages` for all four. Both engines pass the read members `unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql, ...)` with the reason "brain-wide, trusted local only", so no scope transaction or row-security binding applies.

| Member (engine.ts) | Signature (compact) | What it does and SQL | Engine differences |
|---|---|---|---|
| `listEntityKeyRows` (:1202) | `listEntityKeyRows(opts: EntityKeyRowsOpts): Promise<EntityKeyRow[]>` | Duplicate finder read. It returns one row per live, unretired page of the wanted types, in unarchived sources, with only the named frontmatter keys. `engine-sql/entity-keys.ts:39-79`, statement at :43-74. `FROM pages p JOIN sources s ON s.id = p.source_id LEFT JOIN entity_identities ei ON ei.source_id = p.source_id AND ei.page_id = p.id`. Predicates are `p.deleted_at IS NULL`, `s.archived IS NOT TRUE`, `p.type = ANY($types::text[])` and `NOT retiredPageSql('p')` (`entities/resolve.ts:228`; retired means frontmatter `canonical` false or no, a `superseded_by` or `merged_into` value, or an alias or redirect `status`). The `keys` column is a `jsonb_object_agg` over `unnest($keys::text[])`, limited to keys the page carries, defaulting to `{}` (:46-48). Optional `members` narrows to `(source_id, slug)` pairs through two parallel `unnest` arrays (:56-57). Optional `tokens` keeps a page when one of its `fields` values (array, string or number, lowered and trimmed) reads as one of the token values in any of seven forms. These are the raw value, without a leading `@`, the URL host, a LinkedIn company name, a LinkedIn profile name, a `/pub/` name or a GitHub login (:58-71). Keyset cursor is `(p.source_id, p.slug) > (after.source_id, after.slug)` with empty strings when no cursor is given (:72). `ORDER BY p.source_id, p.slug LIMIT $limit::int` (:73-74). `created_at` is rendered as UTC ISO text with microseconds by `to_char` so two rows compare as strings. `page_id` and `generation` are converted to numbers, and `identity` is null when no `entity_identities` row exists (:75-78). A Rust engine must keep the order, the strict cursor comparison and the microsecond text. | PGLite :1431, Postgres :1088. Same SQL on both engines, through the same unscoped executor. |
| `listIdentityDecisions` (:1204) | `listIdentityDecisions(): Promise<IdentityDecisionsRead>` | Reads every identity decision plus the members a "same" decision could not link yet. `engine-sql/identity-decisions.ts:96-121`. Statement one (:97-101) is `SELECT decision_id, verdict, members, group_id, entity_id, canonical_source_id, canonical_slug, decided_by, decided_at::text, origin, home_source_id, git_blob, evidence FROM entity_identity_reviews ORDER BY decision_id`. Statement two (:102-108) finds pending members. It builds from `MEMBER_PAGES` (:92-94), which is `entity_identity_reviews r CROSS JOIN LATERAL jsonb_array_elements(r.members)` with a `LEFT JOIN pages p` on source and slug and `p.deleted_at IS NULL`, then `LEFT JOIN entity_identities x`. Predicate is `r.verdict = 'same' AND (p.id IS NULL OR x.entity_id IS DISTINCT FROM r.entity_id)`. The reason is `page_missing` when no live page exists, `unlinked` when the page sits in no group, otherwise `other_group`. Order is `r.decision_id`, source id, slug. The `revision` string is built in code by `decisionsRevision` (:81-84). It sorts the rows by `decision_id` with a plain string comparison and joins `decision_id:verdict:entity_id:origin:git_blob` per row with commas. `applyIdentityDecisions` compares against this exact format, so a Rust engine must produce the same text. JSON text columns (`members`, `evidence`) are parsed when the driver returns text. | PGLite :1435, Postgres :1092. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `listRestrictedIdentityMembers` (:1206) | `listRestrictedIdentityMembers(members: IdentityMemberRef[]): Promise<IdentityMemberRef[]>` | Names which of the given pages not every reader may read. Empty input returns `[]` with no statement (`identity-decisions.ts:125`). Otherwise `SELECT p.source_id, p.slug FROM pages p JOIN unnest($sourceIds::text[], $slugs::text[]) AS t(source_id, slug) ON p.source_id = t.source_id AND p.slug = t.slug WHERE NOT <privatePagesFilterFragment('p')>` (:124-131). The fragment (`search/private-visibility.ts:82`) is a folder-rule check joined with the privacy rules, and the member's comment describes the result as pages that name readers, sit under a folder rule or carry a private label. There is no `deleted_at` filter and no `ORDER BY`. | PGLite :1439, Postgres :1096. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `applyIdentityDecisions` (:1208) | `applyIdentityDecisions(input: ApplyIdentityDecisionsInput): Promise<ApplyIdentityDecisionsResult>` | Writes and removes decisions and reconciles the identity rows they imply, in one transaction. `identity-decisions.ts:134-243`. In order, (1) on a dialect with transaction advisory locks, `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('gbrain:identity-decisions', 0))` so applies run one at a time (:135). (2) When `expectRevision` is given, it reads `decision_id, verdict, entity_id, origin, git_blob` from `entity_identity_reviews`, and if the recomputed revision differs it returns `{upserted: 0, removed: 0, linked: 0, unlinked: 0, stale: true}` and writes nothing (:136-140). (3) `DELETE FROM entity_identity_reviews WHERE decision_id = ANY($remove) RETURNING 1` (:143). (4) One `INSERT INTO entity_identity_reviews ... ON CONFLICT (decision_id) DO UPDATE` per upsert, with `members` and `evidence` bound as JSONB and `decided_at` defaulting to `now()` (:146-156). (5) Automatic-group cleanup. It deletes `entity_identities` rows with `established_by = 'dedupe:auto'` for the entity ids in `auto.unlink` (:159-161), then for any automatic group that a "same" decision now covers or that two members of one "different" decision sit in (:162-179). (6) Automatic-group writes. One CTE (:183-198) unnests the plan, keeps an entity only when every member page still exists at the planned `generation` with `deleted_at IS NULL` and sits in no other group, and inserts survivors with confidence 1.0 and `ON CONFLICT (source_id, page_id) DO NOTHING`. The skipped count is planned minus ok. (7) It deletes automatic groups left with fewer than two members, no canonical member or a missing or deleted member (:202-211). (8) It deletes `dedupe:confirm` rows no "same" decision backs any more (:212-218). (9) It inserts `dedupe:confirm` rows for live member pages that sit in no group, with the canonical flag set only on the decision's canonical member, when it is first in its group and the group has no canonical row yet (:219-232). (10) A final `UPDATE entity_identities SET canonical = true` for the decision's canonical member when its group has none (:233-241). Rows any other actor wrote are never touched. The result carries `auto` counts only when `input.auto` was given (:242). | PGLite :1443, Postgres :1100. Both run `this.transaction(tx => applyIdentityDecisions(tx.engineSql, input))`. Only Postgres takes the advisory lock, because `capabilities.transactionAdvisoryLocks` is true there and false on PGLite. |

### Source reads, page-write reads and memory proposals

A Rust brain refuses the 21 write-misc members with `engine_capability_missing` (the `engine.ts:1276-1279` comment says so, and `rust-engine/members.ts` classes each as `raw_sql`). `getSource` and `listSourceLocalPaths` are classed under capability `sources` (:98-99), and `test/engine-parity/coverage.tsv` marks the Rust arm `serves` for both.

The Postgres wrappers in this group work as follows. `unscopedExecutor` (`engine-sql/brands.ts:49`) only re-types the executor as an unscoped read, with no runtime effect. `refuseAborted` (`engine-sql/abort-aware.ts:10`) wraps an executor so each statement first checks the request's abort signal and sends nothing when it has fired. `whenNotAborted` (`postgres-engine.ts:257`) does the same check once before a whole call. `preparationRead` (:464) runs the read on `autocommitSql` unless a read deadline is set, in which case it runs in a reserved read-only transaction with `statement_timeout` set to the time left (`runDeadlineRead`). A page transaction or an `unbounded` flag skips the deadline path. `autocommitSql` (:364) is the pool executor that retries once inside `BEGIN READ WRITE` when a read-only session default refused the statement (`retryReadOnlyAutocommit`, `postgres-engine/read-write-transactions.ts:65`). `writeMiscReads` (:1167) is `refuseAborted(unscopedExecutor(engineSql))` on the current connection. `writeMiscRead` (:1168) is `whenNotAborted(preparationRead(refuseAborted(unscopedExecutor(...))))`. PGLite has none of this. Its `writeMiscReads` (`pglite-engine.ts:1508`) is a bare `unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql)`, and its three writes use `this.engineSql` directly.

| Member (engine.ts) | Signature (compact) | What it does and SQL | Engine differences |
|---|---|---|---|
| `getSource` (:1268) | `getSource(sourceId): Promise<SourceDetailRow \| null>` | One source row by id, or null. `engine-sql/sources.ts:84-93`. `SELECT id, name, local_path, last_commit, last_sync_at, config, created_at, contextual_retrieval_mode, trust_frontmatter_overrides FROM sources WHERE id = $1`, first row only. Rows come back as the driver returns them, with no normalizing. | PGLite :1457, direct unscoped executor. Postgres :1114 runs `preparationRead` with `refuseAborted`, so it honors a read deadline and refuses an aborted request. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `listSourceLocalPaths` (:1274) | `listSourceLocalPaths(): Promise<string[]>` | Local paths of every source that has one, in no particular order. `sources.ts:98-102`. `SELECT local_path FROM sources WHERE local_path IS NOT NULL AND local_path <> ''`. Used by the source filesystem lock to find the outermost worktree root. | PGLite :1503, direct unscoped executor. Postgres :1162 runs `preparationRead` with no abort wrapper, so a read deadline applies but an aborted signal is not checked. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `readSourceState` (:1280) | `readSourceState(sourceId, opts?: SourceStateOptions): Promise<SourceStateRow \| null>` | A source's incarnation, archived flag, local path and config, or null for an unknown id. `engine-sql/write-misc.ts:46-52`. `SELECT incarnation,archived,local_path,config FROM sources WHERE id=$1`, plus `FOR SHARE` when `opts.lock === 'share'`, which holds the row until the caller's transaction ends. Config text is parsed, and a missing or non-object config becomes `{}` (:40-43). | PGLite :1509, same SQL. Postgres :1171. With `lock: 'share'` it runs `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads` (the current connection, so the lock lasts with the caller's transaction), otherwise through `writeMiscRead`. |
| `readAdmissionSource` (:1281) | `readAdmissionSource(sourceId): Promise<AdmissionSourceRow \| null>` | Page admission's source row joined to its guest-source row in one statement. `write-misc.ts:35`, run at :55-59. `SELECT s.incarnation,s.archived,s.local_path, <config key kind> AS kind, <config key role> AS role, g.writers AS guest_writers, g.trust_ceiling AS guest_trust_ceiling, <GUEST_SOURCES_HIDDEN_SQL> AS guest_sources_hidden FROM sources s LEFT JOIN guest_sources g ON g.source_id=s.id WHERE s.id=$1`. The hidden flag (`guests/note.ts:33`) is true when row security is active on `guest_sources` and no permissive read policy covers the role, so admission refuses. A missing table on an old schema throws. The exported statement text `ADMISSION_SOURCE_SQL` (:37) is the key of the pre-admission cache, so its text must not drift. | PGLite :1510, Postgres :1174 through `writeMiscRead`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `sharedSkillPackTablePresent` (:1282) | `sharedSkillPackTablePresent(): Promise<boolean>` | Whether the schema has a `shared_skill_packs` table. `write-misc.ts:62-65`. `SELECT to_regclass('shared_skill_packs') IS NOT NULL AS present`. True only when the value is exactly `true`. | PGLite :1511, Postgres :1175 through `writeMiscRead`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `listSharedSkillPackRoots` (:1283) | `listSharedSkillPackRoots(hostId): Promise<SharedSkillPackRoot[]>` | Every shared skill pack bound to its source's current incarnation, with the roots its files live under on one host. `write-misc.ts:124-129`. `FROM shared_skill_packs p JOIN sources s ON s.id=p.source_id AND s.incarnation=p.source_incarnation LEFT JOIN persistence_source_bindings b ON b.source_id=p.source_id AND b.source_incarnation=p.source_incarnation LEFT JOIN persistence_host_bindings h ON h.worktree_id=b.worktree_id AND h.host_id=$1::uuid`. It returns `source_id, source_incarnation, s.local_path AS source_root, h.local_path AS worktree_root, b.relative_path`. No `WHERE` and no `ORDER BY`. | PGLite :1512, Postgres :1176 through `writeMiscRead`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `getWorktreeBinding` (:1285) | `getWorktreeBinding(sourceId, hostId: string \| null): Promise<WorktreeBinding \| null>` | The source's writer binding as one host sees it, or null when the source has none. `write-misc.ts:159-171`. `FROM persistence_source_bindings s JOIN persistence_worktrees w ON w.id=s.worktree_id LEFT JOIN persistence_host_bindings h ON h.worktree_id=w.id AND h.host_id=$2::uuid WHERE s.source_id=$1`. It casts `topology_generation` and `owner_epoch` to text, reads `prepared_at` from the worktree manifest, and adds a `canonical_git` subselect on the source's `config` key `canonical` equal to `git`. A null host id gives null host columns. The text of `WORKTREE_BINDING_SQL` (:159) is memoized by the legacy sweep screen, so a change changes what that screen answers. | PGLite :1513, Postgres :1177 through `writeMiscRead`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `hasExtractedEvents` (:1286) | `hasExtractedEvents(sourceId, pageId): Promise<boolean>` | Whether the page has an extracted chronicle generation with events. `write-misc.ts:174-179`. `SELECT 1 FROM chronicle_page_state WHERE source_id=$1 AND page_id=$2 AND state='extracted' AND cardinality(event_hashes) > 0 LIMIT 1`. True when a row comes back. | PGLite :1514, Postgres :1178 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads` (no deadline path). Same SQL on both engines. |
| `listActiveAccessMembers` (:1287) | `listActiveAccessMembers(): Promise<string[]>` | Ids of access members that are neither disabled nor removed. `write-misc.ts:182-185`. `SELECT id FROM access_members WHERE disabled_at IS NULL AND tombstoned_at IS NULL ORDER BY id`. | PGLite :1515, Postgres :1179 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `hasLabelledOnlyMember` (:1289) | `hasLabelledOnlyMember(): Promise<boolean>` | Whether any access member carries the labelled-only grant, disabled and removed members included. `write-misc.ts:192-194`. `SELECT 1 FROM access_members WHERE labelled_only LIMIT 1`. A failed read throws, and the caller counts a throw as yes. | PGLite :1516, Postgres :1180 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `listAccessTokens` (:1291) | `listAccessTokens(): Promise<AccessTokenRow[]>` | Every legacy access token, revoked ones too, newest first, as `auth list` reads them. `write-misc.ts:208-210`. `SELECT * FROM access_tokens ORDER BY created_at DESC`. The `*` is deliberate so every schema generation reads, and the row type allows extra columns. | PGLite :1517, Postgres :1181 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `notifierCoverageRecorded` (:1297) | `notifierCoverageRecorded(): Promise<boolean>` | Whether a resident notifier recorded coverage for any source. `write-misc.ts:71-75`. `SELECT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sync_notifier_sources WHERE observed_at IS NOT NULL) AS covered`. The value counts as true when it is `true`, `'t'` or `1`. A schema without the table rejects with the missing-table error, which the caller catches. | PGLite :1518, Postgres :1182 through `writeMiscRead`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `readOpCheckpointKeys` (:1299) | `readOpCheckpointKeys(op, fingerprint): Promise<{keys: string[], corruptParent: boolean}>` | Completed keys of one resumable operation's checkpoint, deduplicated. `write-misc.ts:82-103`. One statement with three `UNION ALL` arms (:86-93), all filtered by `op = $1 AND fingerprint = $2`. Arm one reads `path` from `op_checkpoint_paths`. Arm two reads `jsonb_array_elements_text(completed_keys)` from `op_checkpoints` only when `jsonb_typeof(completed_keys) = 'array'`. Arm three emits a marker row when `completed_keys` is not an array. Code dedupes with a `Set` in first-seen order and sets `corruptParent` on the marker. `UNION ALL` is deliberate so the server does no sort over a large checkpoint, and the array guard stops `jsonb_array_elements_text` from failing on a scalar. | PGLite :1519, Postgres :1183 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `readHealthMemoClock` (:1301) | `readHealthMemoClock(): Promise<string>` | The page clock of the `get_health` memo, as `<brain id>:<clock>`. `write-misc.ts:109-114`. `SELECT (SELECT brain_id::text FROM persistence_brain WHERE singleton = 1) AS brain, COALESCE((SELECT last_value FROM page_generation_clock_seq), 0)::text AS clock`. A null part becomes an empty string. The clock moves on every page insert, update or delete. | PGLite :1520, Postgres :1184 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `listSynopsisSourceIds` (:1303) | `listSynopsisSourceIds(): Promise<string[]>` | Live sources whose own contextual-retrieval mode is `per_chunk_synopsis`. `write-misc.ts:117-121`. `SELECT id FROM sources WHERE contextual_retrieval_mode = 'per_chunk_synopsis' AND NOT archived ORDER BY id`. | PGLite :1521, Postgres :1185 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `expireMemoryProposals` (:1304) | `expireMemoryProposals(reason): Promise<number>` | Expires every awaiting memory proposal past its expiry and returns how many. A write. `write-misc.ts:234-238`. `UPDATE memory_proposals SET state='expired', reason=$1, decided_by='gbrain:expiry', decided_at=now(), updated_at=now() WHERE state='awaiting_owner' AND expires_at<=now() RETURNING 1`. The count is the number of returned rows. | PGLite :1522 runs on `this.engineSql`. Postgres :1186 runs `whenNotAborted` on `refuseAborted(this.autocommitSql)`, so a read-only session default is retried once in `BEGIN READ WRITE`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `listAwaitingMemoryProposals` (:1305) | `listAwaitingMemoryProposals(limit): Promise<Array<Record<string, unknown>>>` | The oldest `limit` proposals still awaiting their owner, as stored rows. `write-misc.ts:241-244`. `SELECT * FROM memory_proposals WHERE state='awaiting_owner' ORDER BY created_at LIMIT $1`. | PGLite :1523, Postgres :1187 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `readMemoryProposal` (:1307) | `readMemoryProposal(principalKind, principalId, requestId): Promise<Record<string, unknown> \| null>` | The proposal a principal holds under a request id, as its stored row, or null. `write-misc.ts:247-251`. `SELECT * FROM memory_proposals WHERE principal_kind=$1 AND principal_id=$2 AND request_id=$3::uuid`, first row. | PGLite :1524, Postgres :1188 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `purgeMemoryProposals` (:1308) | `purgeMemoryProposals(retentionDays): Promise<number>` | Deletes every closed proposal decided, or last updated, at least `retentionDays` days ago, and returns how many. A write. `write-misc.ts:281-285`. `DELETE FROM memory_proposals WHERE state<>'awaiting_owner' AND COALESCE(decided_at, updated_at) <= now() - ($1::integer * interval '1 day') RETURNING 1`. | PGLite :1525 runs on `this.engineSql`. Postgres :1189 runs `whenNotAborted` on `refuseAborted(this.autocommitSql)`, with the read-only retry. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `readPendingProposalSummary` (:1310) | `readPendingProposalSummary(): Promise<PendingProposalRow \| null>` | How many proposals still await their owner unexpired, and the oldest one's age in hours. `write-misc.ts:257-261`. `SELECT count(*) AS n, EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM now() - min(created_at)) / 3600 AS oldest FROM memory_proposals WHERE state='awaiting_owner' AND expires_at>now()`. `oldest` is null when no row matches. Values come back as the driver gives them, so `n` can be a number or a string. | PGLite :1526, Postgres :1190 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `listMemoryProposalRows` (:1312) | `listMemoryProposalRows(query: ProposalListQuery): Promise<Array<Record<string, unknown>>>` | One page of the owner's proposal list, oldest first, as stored rows. `write-misc.ts:267-273`. `SELECT * FROM memory_proposals WHERE ($1::text IS NULL OR source_id=$1) AND ($2::boolean OR state='awaiting_owner') AND ($4::timestamptz IS NULL OR (date_trunc('milliseconds', created_at), request_id) > ($4::timestamptz, $5::uuid)) ORDER BY date_trunc('milliseconds', created_at), request_id LIMIT $3`. The keyset cursor is the previous page's creation time cut to milliseconds plus its request id, so a Rust engine must keep the millisecond cut in both the filter and the order. | PGLite :1527, Postgres :1191 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `listOwnerProposalRows` (:1314) | `listOwnerProposalRows(requestId): Promise<Array<Record<string, unknown>>>` | The stored proposals of any credential with this request id, at most two, so a collision of generated ids is seen and never guessed. `write-misc.ts:276-278`. `SELECT * FROM memory_proposals WHERE request_id=$1::uuid LIMIT 2`. | PGLite :1528, Postgres :1192 through `whenNotAborted` on `writeMiscReads`. Same SQL on both engines. |
| `attachSourcePath` (:1316) | `attachSourcePath(sourceId, localPath, opts?: AttachSourcePathOptions): Promise<void>` | Attaches a working tree to an existing source for `sources add --path`. A write. `write-misc.ts:220-231`. `UPDATE sources SET local_path = $2, name = COALESCE($3, name), config = CASE WHEN $4::boolean IS NULL THEN config ELSE COALESCE(config, '{}'::jsonb) \|\| jsonb_build_object('federated', $4::boolean) END WHERE id = $1`. The federated flag merges as a bound boolean, never a stringified JSON value. An unknown id changes nothing. The SQL does not check that the source was path-less. | PGLite :1529 runs on `this.engineSql`. Postgres :1193 runs `whenNotAborted` on `refuseAborted(this.autocommitSql)`, with the read-only retry. Same SQL on both engines. |

### Search ranking-stage reads

These ten reads run after hybrid search has fused its result lists. They feed the supersede down-rank, the alias boost, the collapse stage, the generated-page demotion and the SQL cosine rescore, plus one onboarding count. Their SQL lives in `src/core/engine-sql/search-reads.ts` (and `src/core/engine-sql/health.ts` for `countOnboardSignal`) and both engines call it through the same getter. The callers fail open on an ordinary error, but every caller checked for this fragment rethrows an `engine_capability_missing` refusal first (`src/core/engine-capability-refusal.ts:1-9`), so a brain that lacks a method is not mistaken for a brain with no data.

`this.searchRead` is the same one-line getter on both engines (PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:1892`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:1703`). It returns `unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql, 'search reads ...')`. `unscopedExecutor` (`engine-sql/brands.ts:49`) only stamps a type brand on the executor and returns it unchanged. These reads therefore open no scope transaction, bind no row-security scope and add no abort check of their own. On Postgres `engineSql` (`postgres-engine.ts:359`) is a fresh executor over the current connection, which is the transaction handle inside a transaction clone. On PGLite `engineSql` (`pglite-engine.ts:832`) is the in-process executor over the database. The shared helper `rows()` (`search-reads.ts:48`) renders a bound-value fragment and calls `exec.executeRaw`. A read policy's source clause goes through `readFilter` (`search-reads.ts:37`), which binds the source id (or `= ANY(text[])` for a list) and keeps the rest of `pageReadFilter` as text.

| Member (engine.ts) | Signature (compact) | What it does and SQL | Engine differences |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cosinesByChunkIds` (:1390) | `cosinesByChunkIds(ids: number[], column: string, query: Float32Array): Promise<Map<number, number>>` | Cosine score per chunk, computed in the database, for the rescore after vector search. An id with no embedding in `column`, or on an unreadable page, gets no entry. `search-reads.ts:149` builds the statement with `cosineByChunkIdsStatement` (`engine-sql/chunks.ts:1206`). `column` must match `COLUMN_NAME_REGEX` (else `EmbeddingColumnNotRegisteredError`) and is identifier-quoted. `SELECT cc.id, CASE WHEN vector_dims(col) <> $len OR vector_norm(col) = 0 OR vector_norm(q) = 0 THEN 0 ELSE 1 - (col <=> q) END AS cosine FROM content_chunks cc JOIN pages p ON p.id = cc.page_id WHERE cc.id = ANY($::int[]) AND col IS NOT NULL AND chunkVisibleFilter('p','cc')`. The query vector is sent as a text literal cast to `vector`. No `ORDER BY`. A Rust brain must return 0 (not an error) for a dimension mismatch or a zero vector. | PGLite :1893, Postgres :1704. Same SQL on both engines through `searchRead`. Rust brain serves it natively (`rust-engine/store-members/embeddings.ts:50`, class `vector_search` at `members.ts:170`) but only for the `embedding` column. Any other column refuses (`embeddings.ts:55`). |
| `hasSupersedeEdges` (:1393) | `hasSupersedeEdges(): Promise<boolean>` | True when the brain has any `supersedes` link. `search-reads.ts:54` runs `SELECT 1 AS one FROM links WHERE link_type = 'supersedes' LIMIT 1` (`SUPERSEDE_EDGE_PROBE_SQL`, `search-reads.ts:31`) and tests for at least one row. `link_type` stays a literal on purpose so the planner can match the partial index `idx_links_supersedes_to` by its predicate. A bound parameter can leave a generic plan that reads the whole composite links index. | PGLite :1894, Postgres :1705. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain serves it natively (`store-members/link-queries.ts:303`, class `links` at `members.ts:173`). The caller (`search/hybrid.ts:759`) caches a true answer for a time window and fails open, after rethrowing a capability refusal. |
| `supersedingPageSlugs` (:1395) | `supersedingPageSlugs(pageIds: number[], policy?: PageReadPolicy): Promise<Map<number, string>>` | For each superseded page id, the slug of a live page in the same source that supersedes it. An empty id list returns an empty map with no query. `search-reads.ts:63`. `SELECT DISTINCT l.to_page_id, pf.slug AS by_slug FROM links l JOIN pages pf ON pf.id = l.from_page_id JOIN pages pt ON pt.id = l.to_page_id WHERE l.link_type = 'supersedes' AND pf.deleted_at IS NULL AND pf.source_id = pt.source_id AND l.to_page_id = ANY($::bigint[]) AND readFilter(pf) AND readFilter(pt) AND origin clause`. With a policy, both pages must be readable and the link's origin page (when it has one) must be readable too (an `EXISTS` over `pages origin`). Without a policy the live filter is off for `pt` but `pf.deleted_at IS NULL` still holds. There is no `ORDER BY`, and the code keeps the first row per id, so several superseding pages give whichever row the database returns first. | PGLite :1895, Postgres :1706. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain serves it natively (`link-queries.ts:310`, class `links` at `members.ts:174`). It treats any object as a policy that turns the live filter on, as the SQL's policy test does. |
| `aliasCanonicalRefs` (:1397) | `aliasCanonicalRefs(refs: {slug, source_id}[], policy?: PageReadPolicy): Promise<{source_id, canonical_slug}[]>` | Of the given (source, slug) refs, the ones that are the canonical slug of some alias row, on a page the policy may read. Empty refs return an empty array with no query. `search-reads.ts:86`. `SELECT DISTINCT a.source_id, a.canonical_slug FROM slug_aliases a JOIN pages p ON p.source_id = a.source_id AND p.slug = a.canonical_slug WHERE (a.source_id, a.canonical_slug) IN (SELECT * FROM unnest($::text[], $::text[])) AND readFilter(p)`. The two arrays are the refs' source ids and slugs, matched by position. No `ORDER BY`. | PGLite :1896, Postgres :1707. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain serves it natively (`store-members/aliases.ts:97`, class `pages` at `members.ts:43`), reading the policy as `pageReadFilter('p', policy, !!policy)` does. The caller (`search/hybrid/alias-resolved.ts:26`) treats an SQL miss as no aliases and rethrows a capability refusal. |
| `pageCollapseFacts` (:1399) | `pageCollapseFacts(ids: number[], bodyIds: number[]): Promise<PageCollapseFactRow[]>` | Facts the collapse stage needs per page id. A missing page has no row. `search-reads.ts:111`. `SELECT p.id, target, body_chars, body, lead FROM pages p WHERE p.id = ANY($::int[])`. `target` is the first non-blank of frontmatter `redirect_to`, `redirect`, `merged_into`, else `superseded_by` only when frontmatter `status` matches an alias or redirect word (`STATUS_REDIRECT`, `search-reads.ts:97`). `body_chars` is the length of `btrim(COALESCE(boundedBodyPrefixSql('p'), ''))`. For ids also in `bodyIds`, `body` is an md5 of that bounded body when it is at least 200 characters (`IDENTICAL_BODY_MIN_CHARS`) and `lead` is its first 4000 characters (`OVERLAP_PREFIX_CHARS`). The SQL has no `deleted_at` predicate and no `ORDER BY`, so soft-deleted pages answer and row order is the database's. | PGLite :1897, Postgres :1708. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain serves it natively (`store-members/facts.ts:230`, class `facts` at `members.ts:209`). It refuses an id that is not a 32-bit integer, because the SQL binds `int[]`. The caller (`search/collapse.ts:133`) rethrows a capability refusal (`collapse.ts:147`). |
| `slugsEndingIn` (:1401) | `slugsEndingIn(sourceId: string, word: string): Promise<string[]>` | Up to two live slugs in one source that equal `word` or end in `/word`. The caller uses it to resolve a bare-word target and accepts only a single hit. `search-reads.ts:123`. `SELECT slug FROM pages WHERE source_id = $1 AND deleted_at IS NULL AND (slug = $2 OR slug LIKE $3) LIMIT 2`. `$3` is `%/` plus `word` with a backslash put before each `%`, `_` and `\`. No `ORDER BY`, so with two hits the order is the database's. | PGLite :1898, Postgres :1709. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain serves it natively (`store-members/cli-reads-search-pages.ts:9`, class `pages` at `members.ts:44`). |
| `generatedPagesIn` (:1403) | `generatedPagesIn(ids: number[]): Promise<GeneratedPageRow[]>` | Of the given page ids, the pages gbrain generated. Those are pages whose frontmatter `dream_generated` equals the text `true`, or whose type is `extract_receipt`. `search-reads.ts:131`. `SELECT id, frontmatter, compiled_truth FROM pages WHERE id = ANY($::int[]) AND (frontmatter ->> 'dream_generated' = 'true' OR type = 'extract_receipt')`. No live or source filter, no `ORDER BY`. The rows carry the body (`compiled_truth`) so the demotion code can inspect it. | PGLite :1899, Postgres :1710. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain serves it natively (`cli-reads-search-pages.ts:12`, class `pages` at `members.ts:45`) and returns an empty list for empty input with no store call. |
| `livePageIdsAt` (:1405) | `livePageIdsAt(refs: {slug, source_id}[]): Promise<number[]>` | Ids of the live pages at the given (source, slug) refs. `search-reads.ts:137`. `SELECT id FROM pages WHERE deleted_at IS NULL AND (source_id, slug) IN (SELECT * FROM unnest($::text[], $::text[]))`. Ids come back as numbers, in database order. A ref with no live page simply has no id. | PGLite :1900, Postgres :1711. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain serves it natively (`cli-reads-search-pages.ts:15`, class `pages` at `members.ts:46`) and returns an empty list for empty input with no store call. |
| `pagesLinkingTo` (:1407) | `pagesLinkingTo(fromIds: number[], toIds: number[]): Promise<number[]>` | Of `fromIds`, the pages that have a ranking link to any page in `toIds`. `search-reads.ts:143`. `SELECT DISTINCT l.from_page_id AS id FROM links l WHERE l.from_page_id = ANY($::int[]) AND l.to_page_id = ANY($::int[]) AND l.link_type <> ALL(ARRAY['amends']::text[])`. The last predicate is `rankingLinkSql('l')` (`link-ranking-policy.ts:33`) and the excluded list is `NON_RANKING_LINK_TYPES` (`link-ranking-policy.ts:21`, currently one type). No `ORDER BY`. | PGLite :1901, Postgres :1712. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain serves it natively (`link-queries.ts:326`, class `links` at `members.ts:175`). The demotion caller (`search/provenance-demotion.ts:81`) rethrows a capability refusal. |
| `countOnboardSignal` (:1409) | `countOnboardSignal(signal: OnboardSignal, entityTypes: EntityTypeFilter, opts?: {signal?: AbortSignal}): Promise<number>` | One brain-wide count for the MCP onboarding coach. `engine-sql/health.ts:285`. The six `signal` values map to six statements, all unscoped. `stale_chunks` counts `content_chunks` with a null `embedding` joined to live pages whose frontmatter lacks the `embed_skip` key (`EMBED_SKIP_FILTER_FRAGMENT`, `embed-skip.ts:74`). `entities` counts live pages whose type is in the entity-type list (`entityTypePredicateSql`, per-source lists via a `CASE` on `source_id` when sources disagree). `linked_entities` adds `EXISTS` of a link row with `to_page_id = p.id`. `timeline_entities` adds `EXISTS` of a `timeline_entries` row for the page, with no filter on event rows. `takes` is `COUNT(*) FROM takes` with no filter. `pages` is `COUNT(*)` of live pages. Returns the first row's `count` as a number, or 0 with no row. The abort signal goes to `executeRaw` as a statement option. | PGLite :1902, Postgres :1713. Same SQL on both engines through `searchRead`. Rust brain has no native method. `members.ts:264` classes it `health` and no store member serves it, so it refuses with `engine_capability_missing`. The caller (`onboard/mcp-onboarding.ts:86`) rethrows that refusal instead of reporting a partial count. |

### Timeline, field-usage and alias reads

Typed versions of statements that callers used to send through `executeRaw`. Their SQL is in `engine-sql/timeline-reads.ts`, `engine-sql/field-usage.ts` and `engine-sql/alias-fanout-reads.ts`. All read unscoped, with no row-security scope, as before. The one write is `deleteTimelineRows`. The getters and helpers differ between the engines as follows.

- **PGLite `timelineRead`** (`pglite-engine.ts:1905`) is `unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql, ...)` and nothing more. **`aliasFanoutRead`** (`pglite-engine.ts:1916`) is the same shape. Neither getter adds an abort check.
- **Postgres `timelineRead`** (`postgres-engine.ts:1716`) wraps the same executor in `refuseAborted` (`engine-sql/abort-aware.ts:10`). Every `query`, `run`, `executeRaw`, `unsafe` and `transaction` call first throws if the request's abort signal has fired, so an aborted request sends no statement. `whenNotAborted` (`postgres-engine.ts:257`) makes the same check before the member starts.
- **Postgres `timelineBoundedRead`** (`postgres-engine.ts:1719`) is `whenNotAborted` plus `preparationRead` plus `refuseAborted`. `preparationRead` (`postgres-engine.ts:464`) runs the read on `autocommitSql` unless a read deadline is set and no page transaction is open. `autocommitSql` (`postgres-engine.ts:364`) is the pool executor that reruns a statement once in `BEGIN READ WRITE` when a read-only session default refused it (25006). With a deadline set, the read runs in a reserved read-only transaction whose `statement_timeout` is the time left (`runDeadlineRead`). Three of these members are also named in `BOUNDED_TYPED_READS` (`statement-deadline.ts:99`) so a doctor deadline ends them.
- **Postgres `engineRead`** (`postgres-engine.ts:1137`) is used by the four alias reads. It checks for an aborted request, then runs `preparationRead` with the executor wrapped in `abortGuarded` (`request-signal.ts:26`, a proxy that checks the abort signal before `executeRaw`, `query`, `run` and `unsafe`).
- **A Rust brain** has no native method for any member in the timeline and alias groups below. `members.ts` classes each as `raw_sql` (lines are in the rows) and no store member serves them, so each refuses with `engine_capability_missing` (`members.ts:1-6`). Only `readFieldUsageSamples` is served natively.

| Member (engine.ts) | Signature (compact) | What it does and SQL | Engine differences |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sampleEffectiveDates` (:1411) | `sampleEffectiveDates(): Promise<EffectiveDateSampleCounts>` | Doctor's effective-date check. `timeline-reads.ts:43`. A CTE takes the newest 1000 pages by id (`SELECT effective_date, effective_date_source FROM pages ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1000`), soft-deleted pages included, no source scope. Two counts are joined with `UNION ALL`. `future_dated` counts `effective_date > NOW() + INTERVAL '1 year'`. `pre_1990` counts `effective_date < TIMESTAMPTZ '1990-01-01'` where `COALESCE(effective_date_source, '')` is not one of `event_date`, `date`, `published`, `created`. Counts come back as text and are parsed. A missing kind reads as 0. | PGLite :1906, Postgres :1717. Same SQL on both engines. Postgres wraps it in `whenNotAborted` and the aborted-request `timelineRead`, with no deadline. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:412`, `raw_sql`). |
| `sampleEffectiveDateSources` (:1412) | `sampleEffectiveDateSources(): Promise<EffectiveDateFallbackRow[]>` | Doctor re-parses these in JS. `timeline-reads.ts:66`. The same newest-1000 CTE (`slug, frontmatter, effective_date_source, created_at, updated_at`), then `WHERE effective_date_source = 'fallback' AND (frontmatter ? 'event_date' OR frontmatter ? 'date' OR frontmatter ? 'published')`. Returns `slug, frontmatter, created_at, updated_at`. The `?` is the JSONB key-exists operator. No outer `ORDER BY`. | PGLite :1907, Postgres :1718. Same SQL on both engines, with the same Postgres wrapping as `sampleEffectiveDates`. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:411`, `raw_sql`). |
| `readMaxTimelineEntryId` (:1413) | `readMaxTimelineEntryId(): Promise<number>` | The largest timeline entry id, 0 when there are none. It is doctor's timeline-history scan clock. `timeline-reads.ts:82`. `SELECT COALESCE(max(id), 0) AS max_entry FROM timeline_entries`. | PGLite :1908, Postgres :1720. Same SQL on both engines. Postgres runs it through `timelineBoundedRead`, and it is in `BOUNDED_TYPED_READS`. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:405`, `raw_sql`). |
| `listStoredTimelineRowsOrdered` (:1414) | `listStoredTimelineRowsOrdered(pageId: number): Promise<OwnTimelineRow[]>` | A page's own (non-event) timeline rows, for the canonical projection's stored-timeline read. `timeline-reads.ts:88` sends `STORED_TIMELINE_SQL` (`timeline-reads.ts:40`). `SELECT id, date::text AS date, source, summary, detail FROM timeline_entries WHERE page_id = $1 AND event_page_id IS NULL ORDER BY date, id`. `date` comes back as `YYYY-MM-DD` text. A Rust brain must keep the date-then-id order. | PGLite :1909, Postgres :1721. Same SQL on both engines. Postgres inlines the `timelineBoundedRead` wrapper (`whenNotAborted`, `preparationRead`, `refuseAborted`). It is not in `BOUNDED_TYPED_READS`. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:395`, `raw_sql`). |
| `deleteTimelineRows` (:1415) | `deleteTimelineRows(pageId: number, deletions: readonly TimelineRowDeletion[]): Promise<{date, removed}[]>` | Deletes the page's non-event rows that still match a listed row on id, date, source, summary and detail. It returns the date and `removed` mark of each row it deleted. `timeline-reads.ts:97`. `DELETE FROM timeline_entries t USING jsonb_to_recordset($2::text::jsonb) AS d(id integer, date date, source text, summary text, detail text, removed boolean) WHERE t.page_id = $1 AND t.event_page_id IS NULL AND t.id = d.id AND t.date = d.date AND t.source = d.source AND t.summary = d.summary AND t.detail = d.detail RETURNING t.date::text AS date, d.removed`. Parameters are the page id and the JSON text of `deletions`. A row that changed since it was read is left alone. The function is not `async`, so the statement is sent before it returns and a pipelined transaction keeps its order (`timeline-reads.ts:95`). | PGLite :1910 passes `this.engineSql` (the write executor, not the branded read executor). Postgres :1722 passes `refuseAborted(this.autocommitSql)` inside `whenNotAborted`. It runs inside the caller's transaction when there is one, and a read-only session default is retried in `BEGIN READ WRITE`. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:361`, `raw_sql`). |
| `storedTimelineRowsByPageIds` (:1416) | `storedTimelineRowsByPageIds(pageIds: readonly number[]): Promise<PageTimelineRow[]>` | Non-event timeline rows of many pages in one read, for `gbrain repair timeline`. `timeline-reads.ts:105`.
