An empty list returns an empty array with no query. `SELECT t.page_id, t.id, to_char(t.date, 'YYYY-MM-DD') AS date, t.source, t.summary, t.detail FROM timeline_entries t WHERE t.page_id = ANY($1::bigint[]) AND t.event_page_id IS NULL`. No `ORDER BY`, so the order is the database's. | PGLite :1911, Postgres :1723. Same SQL on both engines. Postgres runs it through `timelineBoundedRead`, and it is in `BOUNDED_TYPED_READS`. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:416`, `raw_sql`). |
| `countSegmentedTimelinePages` (:1417) | `countSegmentedTimelinePages(sourceIds: readonly string[], limit: number): Promise<SegmentedTimelinePages>` | Counts live segmented pages that still carry non-event timeline rows, and samples the first `limit` of them (repair timeline's residual check). `timeline-reads.ts:116`. It throws unless `limit` is a non-negative safe integer. Statement one is `SELECT count(DISTINCT p.id)::int AS pages, count(*)::int AS rows FROM timeline_entries t JOIN pages p ON p.id = t.page_id WHERE p.source_id = ANY($1::text[]) AND p.deleted_at IS NULL AND p.body_storage = 'segmented' AND t.event_page_id IS NULL`. With zero pages it returns `{pages: 0, rows: 0, sample: []}` and stops. Otherwise statement two is `SELECT p.source_id, p.slug FROM pages p WHERE <same page filter> AND EXISTS (non-event timeline row) ORDER BY p.id LIMIT <limit>`. `limit` is written into the text as an integer literal (`LIMIT 10`) so the text matches the caller's old statement. | PGLite :1912, Postgres :1724. Same SQL on both engines. Postgres runs both statements on the same `timelineBoundedRead` executor, and it is in `BOUNDED_TYPED_READS`. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:355`, `raw_sql`). |
| `countTimelineCommentMarkupRows` (:1418) | `countTimelineCommentMarkupRows(sourceIds: readonly string[]): Promise<number>` | Counts non-event timeline rows on live pages of the given sources whose summary or source contains an HTML comment opener or closer. `timeline-reads.ts:130`. `SELECT count(*)::int AS n FROM timeline_entries t JOIN pages p ON p.id = t.page_id WHERE p.source_id = ANY($1::text[]) AND p.deleted_at IS NULL AND t.event_page_id IS NULL AND (t.summary LIKE ... OR t.source LIKE ...)`. The `LIKE` patterns are the literal comment markers. | PGLite :1913, Postgres :1725. Same SQL on both engines. Postgres uses `whenNotAborted` and the aborted-request `timelineRead`, with no deadline. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:356`, `raw_sql`). |
| `listTimelineOrphanPages` (:1419) | `listTimelineOrphanPages(opts: {afterId, limit, sourceId?}): Promise<TimelineOrphanPage[]>` | One page of the walk used by `extract timeline --prune-orphans`. Live pages after `afterId` (optionally in one source) that hold non-event timeline rows, in id order, at most `limit`. `timeline-reads.ts:141`. `SELECT p.id, p.slug, p.source_id, p.compiled_truth, p.timeline, p.body_storage, p.knowledge_revision::text AS revision FROM pages p WHERE p.id > $afterId AND p.deleted_at IS NULL [AND p.source_id = $sourceId] AND EXISTS (non-event timeline row) ORDER BY p.id LIMIT $limit`. The caller pages by passing the last id back as `afterId`. A segmented page's body columns come back NULL. | PGLite :1914, Postgres :1726. Same SQL on both engines. Postgres uses `whenNotAborted` and the aborted-request `timelineRead`, with no deadline. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:397`, `raw_sql`). |
| `readFieldUsageSamples` (:1421) | `readFieldUsageSamples(query: FieldUsageQuery): Promise<FieldUsageTypeSample[]>` | Page type counts and per-type page samples for `gbrain schema detect --fields`. `field-usage.ts:43`. Statement one is `SELECT pages.type, count(*)::int AS pages FROM pages WHERE pages.deleted_at IS NULL AND coalesce(pages.type, '') <> '' [AND pages.source_id = $] [AND pages.type = $] GROUP BY pages.type ORDER BY count(*) DESC, pages.type LIMIT max(0, floor(maxTypes))`. Then, unless `countsOnly` is true, one statement per type. `SELECT pages.frontmatter, pages.compiled_truth FROM pages WHERE pages.deleted_at IS NULL AND pages.type = $ [AND pages.source_id = $] ORDER BY pages.id LIMIT max(1, floor(samplePerType))`. It does not join `sources`, so pages of an archived source are counted and sampled. A segmented page (NULL `compiled_truth`) is sampled with a null body. Frontmatter that arrives as a JSON string is parsed. The JSDoc says ties in the count order are in byte order, but the SQL has no `COLLATE` clause, so that tie order is the database's collation. | PGLite :1915, Postgres :1727. Same SQL on both engines. Both build `unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql, ...)` inline, and Postgres adds no abort check here. Rust brain serves it natively (`store-members/field-usage.ts:10`, class `pages` at `members.ts:79`) and keeps both behaviours above. |
| `resolveCaseSensitiveAliases` (:1422) | `resolveCaseSensitiveAliases(aliasNorms: readonly string[], sourceIds: readonly string[]): Promise<CaseSensitiveAliasRow[]>` | Alias rows that match only as written, for `resolveQueryEntities`. `alias-fanout-reads.ts:25`. Empty norms or empty sources return an empty array with no query. `SELECT slug, alias_norm, alias_text FROM page_aliases WHERE alias_norm = ANY($1::text[]) AND case_sensitive AND source_id = ANY($2::text[])`. The SQL has no `ORDER BY`. The code sorts the rows in JS by `slug`, then `alias_norm`, then `alias_text`, comparing bytes (`Buffer.compare`, `alias-fanout-reads.ts:16`) so every engine answers alike. A Rust brain must sort the same way. | PGLite :1917 uses `this.aliasFanoutRead`. Postgres :1728 uses `this.engineRead`. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:320`, `raw_sql`). The caller keeps a fail-open `.catch(() => [])`, so a refusal reads as no rows (`alias-fanout-reads.ts:9-11`). |
| `readPageTitle` (:1423) | `readPageTitle(sourceId: string, slug: string): Promise<string \| null>` | The title of the page at (source, slug), or null with no page. Soft-deleted pages are included. `alias-fanout-reads.ts:34`. `SELECT title FROM pages WHERE source_id = $1 AND slug = $2`, first row only. | PGLite :1918 uses `aliasFanoutRead`. Postgres :1729 uses `engineRead`. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:319`, `raw_sql`). |
| `readPageAliasRows` (:1424) | `readPageAliasRows(sourceIds: readonly string[], slugs: readonly string[]): Promise<PageAliasRow[]>` | Every alias row whose source is in `sourceIds` and slug is in `slugs`, for the fan-out name lookup. `alias-fanout-reads.ts:40`. Empty input returns an empty array with no query. `SELECT slug, source_id, alias_norm, alias_text, origin FROM page_aliases WHERE source_id = ANY($1::text[]) AND slug = ANY($2::text[])`. No `ORDER BY`. The code sorts in JS bytewise by `source_id`, `slug`, `alias_norm`, then `origin`, because the caller keeps the first row of equal rank. | PGLite :1919 uses `aliasFanoutRead`. Postgres :1730 uses `engineRead`. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:318`, `raw_sql`). |
| `readEntityAliasRows` (:1426) | `readEntityAliasRows(sourceId: string, slugs: readonly string[]): Promise<EntityAliasRow[]>` | The entity card's alias rows. Every alias row of a listed slug in one source, ordered by alias. `alias-fanout-reads.ts:55`. `SELECT slug, alias_norm, origin, alias_text FROM page_aliases WHERE source_id = $1 AND slug = ANY($2::text[]) ORDER BY alias_norm`. Rows with the same `alias_norm` have no defined order (the JSDoc says "no tie order") and the code does no JS sort. There is no empty-list shortcut, so an empty `slugs` still sends the statement. | PGLite :1920 uses `aliasFanoutRead`. Postgres :1731 uses `engineRead`. Same SQL on both engines. Rust brain refuses (`members.ts:317`, `raw_sql`). |

### Engine-only members adjacent to this range (for completeness)

Not on the interface, but on one or both engines in the same area: `connectForRestore`, `connectWithHeldLock`, `closeRetainingLock`, `holdsDatastoreLock`, `walRepairReceipt` (PGLite); `onCheckout`, `getPoolDiagnostics`, `keywordBoundedRead`, `refreshPageBody`, `updatePageContextualRetrievalState`, `migrateFactsToCanonical`, `explainVectorSearch`, `searchKeywordChunks` (Postgres and PGLite as noted). `putPage` also gets its own write-side guard `assertOriginUnclaimed` from `persistence/origin-guard.ts`.

### Parity risks noted

- Keyword timeouts, JIT and work_mem: Postgres bounds keyword, title, count and CJK reads with `statement_timeout` (default 8000 ms, configurable 1-60 s), JIT off and `work_mem 32MB`. PGLite has none of these, so a slow query can run unbounded there. A Rust engine needs an explicit per-query deadline or it will not match Postgres behavior.
- Keyword candidate bound: Postgres caps keyword candidates at 50,000 chunks (`KEYWORD_RANK_CANDIDATES`). PGLite does not, so PGLite can rank more candidates and return different top-N under load.
- `stale` flag: PGLite computes per row `stale` from `timeline_entries`. Postgres `searchKeyword` returns `false AS stale` (postgres-engine.ts:1217), and `searchTitles` on Postgres disables the stale probe (`staleProbe: false`). Consumers see different `stale` values per engine.
- Vector search: Postgres has an exact-scan fallback and iterative HNSW scan (pgvector 0.8+), with an index-walk path and per-attempt budgets. PGLite has no exact fallback and no pool escalation beyond its own attempts (pglite-engine.ts:1733 comment). Result sets can differ when the ANN pool is short.
- Planner stats: PGLite runs `beforePlannerRead` (ANALYZE settle) around reads (`planner-stats.ts:389-420`). No planner-stats call was found in the Postgres engine file. Plan shapes differ, which can change ties in `ORDER BY score`.
- Transactions: Postgres honors `readOnly` (BEGIN READ ONLY), `pipelineEarly`/`pipelineFirst`, signal abort and commit checks. PGLite honors only `signal`. A read-only transaction on PGLite can write.
- `withReservedConnection`: PGLite ignores `route` and `selfContained`, has no `transaction` member, and does not enforce pool permits. Postgres enforces `PoolCapacityError` and direct-pool routing.
- `reconnect`: PGLite in-memory is a deliberate no-op (state preserved). File-backed PGLite does a full disconnect and reconnect. Postgres rebuilds pools with build-then-swap. Callers that rely on "reconnect restores the same state" behave differently in-memory vs file-backed PGLite.
- Schema lock: Postgres uses cross-process advisory key 42 (session or transaction form). PGLite serializes in-process only (`schema-migration-lock.ts:22-30`). PGLite `initSchema` also skips `verifySchema` and the zombie-index sweep that Postgres runs after migrations.
- Soft delete asymmetry: `softDeletePage` has no `'default'` source fallback. If `sourceId` is omitted, it can flip the same slug in every source (`pages.ts:276-295`), while `deletePage` defaults to `'default'`. `deletePages`, `softDeletePages` and `resolveSlugsByPaths` require `sourceId`.
- `getAllSlugs` includes soft-deleted pages (no `deleted_at` predicate, pages.ts:563-572). Its JSDoc says "every page in the brain", so callers doing existence checks can see tombstones.
- Unscoped reads on Postgres: `resolveSlugs`, `resolveSlugsByPaths`, `listAllPageRefs`, `listAllSources`, `getEmbeddingsByChunkIds` run on the pool without an RLS scope transaction (LegacyUnscopedRead brand). The Rust rewrite has no RLS, so these become plain reads, but any access control must be enforced in the app layer.
- Blank-body guard: `putPage` refuses to overwrite a non-empty or segmented body with a blank one unless `allowEmptyOverwrite` (pages.ts:116-133). Both engines share this logic, but PGLite's `reread` callback differs from Postgres's `RETURNING` assumption, and the Rust port must keep the same guard.
- Batch limits: `deletePages`, `softDeletePages`, `resolveSlugsByPaths` throw above `DELETE_BATCH_SIZE` = 500 (engine-constants.ts:25). Callers must chunk; a Rust port must keep the cap or the contract changes.
- Bind-parameter ceiling: PGLite batches under 30,000 bind params (`PGLITE_CAPABILITIES`, dialect-pglite.ts:25-29). Postgres batches at 65,533 (`dialect-postgres.ts:54`), the driver's limit. Batched writes must stay under the PGLite ceiling if both engines stay in use during migration.
- Statement cancellation: PGLite cannot cancel a running statement (WASM). The abort race in `pgliteQuery` only settles the caller, the statement finishes in the background (dialect-pglite.ts:42-56). Postgres sends a real cancel. Timeout behavior differs under contention.
- Lock timeouts: Postgres schema replay runs under `lock_timeout` (schema-lock-timeout.ts) and `retrySchemaReplayOnDeadlock`. PGLite has no equivalent.
- Vector column binding: `getEmbeddingsByChunkIds` validates the column name by regex and quotes it. The Rust port must keep the same allowlist, since column names are interpolated.
- `listPrefixSampledPages` representative chunk uses `embedding IS NOT NULL` (hardcoded column) even though `getEmbeddingsByChunkIds` takes a column. Keep both behaviors aligned.
- Sampling determinism: `listCorpusSample` with `seed` depends on `setseed` followed by `ORDER BY RANDOM()` on the same connection (Postgres forces a transaction). PGLite is single-connection, so it matches, but the Rust engine must reproduce the same seeded order or accept a different sample.


---

## Chunks, Extraction staleness and Links/Graph

Scope: `BrainEngine` interface declarations in `src/core/engine.ts` lines 1428-2069 at `116d737413` (the `// Chunks`, extraction-freshness and `// Links` blocks, ending before `// Tags` at line 2070). That range declares 54 methods: 15 chunk/embedding, 7 extraction staleness, 6 link writes, 17 link/graph reads, plus 9 more members in section E (6 chunk seal and import-reuse members, 1 owner-only trigger check and 2 wanted-link writes). Sections A to D keep their `engine.ts` lines at `8bbfd0b5e8`. None of them is marked optional (`?`) in the interface.

Engine implementations compared: `src/core/pglite-engine.ts` (PGLiteEngine, 1812-2078) and `src/core/postgres-engine.ts` (PostgresEngine, 1626-1899). SQL lives in `src/core/engine-sql/chunks.ts`, `pages.ts`, `links.ts`, and in `src/core/search/read-enrichment.ts` (the graph and boost readers both engines share, via `executeRaw`), plus `src/core/derived-links.ts`.

Engine difference, in short: the SQL text is shared. The two engines differ in the read transaction wrapper and the planner/JIT handling around it:
- PGLite calls `scopedRead(this.engineSql)` directly, with no transaction. Several reads are wrapped in `plannerRead(...)` (`pglite-engine.ts:1992, 2015, 2029, 2077`), which can run `ANALYZE`-style planner refreshes (`src/core/planner-stats.ts:389-410`). Postgres never calls `plannerRead`.
- PostgresEngine wraps scoped reads in `withScopedReadTransaction(sourceIds, sourceId, tx => ...)` (`postgres-engine.ts:368`), which sets the source/RLS scope in a transaction. It also threads `chunkManifestReads` into chunk reads (`postgres-engine.ts:1642, 1650, 1658, 1856`). `chunkManifestReads` returns `true` on every non-Postgres engine (`src/core/search/safe-chunks.ts:107-113`), so PGLite omitting the argument is equivalent by construction.
- `getBacklinkCounts` on Postgres runs in `jitOffRead` (`postgres-engine.ts:1863-1867`, `prepare: true`). PGLite runs it through `executeRaw` (`pglite-engine.ts:2043`).
- The `unscopedExecutor(..., 'EO4 inventory')` wrapper marks reads that are deliberately unscoped on the master path. PGLite and Postgres both use it for `findByTitleFuzzy`, `traverseGraph`, `traversePathsDetailed`, `findOrphanPages`, `sumStaleChunkChars`, `getPageTimestamps`, and the `links`/`pages` reads that take no scope.

---

### A. Chunks (engine.ts 1240-1423)

Shared helpers used below: `chunkReadWhere` (`engine-sql/chunks.ts:396-408`) is the visibility predicate for chunk reads (page slug, source scope `p.source_id = ANY($::text[])` or `= $`, `chunkVisibleFilter`, optional private/safe filters). `staleChunkWhere` (`chunks.ts:509-534`) is the stale predicate: `p.deleted_at IS NULL`, the active embedding column IS NULL (or signature drift), `embed_skip` excluded, and `budgetLimited` excluded. `chunkGenerationFilter` (`search/safe-chunks.ts:77`) gates segmented bodies through `page_body_manifest`.

Notes for section A:
- Embedding-column routing is resolved per call by `resolveActiveEmbeddingColumnFromEngine` (`pglite-engine.ts:1831, 1851`; `postgres-engine.ts:1648, 1671`). The write path resolves from config rows inside the upsert statement's own executor (`chunks.ts:139-170`).
- pgvector: in this range, the only pgvector operations are the cast on insert (`c.embedding::vector` or `::halfvec(N)`, `chunks.ts:260`) and `embedding_image::vector`. Stale and drift predicates use `IS NULL` and `md5()`, not vector distance operators. Vector reads (`vector_send`, cosine) are outside this range (`engine-sql/chunks.ts:1085-1131`, `getEmbeddingsByChunkIds` and `cosineByChunkIdsStatement`, declared at engine.ts:1238 and before).
- Batch pattern: `upsertChunks` uses `jsonb_to_recordset` (one JSON document per batch, `jsonRowBatches`), not `unnest`. `moveDisplacedChunksAndPrune` (`chunks.ts:389`) and `getChunkWindows` (`chunks.ts:1157`) use `unnest` over parallel int arrays.
- Recursive CTE: none in this section.

---

### B. Extraction staleness (engine.ts 1424-1482)

The predicate is `stalePagesWhere` in `engine-sql/pages.ts` (line 827). A page is stale when `deleted_at IS NULL`, not quarantined, and `links_extracted_at IS NULL` OR `links_extracted_at < versionTs` OR `updated_at > links_extracted_at` OR a wanted-producer origin is stale. Options: `attendance` ('exclude' | 'blocked', by `links_attendance_blocked_revision` vs `knowledge_revision`), `held` ('exclude' | 'only', `linkExtractionHeld`), and a guest-source exclusion. This is the shared watermark behind `gbrain extract --stale` and the `links_extraction_lag` doctor check.

WHERE source_id = $ AND ... AND id > $ ORDER BY id LIMIT n`. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:1931`. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:1751`, scope transaction on `sourceId`. | `listStaleFrontmatterKeyPages`: about 10 files, mostly golden and inventory tests plus `repair-frontmatter-keys-sweep-6454.test.ts`. |

Same SQL. | `stripStaleFrontmatterKeys`: about 10 files. `repair-frontmatter-keys-sweep-6454.test.ts`, `helpers/postgres-engine-sql-cases.ts`, `helpers/engine-sql-rollback-cases.ts`. |

Notes for section B:
- `pages.ts` shares one stale predicate between count and list (`stalePagesWhere`), so the count and the batch cursor cannot drift apart.
- Extraction staleness has no recursive CTE and no pgvector use. Batch pattern: `markPagesExtractedBatch` and `markPagesAttendanceBlocked` use three-array `unnest(text[])`. `stripStaleFrontmatterKeys` uses `id = ANY(int[])`.
- `markPagesExtractedBatch` is also the stamp that the `markPagesAttendanceBlocked` revision check must agree with. A concurrent edit makes `updated_at > links_extracted_at`, so the page stays stale (v0.42.7 D4).

Notes for section C:
- The engine.ts JSDoc for `addLinksBatch` (engine.ts:1507-1511) says "single multi-row INSERT...SELECT FROM (VALUES) JOIN pages". The implementation is `jsonb_to_recordset` (`links.ts:216-227`, #1861). This doc is stale.
- Batch pattern: `addLinksBatch` and `removeLinksByPagesAndSource` both bind their rows as one JSONB document via `jsonb_to_recordset`, not `unnest`. That avoids the 65535 parameter cap and the text[] literal crash (#1861).
- No recursive CTE in the writes. `ON CONFLICT` targets the composite `(from_page_id, to_page_id, link_type, link_source, origin_page_id)` key.

### D. Links: reads, graph, boosts (engine.ts 1585-1841)

Shared readers in `src/core/search/read-enrichment.ts` take a `ReadQuery` (the engine's `executeRaw`). The SQL in them is the same on both engines. The engines differ only in the transaction and planner wrapper around them, as described at the top.

Scope by `sourceIds = ANY($::text[])` on all three endpoints. Optional `TEMPORAL_LINK_SELECT_SQL`. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:1987`: `scopedRead(this.engineSql)`, no tx. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:1807`: `withScopedReadTransaction(opts?.sourceIds, opts?.sourceId)`. | `getLinks`: about 60 files. Notes for section D:
- Recursive CTEs: `traverseGraph` (`links.ts:659`), `traversePathsDetailed` (`links.ts:711`) and `relationalFanout` (`read-enrichment.ts:19`). The first two use the array-frontier BFS built by `bfsWalkCtes` (`links.ts:609`). The third walks paths with `path` and `visited` arrays and clamps depth to 3. `relationalChainHop`, `getBacklinkCounts`, `getAdjacencyBoosts` and the rest are not recursive.
- pgvector: none in this section. `getAdjacencyBoosts`, `getBacklinkCounts` and `relationalChainHop` run on `links`/`pages` joins only. Canonical chunk ids come from `content_chunks` through a correlated `LIMIT 1` subquery.
- Batch `unnest` patterns: `getEffectiveDates` and `getSalienceScores` join `unnest(text[], text[])` on `(slug, source_id)`. `traverseGraph`/`traversePaths` use `unnest(bfs.frontier)` and `unnest(bfs.visited)` in array CTEs. `relationalChainHop` uses `unnest` on an inlined id array. Other bulk reads use `ANY($n::int[])`.
- Read-policy seam: every read here authorizes endpoints, hops and origins before returning (`pageReadFilter`, `originFilter` in `read-enrichment.ts:11-16`). Scoping by `sourceId`/`sourceIds` is applied by the same helper in `links.ts` (`sourceScope`, `links.ts:652`).

### E. Chunk seal and reuse, owner-only triggers and wanted links (engine.ts 1463-1752)

Nine members that sit between the sections above. Six are chunk-seal and import-reuse members (`engine.ts:1463`, `1627-1639`), one is the owner-only trigger check (`1469`), and two are wanted-link writes (`1746`, `1752`). All SQL is shared. Both engines call one function in `engine-sql/chunks.ts`, `engine-sql/owner-only-marks.ts` or `engine-sql/wanted-links.ts`. The only engine difference is which executor each call receives.

Executor helpers used in the PGLite vs Postgres column (Postgres side, `postgres-engine.ts`):
- `engineSql` (`postgres-engine.ts:359`) is the executor over the current connection. Inside a transaction it is the transaction handle.
- `autocommitSql` (`postgres-engine.ts:364`) is `engineSql` plus `retryReadOnlyAutocommit` (`postgres-engine/read-write-transactions.ts:65`). A statement that a read-only session default refused is run once more in `BEGIN READ WRITE`.
- `preparationRead` (`postgres-engine.ts:464`) runs a typed read that master ran through `executeRaw`. Under a read deadline it runs in a reserved read-only transaction with a `statement_timeout`. Otherwise it uses `autocommitSql`.
- `unscopedExecutor` (`engine-sql/brands.ts:49`) marks a read as deliberately unscoped (no RLS scope transaction), as master ran it.
- PGLite has none of these. Every call uses `this.engineSql`, except `getChunkReuseRows`, which also wraps it in `unscopedExecutor`.

| # | Method (engine.ts line) | Signature (compact) | What it does | SQL / delegation | PGLite vs Postgres | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 46 | `sealChunks` (1463) | `(slug, chunks: ChunkInput[], opts?: {sourceId, expectedRevision}): void` | Replaces a page's chunks and seals its text projection at the page's current revision, all in one transaction. After this, search and `getChunks` serve the chunks (`upsertChunks` alone leaves the projection unsealed). Refuses a missing or deleted page (`Page not found: slug (source=...)`), a segmented page, a page in an archived source, and a stale `expectedRevision`. | `chunks.ts:1064-1078`, run inside the caller's transaction. The steps run in this order. `lockPageKeys`, then `readPageSnapshot(slug, {sourceId, requireLiveSource: true})`. Then `deleteChunks` (`chunks.ts:1080`, which locks the page row `FOR UPDATE`, deletes its chunks and clears `text_projection_revision`). Then `upsertChunks` with `sealChunkerVersion: 4` (`SAFE_FENCE_CHUNKER_VERSION`, `search/safe-chunks.ts:6`). Then `sealPageTextProjection` (`page-state/text-seal.ts:14`), which calls `writeTextProjectionSeal`. Last, `DELETE FROM page_projection_jobs WHERE source_incarnation = $::uuid AND slug = $ AND revision = $::uuid`. A segmented page throws `sealChunks refuses segmented page`. A non-null `expectedRevision` goes through `assertPageRevision` (`page-state/types.ts:133`). | Same SQL on both engines. PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2060` and Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:1912` both call `this.transaction(tx => chunksImpl.sealChunks(e.engineSql, e, ...))`. | `sealChunks`: 24 files by grep, of which `engine-parity/chunks.test.ts` (case at line 80, stale, deleted and unknown page refused), `engine-parity/chunk-safe-fence.test.ts`, `engine-parity/import-writes.test.ts` and `rust-engine-differential.test.ts` are the main ones. `coverage.tsv:112` records it as covered by 16 parity cases, with the Rust arm `serves`. |

ORDER BY g.tbl`, then one status `SELECT` (`gbrain_owner_only_held()`, the `unguarded` and `row_security` marks in `owner_only_marks`). When the brain is held it also runs `DENIED_LOGINS_SQL` (`:49`) and `CLOSED_LOGINS_SQL` (`:72`) over `pg_roles`, `pg_class` and `pg_policy`. With `ensure`, `holdCopiedMarks` (`:111`) first calls `gbrain_owner_only_hold()` if marks exist without the held flag and the role owns `owner_only_marks`. Then, unless `installing`, it calls `gbrain_owner_only_hold()` and inserts the `('*','unguarded')` mark `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, both only when `gbrain_owner_only_lineage()` is true. Then each owned missing trigger is re-created with one `DO $t$ ... DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS ...; CREATE TRIGGER ... BEFORE DELETE ...` statement. Tables are `sources`, `pages`, `facts`, `takes`, `timeline_entries`. | Same SQL on both engines, both with `this.engineSql` (`pglite-engine.ts:2064`, `postgres-engine.ts:1916`). The SQL reads Postgres catalogs. How PGLite answers the role and policy queries was not traced (unverified). | `ownerOnlyMarkTriggers`: 10 files by grep. `engine-parity/owner-only-marks.test.ts` (fresh brain and dropped trigger, the second case needs `raw_sql`) and `owner-only-removal-lineage.test.ts`. `coverage.tsv:404` records the Rust arm as `refuses:raw_sql`. |

AS v(...)` with `checked_at` set to `'-infinity'` for a `slug` ref whose target is a live page, `$3::timestamptz` (`readAt`) for a `name` ref, and `now()` otherwise. The delete and the insert are two statements, so they are atomic only inside the caller's transaction. | `replaceWantedLinkRows`: 9 files by grep. `engine-parity/wanted-links.test.ts` (14 hits, 4 parity cases for insert, update in place, producer-only delete, live slug target, and refusal of a repeated reference or missing origin). `coverage.tsv:406` records the Rust arm as `refuses:links`. |

Notes for section E:
- `sealChunks` is the only member here that needs a transaction, and both engines open it themselves with `this.transaction`. The other chunk members in this section run one statement and rely on the caller (the import) for ordering.
- `deleteChunks` clears `text_projection_revision`, `deleteChunksExcept` does not. A Rust engine must keep that difference. The import reseals after `deleteChunksExcept` through `writeTextProjectionSeal`.
- `writeTextProjectionSeal` must be a no-op when `knowledge_revision` no longer equals `revision`. That predicate is the guard against sealing a page that changed under the caller.
- `moveChunkIndexes` waves must run in order, one statement each. A parked row has a negative `chunk_index` and keeps its own byte span. The `(page_id, chunk_index)` unique index is why the waves exist.
- `getChunkReuseRows` returns booleans, never `null`. A recorded-hash gap must read as `false`.
- `lockRegisteredSourceIds` returns ids sorted. The sort and `FOR SHARE` are part of the lock order the wanted-link writer uses.
- `replaceWantedLinkRows` keeps `first_seen_at` on update and sets `checked_at` by the three-way rule above. The delete step only touches the producers named in `replacement.producers`.
- The interface comments for these nine members do not mention `engine_capability_missing`. The only refusal statements found are the generated Rust-arm column in `test/engine-parity/coverage.tsv` (`refuses:chunks`, `refuses:links`, `refuses:raw_sql`, and `serves` for `sealChunks`). That column states what the parity test expects of a Rust arm.

### Test coverage summary

- Parity (both engines, `DATABASE_URL`-gated, runs PGLite half always): `test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts`. Covers: `addLink`, `getChunks`, `listLinkSources`, `findOrphanPages`, the stale-extraction group (`countStalePagesForExtraction`, `listStalePagesForExtraction`, `markPagesExtractedBatch`), `countChunklessPagesWithContent`/`listChunklessPagesWithContent`, `relationalFanout`, `relationalChainHop`, `getLinks`, `getBacklinks`, `traverseGraph`, `traversePaths`, `traversePathsDetailed`, `removeLinksByPagesAndSource`, `listStalePagesForExtraction`.
- `test/e2e/engine-parity-salience.test.ts` covers `getSalienceScores`.
- `test/pglite-engine.test.ts` covers PGLite-only behavior: `upsertChunks` round trip and mixed-case slugs (519-560), `deleteChunks` (565-572), `countStaleChunks` (597-703), `removeLink`, `getChunks`.
- No parity or PGLite test directly names these methods: `getChunkPage` (only `get-chunks-paging.test.ts`), `getChunkWindows` (evidence tests, Postgres RLS scope), `sumStaleChunkChars`, `setPageEmbeddingSignature`, `invalidateStaleSignatureEmbeddings`, `invalidateContentDriftEmbeddings`, `readEmbeddingStateCounts`, `listEmbeddingStateRows`, `markPagesAttendanceBlocked`, `countStaleFrontmatterKeyPages`, `listStaleFrontmatterKeyPages`, `stripStaleFrontmatterKeys`, `replaceDerivedLinks`, `replaceDerivedLinksBatch`, `findByTitleFuzzy`, `getAdjacencyBoosts`, `getContentFlagsByPageIds`, `getUnverifiedExtractionPageIds`, `getPageTimestamps`, `getEffectiveDates`, `getBacklinkCounts` (the last is covered by `e2e/jit-off-reads-postgres.test.ts`). Several of these are only exercised through `test/helpers/postgres-engine-sql-cases.ts` and `test/engine-surface-coverage.test.ts` (inventory-style tests).

### Parity risks noted

1. Planner wrapper asymmetry. PGLite wraps `getBacklinks`, `traverseGraph`, `traversePathsDetailed`, `traversePaths`' inner call and `findOrphanPages` in `plannerRead` (`pglite-engine.ts:1992, 2015, 2029, 2077`), which can run planner-stat refreshes. Postgres does not. A Rust engine has no equivalent, so timing and plan behavior differ and nothing in the tests pins it.
2. Transaction and RLS scope asymmetry. Postgres wraps scoped reads in `withScopedReadTransaction` (`postgres-engine.ts:368`), which sets the source/RLS scope. PGLite runs the same SQL on the bare connection. The source-scope predicates are in SQL in both, so behavior matches, but a Rust engine must enforce the same scope in its own read path.
3. JIT off on Postgres only. `getBacklinkCounts` runs in `jitOffRead` on Postgres (`postgres-engine.ts:1863`). PGLite has no JIT, so this is a performance-only divergence, but the inline comment says plan compilation dominated at 50k pages.
4. Generic-plan sensitivity. `getAdjacencyBoosts` and `relationalChainHop` inline integer id arrays and use `OFFSET 0` fences, to keep the planner from choosing a generic plan (`search/read-enrichment.ts:117-130, 265-288`). A Rust engine has no plan cache, so these are not needed, but the output ordering they rely on must hold.
5. Traversal caps. `TRAVERSE_WALK_ROW_CAP` = 20000 and `TRAVERSE_PATH_ROW_CAP` = 5000 (`engine-constants.ts:37, 48`) bound the walk, and `traversePathsDetailed` reports `truncated`. The cap counts raw rows before dedup, so a Rust version must apply the cap at the same point or the `truncated` flag changes.
6. Traversal ordering. `traversePaths` orders by `depth, from_slug, to_slug, from_source_id, to_source_id`. `traverseGraph` orders by `depth, slug, source_id`. `relationalFanout` orders by `hop ASC, edge_count DESC, source_id, slug`. Ties and collation (`ORDER BY slug`) must match on both engines.
7. Stale `addLinksBatch` JSDoc. `engine.ts:1507-1511` says "INSERT...SELECT FROM (VALUES)". The implementation is `jsonb_to_recordset` (`links.ts:216-227`, #1861). The doc is wrong and should not be copied.
8. `getEffectiveDates` JSDoc contradicts itself. `engine.ts:1788-1790` says "Returns NULL for refs with no row; map omits them." The implementation omits them (`read-enrichment.ts:311-320`). Callers must treat a missing key as absent.
9. Stacked JSDoc on `findOrphanPages`. `engine.ts:1802-1829` has two JSDoc blocks in sequence; the `mode` semantics (#4524) sit above a separate block. The `excludePrivate` option is accepted by both implementations but missing from the interface type (`engine.ts:1830-1834`).
10. Unscoped master reads. `sumStaleChunkChars`, `findByTitleFuzzy`, `traverseGraph`, `traversePathsDetailed`, `findOrphanPages`, `getPageTimestamps` use `unscopedExecutor(..., 'EO4 inventory')` on both engines. Their scope comes from the caller's options, not the engine. Any Rust read path that enforces scope centrally would change these results.
11. Segmented-body chunk visibility. `getChunks`, `getChunkPage`, `getChunkWindows`, `countStaleChunks`, `listStaleChunks` and `relationalFanout` all gate on `chunkGenerationFilter` / `chunkGenerationProbe` (`search/safe-chunks.ts:77-89`). It reads `page_body_manifest` (the segmented-body manifest), and the flag `chunkManifest` only changes behavior on Postgres (`safe-chunks.ts:107-113`). Rust must replicate the manifest semantics exactly, or segmented pages will read differently.
12. Content-drift sweep has no saved cursor. `invalidateContentDriftEmbeddings` loops in 5000-row transactions with no saved cursor (`chunks.ts:612, 685-698`). A crash mid-loop re-examines rows, which is safe by design (the predicate is idempotent) but the batch boundary is observable in the returned count.
13. Upsert is retried as a whole. `upsertChunks` and `addLinksBatch` are wrapped in `batchRetry` (`pglite-engine.ts:1814-1815, 1950`; `postgres-engine.ts:1628-1629, 1770`). The engine doc says "callers MUST NOT wrap externally." A Rust engine that retries differently changes the commit-ambiguous replay story (`engine.ts:1248-1253, 1512-1520`).
14. Embedding column routing. Every chunk read and stale count resolves the active column by a registry lookup (`resolveActiveEmbeddingColumnFromEngine`, `search/embedding-column.ts:694`), and the write path resolves it again from config rows inside the upsert. A fallback to the legacy `embedding` column on a broken registry (`fallbackToLegacy: true`) is a silent behavior change for diagnostics and reads.
15. Halfvec and dimension casts. `upsertChunks` writes `::halfvec(N)` or `::vector` depending on the resolved column (`search/embedding-column.ts:279-283`). A Rust store must keep the same dimension and type rules, or writes to a registry-routed brain fail with dimension mismatch errors.


---

## Tags, Timeline, Chronicle, Ontology, Raw data, Files, Takes, Dream and Contradictions

Scope: the 50 BrainEngine methods declared in `src/core/engine.ts:2070-2450` at `116d737413` (interface). Rows for 43 of these methods keep `engine.ts` lines at `8bbfd0b5e8`; rows for the other 7 cite `116d737413`. No method in this range is optional (no `name?()` declarations). Paths below are relative to the gbrain repo root the gbrain repo root. Line numbers are `file:line` in that repo.

How delegation works in this range:
- Interface: `src/core/engine.ts:2070-2449` at `116d737413`.
- Engine methods are implemented twice, once in `src/core/pglite-engine.ts` and once in `src/core/postgres-engine.ts`. Most methods are thin wrappers that call `src/core/engine-sql/*.ts`, which hold one shared SQL text for both engines (`engine-sql/takes.ts:1-9`, `timeline.ts:1-11`, `tags.ts:1-7`, `files.ts:1-7`).
- Two families are not in engine-sql and are inlined per engine: ontology (`mergeOntologyFact`, `getOntology`, `discoverOntologyDimensions`, `findOntologyConflicts`), `putRawData`/`getRawData`, and the dream verdict cache. Their SQL text is written twice, once with `db.query($n)` in PGLite and once with postgres.js tagged templates in Postgres.
- Reads in engine-sql take a `LegacyUnscopedRead` brand (`engine-sql/brands.ts:33`), meaning master ran them on the pool with no RLS scope transaction. Both engines obtain it via `unscopedExecutor(...)` (`brands.ts:49`).
- Dialect executors: `engine-sql/dialect-pglite.ts:58-76` and `engine-sql/dialect-postgres.ts:68-94`. PGLite binds JSONB params with `JSON.stringify` (`dialect-pglite.ts:33`). Postgres binds them with `conn.json` (`dialect-postgres.ts:61`).

Shared helpers referenced below:
- `mutatePageTag` in `src/core/page-state/tags.ts:5-22`.
- Ontology helpers in `src/core/chronicle/ontology.ts`: `valueHash` (:12), `normalizeDimension` (:32), `isNovelDimension` (:43), `isBackdatedObservation` (:54), `ONTOLOGY_SUPERSEDE_GUARD` (:65).
- `deletedPageProjectionHiddenSql` in `src/core/eligibility/sql.ts:55`.
- `privatePagesFilterForCaller` / `privateFactFilterForCaller` in `src/core/search/private-visibility.ts:611` and `:992`.
- `buildTakeRows` / `buildTimelineRows` in `src/core/batch-rows.ts:177` and `:161`.
- `finalizeScorecard` / `deriveResolutionTuple` in `src/core/takes-resolution.ts:90` and `:22`.
- `finalizeLastSeen` in `src/core/chronicle/last-seen.ts:11`.
- `DREAM_VERDICT_TTL_SECONDS = 30 * 86400` at `src/core/engine.ts:528`.

Engine line map (PGLite / Postgres): every method is declared in both engine files, so there are no PGLite-only or Postgres-only methods in this range.

### Tags

| Method (iface line) | Signature | What it does | SQL / impl |
|---|---|---|---|
| `addTag` (1851) | `addTag(slug, tag, opts?: {sourceId?, tagSource?: 'frontmatter'}): Promise<void>` | Adds a tag to a page. Idempotent. An explicit add claims the row. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2081`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:1903`. Both call `mutatePageTag` in one transaction. Inside it: `lockPageKeys`, then `SELECT id FROM pages WHERE source_id=$1 AND slug=$2`. Missing page throws `addTag failed: page ... not found`. Then `INSERT INTO tags(page_id,tag,tag_source) VALUES(...,'added') ON CONFLICT (page_id,tag) DO UPDATE SET tag_source='added'`. With `tagSource:'frontmatter'` it inserts `'frontmatter'` and updates only `WHERE tags.tag_source IS NULL` (`page-state/tags.ts:13-17`). |
| `removeTag` (1852) | `removeTag(slug, tag, opts?: {sourceId?}): Promise<void>` | Removes one tag. | Same `mutatePageTag` path with `add=false` (`page-state/tags.ts:18-20`): `DELETE FROM tags WHERE page_id=(SELECT id FROM pages WHERE source_id=$1 AND slug=$2) AND tag=$3`. No existence check, so a missing page or tag is a silent no-op. |
| `getTags` (1859) | `getTags(slug, opts?: {sourceId?, sourceIds?, excludePrivate?, liveOnly?}): Promise<string[]>` | Returns sorted distinct tag names for a slug. `sourceIds[]` wins over `sourceId`. Unscoped reads default to source `'default'`. | `engine-sql/tags.ts:12-34`. `SELECT DISTINCT tag FROM tags WHERE page_id IN (SELECT id FROM pages WHERE slug=$ AND <scope> <privacy> <live>) ORDER BY tag`. Scope is `source_id = ANY($::text[])` or `source_id = $`. `IN` (not `=`) is used so a federated slug in several sources does not throw. `excludePrivate` adds `privatePagesFilterForCaller('pages')`. `liveOnly` adds `deleted_at IS NULL`. Read is `LegacyUnscopedRead` (`tags.ts:6`). |
| `pruneFrontmatterTags` (2082) | `pruneFrontmatterTags(slug, keep: string[], opts?: {sourceId?}): Promise<void>` | Deletes a page's frontmatter-owned tags that are not in `keep`, that is, tags that left the frontmatter. Tags an explicit add owns (`tag_source = 'added'`) and legacy NULL-source tags stay. | `engine-sql/tags.ts:41-44`, via `executeRaw`. `DELETE FROM tags t USING pages p WHERE p.id = t.page_id AND p.source_id = $1 AND p.slug = $2 AND t.tag_source = 'frontmatter' AND NOT (t.tag = ANY($3::text[]))`. `sourceId` defaults to `'default'`. PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2279` uses `this.engineSql`. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2134` uses `refuseAborted(this.autocommitSql)`, which throws before each statement if the request was aborted (`engine-sql/abort-aware.ts:10`), and retries a read-only refusal once. |
| `addPageTagsById` (2084) | `addPageTagsById(pageId: number, tags: string[], opts?: {tagSource?: 'frontmatter'}): Promise<void>` | Adds many tags to one page by its id in one statement, with the same claim rules as `addTag`. The caller holds the page guard. An empty list does nothing. | `tags.ts:50-57`. `INSERT INTO tags(page_id,tag,tag_source) SELECT $1, t, '<source>' FROM (SELECT DISTINCT unnest($2::text[]) AS t) u ON CONFLICT (page_id,tag) DO UPDATE SET tag_source = '<source>'`. Default source is `'added'` and the update is unconditional (an explicit add claims the row). With `tagSource: 'frontmatter'` the source is `'frontmatter'` and the update only runs `WHERE tags.tag_source IS NULL`, so it adopts a legacy row but never overrides `'added'`. PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2283` uses `this.engineSql`. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2138` uses `this.autocommitSql`. |

Parity: both engines call `tagsImpl.getTags` with the same SQL. `addTag`/`removeTag` are the same shared function. The dialect difference is only the lock call. `PGLITE_CAPABILITIES.transactionAdvisoryLocks=false` (`dialect-pglite.ts:27`) and Postgres sets it to `true` (`dialect-postgres.ts:55`). I did not trace what `lockPageKeys` does on each dialect.

### Timeline

| Method (iface line) | Signature | What it does | SQL / impl |
|---|---|---|---|
| `addTimelineEntry` (1880) | `addTimelineEntry(slug, entry: TimelineInput, opts?: {skipExistenceCheck?, sourceId?}): Promise<boolean>` | Inserts one timeline row. Returns `true` if inserted, `false` if deduped. Throws `PageMissingError` when the page is missing and `skipExistenceCheck` is not set. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2094`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:1916`, both call `engine-sql/timeline.ts:28-67`. One CTE: `page_state` selects `pages` by `(slug, source_id)` `FOR KEY SHARE`, then `inserted` does `INSERT INTO timeline_entries(page_id,date,source,summary,detail) ... ON CONFLICT (page_id,date,md5(summary),source) DO NOTHING RETURNING 1`. Free-text fields go through `sanitizeForJsonb`. Dedup index is `idx_timeline_dedup` (`schema.sql:670`). |
| `addTimelineEntriesBatch` (1897) | `addTimelineEntriesBatch(entries: TimelineBatchInput[], opts?: BatchOpts): Promise<number>` | Bulk insert. Returns count of rows actually inserted. Empty input returns 0 with no query. | Both engines: `batchRetry(...)` wrapper (`pglite-engine.ts:2102`, `postgres-engine.ts:1924`). Impl `timeline.ts:70-90`: `executeRawJsonb` over `jsonb_to_recordset(($1::jsonb)->'rows') AS v(slug,date,source,summary,detail,source_id)`, `JOIN pages p ON p.slug=v.slug AND p.source_id=v.source_id AND p.deleted_at IS NULL`, `ON CONFLICT (page_id,date,md5(summary),source) DO NOTHING RETURNING 1`. Rows built by `buildTimelineRows` (`batch-rows.ts:161`). |
| `getTimeline` (1898) | `getTimeline(slug, opts?: TimelineOpts): Promise<TimelineEntry[]>` | Entries for a slug, newest first. Supports `after`, `before`, `limit` (default 100), `excludePrivate`, `eligibility`, `sourceIds`/`sourceId`. | `timeline.ts:93-126`. `FROM timeline_entries te JOIN pages p ON p.id=te.page_id` when scoped. When unscoped it uses `sources s CROSS JOIN LATERAL (SELECT * FROM pages WHERE source_id=s.id AND slug=$ OFFSET 0) p JOIN timeline_entries te` (`:114-118`). The `OFFSET 0` fences the planner from driving off `pages` (comment cites Cat7-1). Ordering: `te.date DESC, te.id DESC LIMIT n`. Read is `LegacyUnscopedRead`. |
| `removeTimelineRows` (2157) | `removeTimelineRows(pageId: number, rows: TimelineRowRemoval[]): Promise<{date, removed}[]>` | Deletes the page's own (non-event) timeline rows that `rows` name and that still match them on every field (id, date, source, summary, detail). A row changed since it was read stays. A row with an event page (an event projection) is never touched. Returns each deleted row's date as `YYYY-MM-DD` and its `removed` mark. | `timeline.ts:290-292`, via `executeRaw` with the rows as one `JSON.stringify` text bound as `$2::text::jsonb`. `DELETE FROM timeline_entries t USING jsonb_to_recordset($2::text::jsonb) AS d(id integer, date date, source text, summary text, detail text, removed boolean) WHERE t.page_id = $1 AND t.event_page_id IS NULL AND t.id = d.id AND t.date = d.date AND t.source = d.source AND t.summary = d.summary AND t.detail = d.detail RETURNING t.date::text AS date, d.removed` (`timeline.ts:278`). The result order is whatever the delete returns, with no `ORDER BY`. A page write calls it inside its own transaction. PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2329` uses `this.engineSql`. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2184` uses `this.autocommitSql`. |
| `updateTimelineDetails` (2162) | `updateTimelineDetails(pageId: number, rows: TimelineDetailRefresh[]): Promise<void>` | Replaces the detail of the page's own (non-event) timeline rows named in `rows` with `next`, when the stored detail still equals the preimage `detail`. Any other row stays. Event projections are never touched. | `timeline.ts:295-297`. `UPDATE timeline_entries t SET detail = r.next FROM jsonb_to_recordset($2::text::jsonb) AS r(id integer, detail text, next text) WHERE t.page_id = $1 AND t.event_page_id IS NULL AND t.id = r.id AND t.detail = r.detail` (`timeline.ts:282`). Returns nothing, so the caller cannot see how many rows changed. PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2330` uses `this.engineSql`. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2185` uses `this.autocommitSql`. |

Parity: both engines share `engine-sql/timeline.ts`. The `addTimelineEntry` CTE with `FOR KEY SHARE` and the `getTimeline` LATERAL are Postgres planner or locking features. PGLite runs them on its single-connection WASM Postgres, so the same SQL text is used and the locking is not exercised the same way.

### Life Chronicle (timeline reads)

All five filter `pages.deleted_at IS NULL` on the depth page and hide soft-deleted event projections. The shared shape is in `timeline.ts:129-143` (`chronicleSourceCond`). It applies a source scope: `sourceIds` first, then `sourceId`, applied to `p` or to the event alias `ep` (`ep=true`). `excludePrivate` adds `privatePagesFilterForCaller('p')` and `privateTimelineEventFilterForCaller('te')`.

| Method (iface line) | Signature | What it does | SQL / impl |
|---|---|---|---|
| `getTimelineForDate` (1904) | `getTimelineForDate(date: string, opts?: ChronicleTimelineOpts): Promise<ChronicleTimelineRow[]>` | Rows for one day, or the ISO week when `opts.week`. Limit default 200. | `timeline.ts:146-166`. Week bounds are `date_trunc('week', d)::date` through `+6 days`, else `d::date`. Joins `timeline_entries te JOIN pages p ... AND p.deleted_at IS NULL LEFT JOIN pages ep ON ep.id=te.event_page_id`. Filter `(te.event_page_id IS NULL OR ep.deleted_at IS NULL)`. Order `COALESCE(ep.effective_date, te.date::timestamptz) ASC, te.id ASC`. Returns `kind` from `ep.frontmatter->'event'->>'kind'`. |
| `getSince` (1906) | `getSince(date: string, opts?: ChronicleTimelineOpts): Promise<ChronicleTimelineRow[]>` | Rows on or after `date`. Optional `kind` filter. Limit 200. | `timeline.ts:169-188`. Same join and order as above. Adds `te.date >= $date::date` and an optional `ep.frontmatter->'event'->>'kind' = $kind`. |
| `getOnThisDay` (1908) | `getOnThisDay(opts?: PageReadScope & {date?, limit?}): Promise<ChronicleTimelineRow[]>` | Same month and day as `date` (default today) in prior years only. Limit 50. | `timeline.ts:191-211`. `EXTRACT(MONTH/DAY FROM te.date) = EXTRACT(... FROM target)`, `te.date < target`. `target` is `$date::date` or `current_date` (DB session clock). Order `te.date DESC, te.id ASC`. |
| `getLastSeen` (1910) | `getLastSeen(entitySlug, opts?: PageReadScope & {asof?}): Promise<LastSeenResult>` | Most recent date an entity is seen: its own page's timeline, or an event whose `who` array names it exactly or by wikilink (`[[slug]]`, `[[slug|label]]`). Substring matches are excluded. | `timeline.ts:214-253`. `te.date <= seenThrough` (`asof::date` or `current_date`). Matching is `p.slug = $slug` OR `EXISTS (jsonb_array_elements_text(ep.frontmatter->'event'->'who') w WHERE w.name = $slug OR w.name LIKE '%[[slug]]%' ESCAPE '\' OR w.name LIKE '%[[slug|%' ESCAPE '\')`. The LIKE metacharacters in the slug are escaped. Order `te.date DESC, COALESCE(ep.effective_date, te.date::timestamptz) DESC, te.id DESC LIMIT 1`. `finalizeLastSeen` (`chronicle/last-seen.ts:11`) computes `days_ago` in JS from UTC midnight and `Date.now()` (or `asof`). |
| `upsertEventProjection` (1917) | `upsertEventProjection(opts: {depthSlug, eventSlug, date, summary, detail?, sourceId?}): Promise<{projected: boolean}>` | Upserts the date-index row for an event page, keyed `(event_page_id, date)`. Returns `projected=false` when either slug is missing. | `timeline.ts:256-269`. `INSERT INTO timeline_entries(page_id,date,source,summary,detail,event_page_id) SELECT dp.id, $date::date, 'life-chronicle:event:'||$eventSlug, $summary, $detail, ep.id FROM pages dp, pages ep WHERE dp.slug=$depth AND dp.source_id=$src AND ep.slug=$event AND ep.source_id=$src ON CONFLICT (event_page_id, date) WHERE event_page_id IS NOT NULL DO UPDATE SET summary, detail, page_id, source RETURNING id`. Partial unique index `idx_timeline_event_dedup` (`schema.sql:674`). |

Parity for this family: `PGLite` (`pglite-engine.ts:2111-2131`) and `Postgres` (`postgres-engine.ts:1933-1952`) both call `timelineImpl` with the same unscoped executor. Both `getOnThisDay` and `getLastSeen` depend on the database session date (`current_date`), not on the process clock. `getLastSeen`'s `days_ago` uses the JS clock. Parity risk is listed at the end.

### Ontology (rides `facts`)

Ontology is inlined per engine, not in engine-sql. The shared logic is in `chronicle/ontology.ts`. The facts table is defined elsewhere in this research set. Here the relevant columns are `source_id`, `entity_slug`, `dimension`, `value`, `value_hash`, `dim_status`, `confidence`, `source_markdown_slug`, `valid_from`, `valid_until`, `expired_at`, `superseded_by`, `consolidated_into`, `visibility`, `trust_tier`.

| Method (iface line) | Signature | What it does | SQL / impl |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mergeOntologyFact` (1928) | `mergeOntologyFact(obs: OntologyObservationInput): Promise<OntologyMergeResult>` | Records one observation (entity has dimension=value). Returns `action` of `noop`, `corroborated`, `superseded_prior` or `inserted`. Defaults: confidence 0.7, visibility `private`, status `quarantined` for novel dimensions, else `active`. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2131-2198` (`this.db.query`). Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:1953-2023` (tagged templates). Steps: (1) normalize `dimension`, `valueHash` (`ontology.ts:12-32`); (2) find current open row: `expired_at IS NULL AND valid_until IS NULL AND (dim_status IS NULL OR dim_status='active')`, `ORDER BY valid_from DESC NULLS LAST, confidence DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1`; (3a) same value and not backdated: check for an existing row from the same provenance (`source_markdown_slug`) at or after current `valid_from`; if found, `noop`; else insert a row with `expired_at=now()` and `consolidated_into=current.id`, `ON CONFLICT (source_id,entity_slug,dimension,value_hash,source_markdown_slug,valid_from) WHERE dimension IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING RETURNING id`, giving `corroborated`; (3b) otherwise insert a new row (before either insert, `freeKey` clears the slot of a conversation fact holding the same stint key, so the observation can be stored; PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2353-2369`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2211-2225`; the current-row and same-provenance reads skip conversation slots with `NOT_CONVERSATION_SLOT_SQL`), then if current is open and status is `active` and the new `valid_from` is not earlier, close the prior row with `UPDATE facts SET valid_until=COALESCE($validFrom, now()), superseded_by=$new WHERE id=$cur AND valid_until IS NULL` + `ONTOLOGY_SUPERSEDE_GUARD`, giving `superseded_prior`, else `inserted`. A backdated conflicting value is inserted without touching the prior row. |
| `getOntology` (1930) | `getOntology(entitySlug, opts?: OntologyReadOpts): Promise<OntologyValue[]>` | Current resolved value per dimension at `asof` (default now). Honors `minConfidence` (default 0), `includeQuarantined`, `sourceId`/`sourceIds`, `visibility[]`, `excludePrivate`, `excludeDiary`. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2200-2227`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2025-2050`. `SELECT DISTINCT ON (dimension) dimension, value, confidence, source_markdown_slug AS source, valid_from, valid_until AS valid_to, COALESCE(dim_status,'active') AS status, id AS fact_id FROM facts WHERE entity_slug=$ AND dimension IS NOT NULL AND expired_at IS NULL <scope> <deletedPageHidden> [<privateFactFilterForReader>] [<excludeDiary filter, AND NOT starts_with(source_markdown_slug,'life/diary/')>] AND NOT conversation slot <visibility> AND valid_from <= asof AND valid_until > asof AND confidence >= $min AND ($includeQ OR dim_status IS NULL OR dim_status='active') ORDER BY dimension, valid_from DESC NULLS LAST, confidence DESC, id DESC`. Page-visibility gate runs before `DISTINCT ON` (comment at `postgres-engine.ts:2033`). |
| `discoverOntologyDimensions` (1932) | `discoverOntologyDimensions(opts?: {sourceId?, sourceIds?}): Promise<OntologyDimensionStat[]>` | Lists every dimension in the brain with entity and observation counts. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2229-2244`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2052-2063`. `SELECT dimension, count(DISTINCT entity_slug)::int AS entities, count(*)::int AS observations FROM facts WHERE dimension IS NOT NULL AND expired_at IS NULL AND NOT conversation slot <scope> GROUP BY dimension ORDER BY entities DESC, dimension`. No privacy filter in this query. |
| `findOntologyConflicts` (1934) | `findOntologyConflicts(opts?: PageReadScope & {minConfidence?, visibility?, excludeDiary?}): Promise<OntologyConflict[]>` | Dimensions with at least 2 distinct current values from at least 2 distinct provenances. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2246-2270`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2065-2091`. CTE `cur` selects open rows (`expired_at IS NULL AND valid_until IS NULL AND dim_status active`, `confidence >= $min`, scope, page gates, visibility). Then `GROUP BY entity_slug, dimension HAVING count(DISTINCT value) >= 2 AND count(DISTINCT source) >= 2`, returning `json_agg(json_build_object('value',..,'source',..,'confidence',..,'fact_id',..) ORDER BY fact_id)`. Rows that hold a conversation slot are left out (`NOT_CONVERSATION_SLOT_SQL`), `excludeDiary` drops provenance under `life/diary/`, and `excludePrivate` uses `privateFactFilterForReader` (PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2456-2475`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2305-2325`, at `116d737413`). |

Parity: the logic is identical. Driver differences only. PGLite builds params as an array with `$n` placeholders and a dynamic scope. Postgres uses `sql\`...\`` fragments and `sql.unsafe()` for the privacy fragment. PGLite parses `json_agg` text with `JSON.parse` when needed (`pglite-engine.ts:2272`).

### Raw data

| Method (iface line) | Signature | What it does | SQL / impl |
|---|---|---|---|
| `putRawData` (1943) | `putRawData(slug, source, data: object, opts?: {sourceId?}): Promise<void>` | Upserts one raw_data sidecar row per `(page, source)`. Throws if the page is missing. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2277-2320`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2093-2128`. With `sourceId`: `INSERT INTO raw_data(page_id,source,data) SELECT id,$2,$3::jsonb FROM pages WHERE slug=$1 AND source_id=$4 ON CONFLICT (page_id,source) DO UPDATE SET data=EXCLUDED.data, fetched_at=now() RETURNING id`. Without `sourceId`: the same without the source filter (matches every same-slug page; comment at `postgres-engine.ts:2101`, `pglite-engine.ts:2284`). Zero rows throws `putRawData failed: page ... not found`. The two engines differ only in JSON binding: PGLite `JSON.stringify` with `$3::jsonb`, Postgres `sql.json`. |
| `getRawData` (1949) | `getRawData(slug, source?, opts?: PageReadScope & {includeDeleted?}): Promise<RawData[]>` | Returns `{source, data, fetched_at}` rows for a slug. Hides rows of soft-deleted pages unless `includeDeleted`. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2322-2352` builds a dynamic WHERE. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2130-2168` has explicit branches. Both produce `FROM raw_data rd JOIN pages p ON p.id=rd.page_id WHERE p.slug=$1 [AND rd.source=$] [AND p.deleted_at IS NULL] [AND p.source_id = ANY/=] [AND privatePagesFilter]`. `sourceIds[]` wins over `sourceId`. Rows come back ordered by `p.source_id COLLATE "C", rd.source COLLATE "C"` on both engines (`pglite-engine.ts:2557`, `postgres-engine.ts:2384-2404`, at `116d737413`). Read is unscoped on master, not `LegacyUnscopedRead` in the engine (this method has no engine-sql home). |

### Files

Files are binary asset metadata only. Image bytes never enter the DB. Shared SQL is in `engine-sql/files.ts`, used by both engines. Schema: `files` (`schema.sql:1136-1149`), `UNIQUE(storage_path)`.

| Method (iface line) | Signature | What it does | SQL / impl |
|---|---|---|---|
| `upsertFile` (1954) | `upsertFile(spec: FileSpec): Promise<{id, created}>` | Upserts a file row keyed on `storage_path`. `created` is true on insert. | `files.ts:50-68`. `INSERT INTO files(source_id,page_slug,page_id,filename,storage_path,mime_type,size_bytes,content_hash,metadata) VALUES(...) ON CONFLICT (storage_path) DO UPDATE SET page_slug, page_id, filename, mime_type, size_bytes, content_hash, metadata = EXCLUDED.* RETURNING id, (xmax = 0) AS created`. `source_id` defaults to `'default'`, `metadata` to `{}` via `jsonbParam`. The `DO UPDATE` list does not touch `source_id`. |
| `getFile` (1955) | `getFile(sourceId, storagePath): Promise<FileRow \| null>` | Looks up one file by source and path. | `files.ts:70-78`. `SELECT ... FROM files WHERE source_id=$1 AND storage_path=$2 LIMIT 1`. Read is `LegacyUnscopedRead`. The row goes through `fileRow`, which turns the BIGINT `size_bytes` into a number on every engine (`files.ts:18`, `files.ts:40` at `116d737413`). |
| `listFilesForPage` (1956) | `listFilesForPage(pageId: number): Promise<FileRow[]>` | Files attached to a page. | `files.ts:80-88`. `SELECT ... FROM files WHERE page_id=$1 ORDER BY created_at ASC, id ASC` (the `id` tie-break and the `size_bytes` number conversion are at `files.ts:72` at `116d737413`). |
| `listFiles` (2203) | `listFiles(opts: {pageSlug?: string \| null, limit: number}): Promise<FileRow[]>` | Lists the files `gbrain files list` prints. With a `pageSlug`, one slug's files from any source by filename. Without it, every file by page slug then filename. At most `limit` rows. | `files.ts:54-70`. With a slug, `SELECT id, source_id, page_slug, page_id, filename, storage_path, mime_type, size_bytes, content_hash, metadata, created_at FROM files WHERE page_slug = $1 ORDER BY filename, id LIMIT $limit`. Without, the same select with `ORDER BY page_slug, filename, id LIMIT $limit`. The `id` tie-break keeps equal names in one fixed order, and the filename and slug orders follow the database collation. No source filter. Both engines pass the executor through `unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql, 'files: unscoped on master (EO4 inventory)')`. PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2576`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2422`. Same SQL on both engines. |

Both engines: `pglite-engine.ts:2354-2364` and `postgres-engine.ts:2170-2180` delegate to `filesImpl`. Engine-level difference: none in SQL. The write path uses `this.engineSql` on both.

### Takes (typed, weighted, attributed claims)

Takes live in table `takes`, keyed `(page_id, row_num)`, with `active`, `superseded_by`, `resolved_*` columns and optional `embedding` (pgvector) plus `embedding_model`, `embedded_text_hash`, `embedded_at`. Takes carry no `source_id`; source scope goes through the page join. Engine-level methods are one-liners that delegate to `engine-sql/takes.ts` for both engines (PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2571-2706`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2430-2562`). The `addTakesBatch` / `updateTakeEmbeddings` wrappers pass `batchRetry` to the impl.

| Method (iface line) | Signature | What it does | SQL / impl (`engine-sql/takes.ts`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `addTakesBatch` (1978) | `addTakesBatch(rows: TakeBatchInput[], opts?: BatchOpts): Promise<number>` | Bulk insert or update takes. Returns count of rows inserted, updated, or already current. Weight is clamped to [0,1] and rounded, with a `TAKES_WEIGHT_CLAMPED` stderr line. | `:47-102`. `buildTakeRows` (`batch-rows.ts:177`) clamps weight and NUL-strips claim and source. Then `executeRawJsonb`: `INSERT INTO takes(...) SELECT ... FROM jsonb_to_recordset(($1::jsonb)->'rows') AS v(...) ON CONFLICT (page_id,row_num) DO UPDATE SET embedding/embedded_at/embedding_model/embedded_text_hash = NULL when claim changes, claim, kind, holder, weight, since_date, until_date, source, superseded_by, active, updated_at=now() WHERE (...) IS DISTINCT FROM (...)`. A row that is already current is not written. An intra-batch duplicate `(page_id,row_num)` fails the statement (`:62-63`). |
| `updateTakeEmbeddings` (1981) | `updateTakeEmbeddings(rows: TakeEmbeddingInput[], opts?: BatchOpts): Promise<number>` | Stores vectors for active takes. Vectors whose claim changed in flight are discarded. Returns count updated. | `:458-510`. Validates `take_id` (positive int, no duplicates), claim and model strings, finite floats. `UPDATE takes AS t SET embedding=v.embedding::vector, embedded_at=now(), embedding_model=v.model, embedded_text_hash=md5(v.claim), updated_at=now() FROM jsonb_to_recordset(...) AS v(take_id bigint, embedding text, claim text, model text) WHERE t.id=v.take_id AND t.active AND t.claim=v.claim RETURNING t.id`. |
| `listTakes` (1984) | `listTakes(opts?: TakesListOpts): Promise<Take[]>` | Filtered list of takes with `page_slug`. Default `active=true`, limit 100 (max 500), offset. | `:294-341`. `FROM takes t JOIN pages p`. Filters: `page_id`, `page_slug`, `holder`, `kind`, `active` (`NULL` means any), `resolved` (true/false/undefined), `takesHoldersAllowList` (`holder = ANY`), `sinceOnOrBefore` (text compare with partial-date padding), `excludePrivate` (also applies `ownerOnlyTakeAllowedSql('t')`, `takes.ts:311`), `sourceIds`/`sourceId` (`p.source_id`), `eligibility` (`projectionEligibleSql`). Sort: `ungradedFirst` (EXISTS in `take_grade_cache`), then `sortBy` in `since_date` / `weight` / `created_at` (default), with `oldestFirst`, tie-break `t.id DESC`. |
| `searchTakes` (1991) | `searchTakes(query, opts?): Promise<TakeHit[]>` | Keyword (trigram) search over active take claims. Limit 30 (max 100). | `:343-371`. `word_similarity(query, t.claim)::real AS score FROM takes t JOIN pages p WHERE t.active AND $query <% t.claim [privacy] [holder allow-list] [source scope] [eligibility] ORDER BY score DESC, t.weight DESC LIMIT n`. Uses the pg_trgm `<%` operator. `excludePrivate` also applies `ownerOnlyTakeAllowedSql('t')` (`takes.ts:358`, and `takes.ts:393` for `searchTakesVector`). Rows go through `takeHitRowToHit` (BigInt coercion). |
| `searchTakesVector` (1997) | `searchTakesVector(embedding: Float32Array, opts?): Promise<TakeHit[]>` | Cosine search over stored take vectors. Limit 30 (max 100). | `:373-406`. Vector is rendered as `[v1,v2,...]`. `SELECT ..., (1 - (t.embedding <=> $vec::vector))::real AS score FROM takes t JOIN pages p WHERE t.active AND t.embedding IS NOT NULL [privacy] [holder] [source] [eligibility] ORDER BY t.embedding <=> $vec::vector LIMIT n`. Rows with no embedding simply do not match, so the result is `[]` when none are embedded. No dimension check in SQL. |
| `getTakeEmbeddings` (2003) | `getTakeEmbeddings(ids: number[]): Promise<Map<number, Float32Array>>` | Vectors by take id. Mirrors `getEmbeddingsByChunkIds`. | `:408-419`. `SELECT id, embedding FROM takes WHERE id = ANY($ids::bigint[]) AND embedding IS NOT NULL`. Parsed with `tryParseEmbedding`. Returns an empty Map for empty input. |
| `countStaleTakes` (2006) | `countStaleTakes(opts?: StaleTakeOpts): Promise<number>` | Count of stale takes (missing vector, claim drift, or other model or width when the caller names a target). | `:427-444`. Shared predicate `staleTakeWhere` (`:427-436`): `t.active AND p.deleted_at IS NULL AND EXISTS(sources s WHERE s.id=p.source_id AND NOT s.archived) AND (sourceId filter) AND (t.embedding IS NULL OR t.embedded_text_hash IS DISTINCT FROM md5(t.claim) OR (model given AND embedding_model IS DISTINCT FROM model) OR (dims given AND vector_dims(embedding) <> dims))`. Read is `LegacyUnscopedRead`. |
| `listStaleTakes` (2009) | `listStaleTakes(opts?: StaleTakeOpts): Promise<StaleTakeRow[]>` | Same predicate, returns `take_id`, `page_slug`, `row_num`, `claim`. No vector in payload. | `:446-456`. Same predicate, `ORDER BY t.id LIMIT 100000`. The hard cap is silent. |
| `updateTake` (2016) | `updateTake(pageId, rowNum, fields: {weight?, since_date?, source?}): Promise<void>` | Mutates weight, since_date or source. Cannot change claim, kind or holder. | `:512-538`. Weight is normalized with `normalizeWeightForStorage` (clamp, stderr note). `UPDATE takes SET weight=COALESCE($w::real, weight), since_date=COALESCE(...), source=COALESCE(...), updated_at=now() WHERE page_id=$ AND row_num=$ RETURNING 1`. Zero rows throws `TAKE_ROW_NOT_FOUND`. |
| `supersedeTake` (2030) | `supersedeTake(pageId, oldRow, newRow): Promise<{oldRow, newRow}>` | Transactionally marks the old row inactive and appends a new row at `MAX(row_num)+1`. Refuses resolved takes. | `:540-569`, inside `exec.transaction`. (1) `SELECT resolved_at FROM takes WHERE page_id AND row_num=oldRow`: missing throws `TAKE_ROW_NOT_FOUND`, resolved throws `TAKE_RESOLVED_IMMUTABLE`. (2) `SELECT COALESCE(MAX(row_num),0)+1 AS next FROM takes WHERE page_id=$`. (3) `INSERT INTO takes(...) VALUES(...)`, weight clamped to [0,1] (default 0.5). (4) `UPDATE takes SET active=false, superseded_by=newRow, updated_at=now() WHERE page_id AND row_num=oldRow`. No row lock is taken for the MAX step. |
| `resolveTake` (2047) | `resolveTake(pageId, rowNum, resolution: TakeResolution): Promise<void>` | Sets the resolved state on a take. Immutable once set. | `:571-592`. Missing row throws `TAKE_ROW_NOT_FOUND`. Already resolved throws `TAKE_ALREADY_RESOLVED`. `deriveResolutionTuple` (`takes-resolution.ts:22`) maps `quality` (correct, incorrect, partial, unresolvable) to `outcome` (true, false, null). `quality` wins if both are set and disagree, and throws. `UPDATE takes SET resolved_at=now(), resolved_quality, resolved_outcome, resolved_value, resolved_unit, resolved_source, resolved_by, updated_at=now() WHERE page_id AND row_num`. |
| `getScorecard` (2062) | `getScorecard(opts: TakesScorecardOpts, allowList: string[] \| undefined): Promise<TakesScorecard>` | Aggregate calibration: totals, accuracy, Brier, partial rate, unresolvable rate. | `:599-638`. One aggregate `SELECT`: `COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE kind='bet')` as total_bets, `FILTER (resolved_quality IN ('correct','incorrect','partial'))` as resolved, FILTER counts for correct, incorrect, partial, unresolvable, and `AVG(POWER(weight - (correct?1:0),2))` over correct and incorrect as brier. Filters: `holder`, `domainPrefix` (EXISTS page slug LIKE prefix%, with `domainPrefixAlso` extra prefixes matched through `LIKE ANY`, `takes.ts:602-603` at `116d737413`), `since`/`until` (on `since_date`), allow-list (`holder = ANY`, inside the GROUP BY scope, so hidden holders contribute zero), source scope, `excludePrivate` (with `ownerOnlyTakeAllowedSql('takes')`, `takes.ts:636`). `finalizeScorecard` (`takes-resolution.ts:90`): accuracy = correct/(correct+incorrect), brier null if no binary bet, partial_rate = partial/resolved, unresolvable_rate = unresolvable/(resolved+unresolvable). |
| `getCalibrationCurve` (2070) | `getCalibrationCurve(opts: CalibrationCurveOpts, allowList): Promise<CalibrationBucket[]>` | Bins resolved correct and incorrect bets by stated weight. Returns observed hit rate and predicted mean per bucket. Excludes partial. | `:650-694`. `bucketSize` clamped to (0,1], default 0.1. `maxIdx = floor(1/bucketSize)-1`. CTE `binned`: `bucket_idx = LEAST(FLOOR(weight::numeric / bucketSize::numeric)::int, maxIdx)`, `hit = (resolved_quality='correct')::int`, over `resolved_quality IN ('correct','incorrect')` with holder, allow-list, source and privacy filters (the privacy filter also applies `ownerOnlyTakeAllowedSql('takes')`, `takes.ts:677`). Output `bucket_lo`, `bucket_hi` (`bucket_idx*size` as numeric then `::float`), `n = COUNT(*)`, `observed = AVG(hit)`, `predicted = AVG(weight)`. Only buckets that contain rows are returned. NUMERIC arithmetic is used on purpose so bucket boundaries like 0.7/0.1 do not drift (comment `:661-665`). |
| `addSynthesisEvidence` (2073) | `addSynthesisEvidence(rows: SynthesisEvidenceInput[]): Promise<number>` | Records which takes a synthesis page cited. Idempotent. Returns number inserted. | `:696-712`. `INSERT INTO synthesis_evidence(synthesis_page_id,take_page_id,take_row_num,citation_index) SELECT v.* FROM unnest($int[], $int[], $int[], $int[]) AS v(...) ON CONFLICT (synthesis_page_id,take_page_id,take_row_num) DO NOTHING RETURNING 1`. Returns `result.length`. |
| `matchTakeRows` (2358) | `matchTakeRows(pageId: number, rows: TakeFenceRow[], limit: number): Promise<number[]>` | Returns the row numbers of the page's stored takes whose claim, kind or holder differs (null-safe) from the fence row with the same number in `rows`. Ascending, at most `limit`. A page write refuses a fence that would take those rows over. | `takes.ts:721-724` (`TAKE_COLLISIONS_SQL`) and `takes.ts:728-730`. `SELECT k.row_num FROM takes k JOIN jsonb_to_recordset($2::text::jsonb) AS n(row_num integer, claim text, kind text, holder text) ON n.row_num = k.row_num WHERE k.page_id = $1 AND (k.claim, k.kind, k.holder) IS DISTINCT FROM (n.claim, n.kind, n.holder) ORDER BY k.row_num LIMIT $3`. A stored row with no fence row of the same number is not returned. The `IS DISTINCT FROM` row comparison is the null-safe part. Row numbers are coerced with `Number`. PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2795` uses `unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql, ...)`. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2684` runs the same inside `preparationRead(...)`, so a preparer's read deadline bounds it. |
| `deleteTakeRows` (2360) | `deleteTakeRows(pageId: number, rowNums: number[]): Promise<void>` | Deletes the page's takes at `rowNums`, together with their synthesis evidence. An empty list deletes nothing. | `takes.ts:725` and `takes.ts:733-735`. `DELETE FROM takes WHERE page_id = $1 AND row_num = ANY($2::integer[])`. The statement itself only names `takes`. Synthesis evidence goes with the take through `FOREIGN KEY (take_page_id, take_row_num) REFERENCES takes(page_id, row_num) ON DELETE CASCADE` (`schema-migrations/v037-takes-and-synthesis-evidence.ts:66-67`). A Rust store must delete the evidence rows too. PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2796` uses `this.engineSql`. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2685` uses `this.autocommitSql`. |

Engine-level differences: none in SQL. Both engines call the same `takesImpl` functions with the same executor shape. Typed-signature difference only: PGLite's `searchTakes` and `searchTakesVector` opts type (`pglite-engine.ts:2648-2658`) omits `sourceId` and `sourceIds`, while Postgres's includes them (`postgres-engine.ts:2507-2513`). Runtime passes the full opts object through, so behavior matches.

### Dream-cycle verdict cache

Table `dream_verdicts`, keyed `(file_path, content_hash)`. Index `dream_verdicts_expires_idx` (`schema.sql:1505`). `expires_at` defaults to `now()+30 days` at the table level. Writes set it explicitly.

| Method (iface line) | Signature | What it does | SQL / impl |
|---|---|---|---|
| `getDreamVerdict` (2078) | `getDreamVerdict(filePath, contentHash): Promise<DreamVerdict \| null>` | Cache lookup. Expired rows are misses. NULL-expiry rows count as hits. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2367-2400`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2183-2216`. `SELECT worth_processing, reasons, judged_at, score, content_type, segments, entities, model, triage_version FROM dream_verdicts WHERE file_path=$ AND content_hash=$ AND (expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > now())`. Maps null arrays to `[]`, `judged_at` to ISO string. Note the NULL-expiry branch, documented as the #4657 bootstrap window (`postgres-engine.ts:2200`; PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2383`). |
| `putDreamVerdict` (2079) | `putDreamVerdict(filePath, contentHash, verdict: DreamVerdictInput): Promise<void>` | Upserts a verdict and resets the TTL. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2402-2430`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2218-2243`. `wellFormedJson(verdict)` first. `INSERT INTO dream_verdicts(file_path,content_hash,worth_processing,reasons,score,content_type,segments,entities,model,triage_version,expires_at) VALUES(...,now()+make_interval(secs => TTL)) ON CONFLICT (file_path,content_hash) DO UPDATE SET <all fields>=EXCLUDED.*, judged_at=now(), expires_at=EXCLUDED.expires_at`. `TTL = DREAM_VERDICT_TTL_SECONDS` (`engine.ts:528`). JSON columns: PGLite `$4::jsonb` with `JSON.stringify`, Postgres `sql.json`. |
| `sweepDreamVerdicts` (2084) | `sweepDreamVerdicts(): Promise<number>` | Deletes expired verdicts. Housekeeping only. | PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2432` returns `affectedRows`. Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2245` returns `count`. Both run `DELETE FROM dream_verdicts WHERE expires_at <= now()`. NULL-expiry rows are not swept. |

### Contradiction probe (batched takes, cache, run history)

Tables: `eval_contradictions_runs` (`schema.sql:1631`), `eval_contradictions_cache` (`schema.sql:1614`, expiry index at `:1626`), and `take_grade_cache` (referenced by `listTakes` ordering, `schema.sql:1727`). All SQL lives in `engine-sql/takes.ts` for both engines.

| Method (iface line) | Signature | What it does | SQL / impl |
|---|---|---|---|
| `listActiveTakesForPages` (2102) | `listActiveTakesForPages(pageIds: number[], opts?: {takesHoldersAllowList?}): Promise<Map<number, Take[]>>` | Active takes for many pages in one round-trip. Every requested page id is a key, with `[]` if it has none. | `takes.ts:109-135`. `SELECT t.*, p.slug AS page_slug FROM takes t JOIN pages p ON p.id=t.page_id WHERE t.page_id = ANY($::int[]) AND t.active = true AND (allow-list IS NULL OR holder = ANY(...)) ORDER BY t.page_id, t.row_num`. Read is `LegacyUnscopedRead`. No `deleted_at` or privacy filter. |
| `writeContradictionsRun` (2115) | `writeContradictionsRun(row: {run_id, judge_model, prompt_version, queries_evaluated, queries_with_contradiction, total_contradictions_flagged, wilson_ci_lower, wilson_ci_upper, judge_errors_total, cost_usd_total, duration_ms, source_tier_breakdown, report_json}): Promise<boolean>` | Persists one probe run row. Idempotent on `run_id`. Returns true if inserted. | `takes.ts:141-174`. `INSERT INTO eval_contradictions_runs(...) VALUES(...,${jsonbParam(source_tier_breakdown)},${jsonbParam(report_json)}) ON CONFLICT (run_id) DO NOTHING`, returning `affectedRows > 0`. |
| `loadContradictionsTrend` (2137) | `loadContradictionsTrend(days: number): Promise<Array<{run_id, ran_at, ... report_json}>>` | Runs from the last N days, newest first. | `takes.ts:180-221`. Cutoff is computed in JS: `Date.now() - max(0,days)*86400000`. `SELECT ... FROM eval_contradictions_runs WHERE ran_at >= $cutoff ORDER BY ran_at DESC`. No LIMIT. Numeric columns go through `Number()`. `ran_at` becomes an ISO string. Read is `LegacyUnscopedRead`. |
| `getContradictionCacheEntry` (2163) | `getContradictionCacheEntry(key: {chunk_a_hash, chunk_b_hash, model_id, prompt_version, truncation_policy}): Promise<Record<string, unknown> \| null>` | Judge-verdict cache lookup. Key is the full five-part key, so prompt or truncation changes miss. | `takes.ts:227-247`. `SELECT verdict FROM eval_contradictions_cache WHERE <5 key cols> AND expires_at > now() LIMIT 1`. Read is `LegacyUnscopedRead`. |
| `putContradictionCacheEntry` (2180) | `putContradictionCacheEntry(opts: {...5 key cols, verdict, ttl_seconds?}): Promise<void>` | Upserts a verdict and slides the TTL. | `takes.ts:253-279`. TTL = `max(60, ttl_seconds ?? 30d)`. `expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + ttl*1000)` is computed in JS. `INSERT INTO eval_contradictions_cache(...,verdict,expires_at) VALUES(...,${jsonbParam(verdict)},$expiresAt) ON CONFLICT (5 key cols) DO UPDATE SET verdict, expires_at, created_at=now()`. |
| `sweepContradictionCache` (2194) | `sweepContradictionCache(): Promise<number>` | Deletes expired judge-cache rows. Called periodically from `cache.ts`. | `takes.ts:282-287`. `DELETE FROM eval_contradictions_cache WHERE expires_at <= now()`, returns `affectedRows`. |

Engine-level: all seven delegate to `takesImpl` in both engines (PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2575-2642`, Postgres `postgres-engine.ts:2434-2500`). Writes use `this.engineSql` (`SqlExecutor`), and the `loadContradictionsTrend`, `listActiveTakesForPages`, and cache reads use `unscopedExecutor`.

Counts are the number of files under `test/` that name the method (grep `\bname\b`). "Parity" means the test runs on both engines through `describeBoth` in `test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts:33`. That helper is gated by `SKIP_PG`, which is set when no `DATABASE_URL` is present (`:32`). `test/pglite-engine.test.ts` has no takes, ontology, chronicle, file, dream or contradiction tests. Its only coverage here is tags, timeline and raw-data basics.
