Summary of coverage: 43 methods in range. Eight have parity tests that run on both engines in `engine-parity.test.ts`: `addTag`, `getTags`, `addTimelineEntry`, `getTimeline`, `putRawData`, `getRawData` (soft-delete), `getDreamVerdict`, `putDreamVerdict`. `removeTag` and `addTimelineEntriesBatch` appear once in that file, but not in a dedicated parity assertion I could confirm. The other methods have no hit in `engine-parity.test.ts`; their parity coverage, where it exists, sits in the separate `*-parity.test.ts` files listed above. Chronicle ontology and takes have parity tests in separate files (`ontology-merge-parity.test.ts`, `chronicle-event-projection-parity.test.ts`, `takes-scorecard-parity.test.ts`). Several methods have Postgres-only tests (`takes-postgres.test.ts`, `eval-contradictions-postgres.test.ts`, `chronicle-last-seen-postgres.test.ts`), gated by `DATABASE_URL`.

### Export, segmented-body and persistence reads (19 methods)

Shared SQL in `src/core/engine-sql/export-reads.ts` (12 members), `persistence-state.ts` (5) and `transaction-settings.ts` (2). The statements are the text `gbrain export` and the import-path guards sent through `executeRaw` before; the file headers say a Rust store answers them from its own tables (`export-reads.ts:1-6`).

How each engine calls them. The `exportReadsImpl` functions take `this`, an engine (type `Pick<BrainEngine, 'executeRaw'>`), and call `tx.executeRaw`. On a transaction engine they therefore read inside that transaction. The `persistence-state.ts` and `transaction-settings.ts` functions take a `SqlExecutor` (a read brand `LegacyUnscopedRead` for reads, plain for writes). Both engines pass the same SQL; only the wrapper differs on Postgres.

| Postgres wrapper | What it does | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| plain `this` into `executeRaw` (`postgres-engine.ts:3251`) | Refuses an aborted request, counts a `raw` checkout, retries once in `BEGIN READ WRITE` after a read-only refusal. No read-deadline bound. | `listExportPageKeys`, `readExportPageRows`, `normalizeFenceLines`, `readSegmentedPageHead`, `readBodyManifestRows`, `readBodyManifestMeta`, `readBodyManifestOutline`, `listAccessPolicySourceIds` |
| `engineRead` (`postgres-engine.ts:1137`) | `throwIfRequestAborted()`, then `preparationRead` with an executor that refuses an aborted request before every statement (`abortGuarded`, `request-signal.ts:26`). | `readExportWithdrawalLedgerBytes`, `listExportWithdrawals`, `listForcedCutSeqs`, `readSegmentedVersionBodyBytes` |
| `preparationRead` (`postgres-engine.ts:464`) | Under an active read deadline, runs in a reserved read-only transaction whose `statement_timeout` is the time left (`runDeadlineRead`, `postgres-engine/bounded-statement.ts:93`); otherwise uses `autocommitSql`. A second argument `unbounded` skips the deadline. | `readManagedModeRows`, `getSourceStrictness`, `readPersistenceBrainId`, `readLocalWriterRow` (unbounded when `lock`) |
| `autocommitSql` (`postgres-engine.ts:364`) | Pool executor with the read-only retry (`retryReadOnlyAutocommit`, `postgres-engine/read-write-transactions.ts:65`). | `insertLocalWriterRow`, `applyTransactionSettings` |
| `engineSql` (`postgres-engine.ts:359`) | The current connection, which is the transaction handle on a transaction engine. No retry, no deadline. | `readTransactionSettings` |

PGLite has none of these wrappers. It passes `this` or `this.engineSql` straight through, and its `executeRaw` (`pglite-engine.ts:3147`) rejects an already-aborted signal with `AbortError` before the query (`:3158-3160`) and races the query against a later abort (`:3166-3172`). A later abort rejects the caller with `AbortError` but does not stop the WASM query. Rust classes are `raw_sql` for the twelve export reads (`rust-engine/members.ts:321-332`), `persistence_journal` for `readManagedModeRows`, `readPersistenceBrainId`, `readLocalWriterRow`, `insertLocalWriterRow` (`members.ts:297-300`), `sources` for `getSourceStrictness` (`members.ts:100`) and `transactions` for the two settings members (`members.ts:37-38`). A member rejects with `engine_capability_missing` before any work only when neither the shell (`implemented`) nor `STORE_MEMBERS` serves it (`rust-engine.ts:313-338`). For example `getSourceStrictness` is classed `sources` but is served by `rust-engine/store-members/sources.ts:24-25`.

| Method (iface line) | Impl PGLite / PG | What it does and SQL | PGLite vs PG; tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `readManagedModeRows(): {enabled}[]` (`engine.ts:2974`) | `pglite-engine.ts:1459` / `postgres-engine.ts:1116`, delegating to `persistence-state.ts:33` | `SELECT enabled FROM persistence_brain WHERE singleton=1` (`MANAGED_MODE_SQL`, `persistence-state.ts:18`). Returns every row as stored, typed `unknown`. A healthy brain has exactly one boolean row; the direct-write guard refuses anything else (`persistence/managed-mode.ts`). The exact text is memoized by `group-publish.ts`, so every plain reader sends it unchanged. | PG `preparationRead`, no `refuseAborted`. PGLite direct. Tests by grep (23 files), mainly `destructive-guard.test.ts`, `serve-sync-runner.serial.test.ts`, `persistence-mode-fail-closed-6482.test.ts`. |
| `listExportPageKeys(q: ExportPageKeyQuery): ExportPageKey[]` (`engine.ts:2977`) | `pglite-engine.ts:1460` / `postgres-engine.ts:1117`, delegating to `export-reads.ts:28` | One page of keys. `SELECT p.id::text, body_storage='segmented' AS segmented, left(p.slug,4097), p.source_id, left(p.source_path,4097), octet_length(p.source_path), (SELECT count(*) FROM raw_data ...) AS raw_count, <bytes> AS bytes FROM pages p WHERE p.id > $1::bigint AND p.deleted_at IS NULL AND ($2::text IS NULL OR p.source_id=$2) AND ($3::text IS NULL OR p.type=$3) AND ($4::text IS NULL OR starts_with(p.slug,$4)) ORDER BY p.id LIMIT 256` (`export-reads.ts:29-40`). The size is a segmented page's `body_bytes`, else `octet_length` of truth plus timeline, plus frontmatter, title, slug, source path, summed `raw_data` data and source, and summed tag lengths. Slug and path are cut at 4097 characters. The page size is a fixed 256 and the order is by id. | PG plain `executeRaw`. PGLite direct. ### Cycle locks (6 methods)

Shared SQL in `src/core/engine-sql/cycle-locks.ts`; the policy (TTL, grace, who holds) lives in `src/core/db-lock.ts`. Table `gbrain_cycle_locks` has one row per lock id (`schema-embedded.generated.ts:1521-1532`). Columns are `id TEXT PRIMARY KEY`, `holder_pid INT`, `holder_host TEXT`, `acquired_at`, `ttl_expires_at`, `last_refreshed_at` (nullable), and `acquisition_token UUID NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()` (`schema-embedded.generated.ts:1535-1536`). Index `idx_cycle_locks_ttl` is on `ttl_expires_at` (`schema-embedded.generated.ts:1533`).

Rules a Rust engine must keep.

- Every timestamp is the database's `NOW()`. Nothing reads the caller's clock (`cycle-locks.ts:17-19`).
- TTL. The default is 30 minutes (`db-lock.ts:73`). It is passed as the text `'<n> minutes'` and cast `::interval` (`cycle-locks.ts:78`).
- Steal grace. A lease is stolen only when `ttl_expires_at < NOW()` and `last_refreshed_at` is null or older than the grace (`cycle-locks.ts:36-38`). The grace comes from `resolveStealGraceSeconds` (`db-lock.ts:97-106`), which honors an environment override, else `max(floor(max(15, ttl*60/6) * 2), 60)` seconds, so 600 for a 30-minute TTL (`DEFAULT_STEAL_GRACE_SECONDS`, `db-lock.ts:95`). A holder that missed its refresh tick keeps the lease while it refreshed inside the grace.
- Fencing token. The caller supplies a UUID `acquisition_token`. Refresh, release and both deletes match it with the holder pid, so a holder whose lease was taken over never touches the new holder's row. The `fence` text from acquire is `extract(epoch from acquired_at)::text`, for diagnostics only and never compared (`cycle-locks.ts:81-82`).
- Rust class for all six is `minion_queue` (`rust-engine/members.ts:305-310`).

Transport. Postgres runs acquire, release, delete and delete-if-stale as `retryReadOnlyAutocommit(this.sql, c => ... engineSqlOn(c))` on the normal pool (`postgres-engine.ts:3328-3337`). It runs refresh through `directEngineSql` (`postgres-engine.ts:3325-3327`), an executor whose `query` is `executeRawDirect`, so the renewal uses the direct session pool when the dual pool is active and does not queue behind pooler exhaustion. `readCycleLocks` runs on `engineSql` with the unscoped brand and no retry wrapper. After a successful acquire, Postgres calls `noteDirectPoolLease()`, which moves a `one_shot` process profile to `short_lived` (`direct-pool-profile.ts:56-58`). PGLite runs all six on `cycleLockSql = pgliteExecutor(this.db)` (`pglite-engine.ts:3196`), the attached database handle, with no checkpoint admission, so a heartbeat never queues behind a WAL checkpoint. Signal handling. Only `refreshCycleLock` takes a signal. On Postgres, `executeRawDirect` checks it before waiting for the pool and cancels the statement on abort. PGLite's `refreshCycleLock` has no signal parameter and never forwards one (comment `pglite-engine.ts:3198`).

| Method (iface line) | Impl PGLite / PG | What it does and SQL | PGLite vs PG; tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `acquireCycleLock(lockId, opts: {holderPid, holderHost, acquisitionToken, ttlMinutes, stealGraceSeconds}): {id, fence} \| null` (`engine.ts:3109`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3197` / `postgres-engine.ts:3328`, delegating to `cycle-locks.ts:70` | `INSERT INTO gbrain_cycle_locks (id, holder_pid, holder_host, acquired_at, ttl_expires_at, last_refreshed_at, acquisition_token) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, NOW(), NOW() + $4::interval, NOW(), $6::uuid) ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE SET holder_pid, holder_host, acquired_at = NOW(), acquisition_token, ttl_expires_at = NOW() + $4::interval, last_refreshed_at = NOW() WHERE ttl_expires_at < NOW() AND (last_refreshed_at IS NULL OR last_refreshed_at < NOW() - $5 * INTERVAL '1 second') RETURNING id, extract(epoch from acquired_at)::text AS fence` (`cycle-locks.ts:26-39`). A free id inserts. An expired id whose holder is past the grace is overwritten. Otherwise no row returns and the method returns `null`. | PG on the pool under `retryReadOnlyAutocommit`, plus `noteDirectPoolLease()` on success. PGLite on `cycleLockSql`. Tests by grep (16 files), mainly `engine-parity/cycle-locks.test.ts` (25 mentions), `db-lock-capability-refusal.test.ts`. |
| `refreshCycleLock(lockId, opts: {holderPid, acquisitionToken, ttlMinutes}, queryOpts?: {signal?}): boolean` (`engine.ts:3123`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3199` / `postgres-engine.ts:3333`, delegating to `cycle-locks.ts:85` | `UPDATE gbrain_cycle_locks SET ttl_expires_at = NOW() + $1::interval, last_refreshed_at = NOW() WHERE id = $2 AND holder_pid = $3 AND acquisition_token = $4::uuid RETURNING id` (`cycle-locks.ts:41-45`). True when a row came back. False means the lease is gone or taken over, and the holder lost it. | PG: direct session pool through `directEngineSql`, signal cancels the statement. PGLite: `cycleLockSql`, no signal. Tests by grep (16 files), mainly `engine-parity/cycle-locks.test.ts`, `cycle-drain-renewal.test.ts`. |
| `releaseCycleLock(lockId, opts: {holderPid, acquisitionToken}): boolean` (`engine.ts:3130`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3200` / `postgres-engine.ts:3334`, delegating to `cycle-locks.ts:96` | `DELETE FROM gbrain_cycle_locks WHERE id = $1 AND holder_pid = $2 AND acquisition_token = $3::uuid` (`cycle-locks.ts:47`). True when `affectedRows > 0`, so the engine's affected-row count must be exact. No `RETURNING`. | PG pool with read-only retry. PGLite `cycleLockSql`. Tests by grep (14 files), mainly `engine-parity/cycle-locks.test.ts`, `supervisor-configuration-blocked.test.ts`. |
| `readCycleLocks(filter: CycleLockFilter): CycleLockRow[]` (`engine.ts:3133`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3201` / `postgres-engine.ts:3335`, delegating to `cycle-locks.ts:104` | Four shapes by `filter.by`. `id` is `WHERE id = $1`. `ids` is `WHERE id = ANY($1::text[])`. `stale` is `WHERE ttl_expires_at < NOW() ORDER BY acquired_at`. `all` is `ORDER BY acquired_at` with no filter (`cycle-locks.ts:65-68`). Each selects `id, holder_pid, holder_host, acquired_at, ttl_expires_at, last_refreshed_at, acquisition_token, now() AS db_now`; `db_now` is the database clock the caller's derived ages compare against. A pure read, no acquire. An unknown filter throws. | Same SQL. Unscoped read on both, no retry wrapper. Tests by grep (15 files), mainly `engine-parity/cycle-locks.test.ts` (18 mentions), `db-lock-capability-refusal.test.ts`. |
| `deleteCycleLock(lockId, opts: {holderPid, acquisitionToken}): boolean` (`engine.ts:3139`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3202` / `postgres-engine.ts:3336`, delegating to `cycle-locks.ts:119` | `DELETE FROM gbrain_cycle_locks WHERE id = $1 AND holder_pid = $2 AND acquisition_token = $3::uuid RETURNING id` (`cycle-locks.ts:49-51`). A row with a null `last_refreshed_at` is never deleted. Returns `{deleted: false, lastRefreshedAt: null}` for no row, else the deleted row's `last_refreshed_at` as a `Date` so the caller can report the stale age (`cycle-locks.ts:135-137`). ### Queue health reads (8 methods)

Shared SQL in `src/core/engine-sql/jobs-health.ts`. These are the reads behind `gbrain doctor`'s queue checks, the advisor's stalled-jobs collector and the submit-time queue probe (header `jobs-health.ts:1-4`). Every statement reads the one table `minion_jobs` and only reads it. Each function takes the engine (or a transaction) and calls its `executeRaw`; Rust class `minion_queue` for all eight (`rust-engine/members.ts:340-347`).

Rules a Rust engine must keep.

- The SQL text fixes the windows at 24 hours and 1 hour. The `windowHours` and `stalledAfterHours` spec fields (and the divergence spec's `windowHours`) are for the store arm, and every caller passes 24 and 1 (header `jobs-health.ts:7-10`). Only `waitingThreshold`, `oldWaitingHours` and `ttlReasonPrefix` reach the SQL.
- Counts are returned as numbers. Rows are re-sorted in TS bytewise (`byteCmp`, `jobs-health.ts:23`), which is the order a Rust store sorts strings in, so ties the planner left open are fixed. The sort keys are listed per row.
- Lock liveness is `lock_until > now()` for healthy and `lock_until < now()` for stalled.

Signal handling. Postgres wraps each member in `engineRead` (`postgres-engine.ts:1137`), so an aborted request is refused before the call and before every statement, and a read deadline ends the read on the server. `readWedgeSignals` and `readOldestWaitingAge` also take `opts.signal`, which reaches `runUnsafe`; on abort Postgres sends a cancel to the server and the statement stops. PGLite passes `this` to the shared function. Its `executeRaw` checks the signal before running, and a later abort rejects the caller while the query keeps running in WASM (`pglite-engine.ts:3147-3174`). The other six members take no signal argument.

| Method (iface line) | Impl PGLite / PG | What it does and SQL | PGLite vs PG; tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `readQueueHealthSignals(spec: QueueHealthSpec): QueueHealthSignals` (`engine.ts:3153`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3205` / `postgres-engine.ts:3339`, delegating to `jobs-health.ts:53` | Five statements, all on `minion_jobs`, for the `queue_health` check. (1) Stalled-forever. `SELECT id, name, started_at::text, count(*) OVER ()::int AS total WHERE status = 'active' AND started_at IS NOT NULL AND started_at < now() - interval '1 hour' ORDER BY started_at ASC LIMIT 5` (`jobs-health.ts:55-63`). `total` counts every stalled job, not only the five. (2) Depth. `SELECT name, queue, count(*)::int AS depth WHERE status = 'waiting' GROUP BY name, queue HAVING count(*) > $1 ORDER BY depth DESC LIMIT 5`, `$1` = `waitingThreshold` (`jobs-health.ts:64-72`). (3) Watchdog kills. `count(*) WHERE status IN ('dead','failed') AND finished_at > now() - interval '24 hours' AND error_text = 'aborted: watchdog'` (`jobs-health.ts:73-79`). (4) Prompt too long. Jobs with `name = 'subagent'`, `status = 'dead'`, finished in 24 hours and `error_text LIKE 'prompt_too_long:%'`, grouped by a CASE on `COALESCE(idempotency_key, data->>'__released_idempotency_key')`, `dream:patterns:%` to `patterns`, `dream:synth%` to `synthesize`, else `other` (`jobs-health.ts:80-92`). (5) Old embed-backfill. `name = 'embed-backfill' AND status = 'waiting'` grouped by name and queue, `HAVING min(created_at) < now() - ($1::text::interval)` with `$1` = `'<oldWaitingHours> hours'`, `ORDER BY oldest_age_seconds DESC` (`jobs-health.ts:93-104`). TS sort keys. depth by count desc then name then queue; prompt_too_long by phase; old embed-backfill by age desc then queue (`jobs-health.ts:106-112`). | PG `engineRead`, PGLite direct. Same SQL. Tests by grep (14 files), mainly `engine-parity/jobs-health.test.ts`, `n3-typed-read-guards.test.ts`, `helpers/queue-engine-members.ts`. |

Sorted in TS by depth desc then queue bytewise. `oldest_age_seconds` may be null. The comment says the filter hits only `status`, so the statement scans the table (`jobs-health.ts:123-126`), fine at doctor frequency. | PG `engineRead`. Tests by grep (14 files), mainly `engine-parity/jobs-health.test.ts`, `n3-typed-read-guards.test.ts`. |

(1) One row per job name that has a waiting job. A subselect of waiting counts joined left to intake (`created_at > now() - interval '24 hours'`) and completions (`finished_at > now() - interval '24 hours' AND status = 'completed'`), each defaulting to `'0'` (`jobs-health.ts:157-167`). ### Trust tier and withdrawal reads (7 methods)

Shared SQL lives in `src/core/engine-sql/trust-tier-reads.ts`, `withdrawal-discovery.ts`, `withdrawal-pages.ts` and `withdrawal-fence.ts`. Both engines pass `this` to the shared function, so every statement runs through that engine's own `executeRaw`. PGLite (`pglite-engine.ts:3147`) pre-checks an explicit `opts.signal` and then queries. Postgres (`postgres-engine.ts:3251`) calls `throwIfRequestAborted()`, counts a `'raw'` checkout on the pool gauge, and runs `retryReadOnlyAutocommit` (`read-write-transactions.ts`), which reopens the statement in `BEGIN READ WRITE` when the session default refuses a write with SQLSTATE 25006. No member here uses the unscoped read brand, `preparationRead` or a statement timeout.

| Method (iface line) | Impl PGLite / PG | What it does and SQL | PGLite vs PG; tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `listVersionTrustTiers(ids): Array<{id, trust_tier}>` (`engine.ts:3171`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3214` / `postgres-engine.ts:3348` delegating to shared `trust-tier-reads.ts:22-26` | Reads the stored trust tier of each listed page version. `SELECT id, trust_tier FROM page_versions WHERE id = ANY($1::bigint[])`, ids bound as one array. There is no `ORDER BY`. The code turns ids into numbers, turns a missing tier into null, and sorts by id in JavaScript. An empty id list still sends the statement. A Rust engine must return rows sorted by numeric id. | Same SQL on both engines. files 12 (engine-parity/trust-tier-reads.test.ts, n3-sql-move-parity.test.ts, swept-member-request-abort.test.ts, corrective-containment.test.ts). |
| `listVersionReasonWriters(ids): Array<{id, type, tier}>` (`engine.ts:3173`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3215` / `postgres-engine.ts:3349` delegating to shared `trust-tier-reads.ts:32-39` | For each listed version, reports the type and tier of the write that replaced it. `SELECT pv.id, COALESCE(nx.type, p.type) AS type, CASE WHEN nx.id IS NULL THEN p.trust_tier ELSE nx.trust_tier END AS tier FROM page_versions pv JOIN pages p ON p.id = pv.page_id LEFT JOIN LATERAL (next snapshot of the same page, `n.id > pv.id ORDER BY n.id LIMIT 1`) nx ON true WHERE pv.id = ANY($1::bigint[])`. With no later snapshot it falls back to the live page. Rows are sorted by numeric id in JavaScript, not in SQL. The join to `pages` has no deleted filter. | Same SQL on both engines. files 12 (engine-parity/trust-tier-reads.test.ts, n3-sql-move-parity.test.ts, swept-member-request-abort.test.ts). |

It returns visibility, fact_hash, `COALESCE(subject,'*')` and `norm = gbrain_fact_normalize(COALESCE(claim, <fact text of the first `facts` row of the source with the same visibility whose `gbrain_fact_fingerprint` equals the hash>, <same with `gbrain_fact_fingerprint_v1`>))`. An empty claim list returns false with no SQL. Otherwise a `WITH incoming AS MATERIALIZED` CTE fingerprints each claim once with `gbrain_fact_fingerprint` and `gbrain_fact_fingerprint_v1` over `jsonb_to_recordset($2::text::jsonb)`. It joins them to `fact_withdrawals` UNION ALL `fact_purges` for `source_id = $1`, on `fact_hash IN (fp, fp_v1)`, claim visibility null or equal, and `($3::text IS NULL OR w.subject = '*' OR w.subject = $3)`, with `LIMIT 1`. The CTE is materialized because the planner re-evaluates fingerprint functions per row pair otherwise (the comment records 4 s for 28 claims against 400 withdrawals). A Rust brain refuses it with `engine_capability_missing`. | Same SQL on both engines. files 9 (withdrawal-fence-sql-pin.test.ts, engine-surface-coverage.test.ts). |

### Code traversal cache (3 methods)

Shared SQL lives in `src/core/engine-sql/code-traversal-cache.ts`. The table is `code_traversal_cache` (columns `id`, `symbol_qualified`, `depth`, `source_id`, `response_json JSONB`, `max_chunk_updated_at`, `xmin_max`, `cluster_generation BIGINT`, `computed_at`). The unique key is `(symbol_qualified, depth, source_id)`, the index `code_traversal_cache_key_idx` (`schema-migrations/v059-code-traversal-cache-v0-34.ts`). `cluster_generation` is the freshness check, because a row at an older generation simply misses on read. Both engines pass `this`, so the statements run through `executeRaw` as described above. A bind the database rejects (a fractional depth, a NUL character, no response) throws, and the cache module in `src/core/code-intel/traversal-cache.ts` decides what to swallow.

| Method (iface line) | Impl PGLite / PG | What it does and SQL | PGLite vs PG; tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `getCodeTraversalCache(key: CodeTraversalCacheKey): CodeTraversalCacheRow \| null` (`engine.ts:3237`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3269` / `postgres-engine.ts:3396` delegating to shared `code-traversal-cache.ts:36-53` | Reads the memoised traversal for `code_blast` and `code_flow`. `SELECT response_json, computed_at, cluster_generation FROM code_traversal_cache WHERE symbol_qualified = $1 AND depth = $2 AND source_id = $3 AND cluster_generation = $4 LIMIT 1`. A row at a different generation counts as a miss and returns null. `cluster_generation` is run through `Number()` because Postgres returns a BigInt for that column and PGLite a number. `computed_at` comes back as the driver's timestamp value. | Same SQL on both engines. files 12 (engine-parity/traversal-cache.test.ts, helpers/postgres-engine-sql-cases.ts, engine-surface-coverage.test.ts). |
| `putCodeTraversalCache(key, response, maxChunkUpdatedAt, xminMax): void` (`engine.ts:3239`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3270` / `postgres-engine.ts:3397` delegating to shared `code-traversal-cache.ts:56-81` | Upserts one row. `INSERT INTO code_traversal_cache (symbol_qualified, depth, source_id, response_json, max_chunk_updated_at, xmin_max, cluster_generation) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4::text::jsonb, $5::timestamptz, $6, $7) ON CONFLICT (symbol_qualified, depth, source_id) DO UPDATE SET response_json, max_chunk_updated_at, xmin_max, cluster_generation = EXCLUDED.*, computed_at = NOW()`. The response is bound as `JSON.stringify` text and cast to JSONB server-side. The conflict update takes the new generation, so one row per key survives. | Same SQL on both engines. files 12 (engine-parity/traversal-cache.test.ts, helpers/postgres-engine-sql-cases.ts). |
| `clearCodeTraversalCache(opts: {sourceId?, allSources?}): number` (`engine.ts:3241`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3271` / `postgres-engine.ts:3398` delegating to shared `code-traversal-cache.ts:84-101` | Deletes cached traversals and returns how many went. With `allSources`, `WITH deleted AS (DELETE FROM code_traversal_cache RETURNING 1) SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM deleted`. Otherwise the same with `WHERE source_id = $1`. The count is parsed from text. With neither option set, the code binds `opts.sourceId!`, which is undefined. What the driver does with that bind was not checked (unverified). | Same SQL on both engines. files 12 (engine-parity/traversal-cache.test.ts, helpers/postgres-engine-sql-cases.ts). |

### Open loops (4 methods)

Shared SQL lives in `src/core/engine-sql/open-loops.ts`, table `open_loops` (`src/schema.sql:1079`). The unique key is `open_loops_dedup (source_id, dedup_key)`. `status` is `open`, `done`, `dropped` or `stale`, and `loop_type` is one of five checked values. The members return raw rows, and `src/core/loops/loops-store.ts` normalizes them. On Postgres every member first calls `whenNotAborted` (`postgres-engine.ts:257`), which throws the request's `AbortError` if its signal already fired. The two writes then run on `refuseAborted(this.autocommitSql)`. `refuseAborted` (`engine-sql/abort-aware.ts:10`) repeats that check before each statement, and `autocommitSql` (`postgres-engine.ts:364`) retries a read-only-session refusal in `BEGIN READ WRITE`. The two reads run on `refuseAborted(unscopedExecutor(this.engineSql, ...))`, so they are marked as reads with no source scope and get no retry. PGLite runs the same functions on `this.engineSql` (the reads wrapped in `unscopedExecutor`) and has no request-abort check in these members.

| Method (iface line) | Impl PGLite / PG | What it does and SQL | PGLite vs PG; tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `upsertOpenLoop(loop: OpenLoopUpsertInput): {id, created, applied}` (`engine.ts:3338`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3353` / `postgres-engine.ts:3483` delegating to shared `open-loops.ts:22-92` | Inserts a loop, or refreshes the row with the same source and dedup key. `INSERT INTO open_loops (...16 columns) VALUES (...) ON CONFLICT (source_id, dedup_key) DO UPDATE SET ... WHERE <guard> RETURNING id, (xmax = 0) AS created`. Evidence is bound as `$7::text::jsonb`, `confidence` defaults to 1.0, and `last_activity_at` and `opened_at` default to `now()`. On conflict the row is forced to `status = 'open'` and clears `closed_at` and `closed_by`. `opened_at` keeps the older value for an open row. `last_activity_at` takes `GREATEST`. Counterparty, page, due date and fact id keep the old value when the new one is null. The counterparty source is bound only when a counterparty slug is given. The guard is the manual-close protection. An update lands only when `status = 'open'`, or the new activity is newer, or `closed_by = 'mute_recheck'`, and also not when `closed_by = 'muted'` unless the counterparty email changed. When the guard blocks the update, no row returns. The function then runs `SELECT id FROM open_loops WHERE source_id = $1 AND dedup_key = $2` and returns `{id (0 if none), created: false, applied: false}`. A Rust engine must keep the guard exactly and the two-step answer. | Same SQL on both engines. Two statements with no wrapping transaction on either engine. eng-parity (`engine-parity/open-loops.test.ts` runs PGLite always, Postgres with `DATABASE_URL`, and a Rust arm behind `GBRAIN_TEST_RUST_ARM=1`); files 29 (engine-parity/open-loops.test.ts, loops-store.test.ts, loop-members-request-abort.test.ts, st-write-move-parity.test.ts). |
| `closeOpenLoop(sourceId \| null, id, status, closedBy): row \| null` (`engine.ts:3340`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3354` / `postgres-engine.ts:3484` delegating to shared `open-loops.ts:94-109` | Closes one open loop. `UPDATE open_loops SET status = $1, closed_at = now(), closed_by = $2, updated_at = now() WHERE id = $3 AND status = 'open' AND ($4::text IS NULL OR source_id = $4) RETURNING *`. Returns the closed row, or null when no open loop matches (wrong id, wrong source, or already closed). A null `sourceId` closes in any source. The status argument excludes `open`. | Same SQL on both engines. files 21 (engine-parity/open-loops.test.ts, st-write-move-parity.test.ts, loops-store.test.ts, loop-members-request-abort.test.ts). |
| `listOpenLoops(opts?: OpenLoopListOpts): row[]` (`engine.ts:3342`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3355` / `postgres-engine.ts:3485` delegating to shared `open-loops.ts:137-161` | Lists loops that pass every filter. `SELECT * FROM open_loops WHERE ($1::text IS NULL OR source_id = ANY(string_to_array($1, ','))) AND ($2 IS NULL OR status = $2) AND ($3 IS NULL OR loop_type = $3) AND ($4 IS NULL OR (counterparty_slug = $4 AND ($6 IS NULL OR COALESCE(counterparty_source_id, source_id) = $6)) OR counterparty_email = $4) AND ($5::bigint IS NULL OR id = $5::bigint) ORDER BY last_activity_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT $7`. Source ids are joined into one comma string. The limit is `round(min(max(limit ?? 200, 1), 1000))`, so the default is 200 and the cap is 1000. | Same SQL on both engines. Neither engine applies a source scope here (unscoped read brand). files 21 (loops-store.test.ts, loop-apply.test.ts, ns-read-move-parity.test.ts, engine-parity/open-loops.test.ts). |
| `readEntityOpenLoops(slug, sourceId, limit): EntityOpenLoopRow[]` (`engine.ts:3344`) | `pglite-engine.ts:3356` / `postgres-engine.ts:3486` delegating to shared `open-loops.ts:127-135` | Reads the open loops shown on an entity card. `SELECT id, loop_type, summary, due_at, last_activity_at, fact_id FROM open_loops WHERE status = 'open' AND counterparty_slug = ${slug} AND COALESCE(counterparty_source_id, source_id) = ${sourceId} ORDER BY last_activity_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT <limit>`. A row with no counterparty source counts as its own source's. The limit is checked before any SQL. It must be a safe integer of at least 1, or the function throws `readEntityOpenLoops: limit must be a positive integer`. It is spliced as a trusted literal, not bound. The `id DESC` tie-break is part of the contract. An email equal to the slug does not match. | Same SQL on both engines. files 11 (engine-parity/open-loops.test.ts, ns-read-move-parity.test.ts, entity-card-loops.test.ts). |

### Decide proposals (16 methods)

Shared SQL lives in `src/core/engine-sql/decide-proposals.ts`. It covers four tables.

- `decide_proposals`, the supersede proposals from the conflict sweep. Primary key `id`, unique `(sweep_id, pair_index)`, and a partial unique index `decide_proposals_claim_pair_uidx` on `(source_id, LEAST(new_fact_id, old_fact_id), GREATEST(new_fact_id, old_fact_id)) WHERE sweep_id LIKE 'claim_conflicts:%'` (`src/schema.sql:3208`, `3249`).
- `decide_review_proposals`, the review lane's proposals. Unique `(kind, source_id, a_ref, b_ref, subject)` (`src/schema.sql:3265`).
- `decide_review_queue`, the review lane's work queue. Primary key `(kind, source_id, a_ref, b_ref)` (`src/schema.sql:3253`).
- `decide_sweep_deferred`, the conflict sweep's retry table. Primary key `(slot, source_id, fact_id)` (`src/schema.sql:3228`).

Both engines pass `this`, so statements run through `executeRaw` as described under the first subsection. There is no unscoped read brand, no `preparationRead` and no deadline on Postgres for these members. Reads of `facts`, `pages`, `fact_withdrawals` and `decision_receipts` for fact selection stay in `proposals-store.ts` and `review-lane.ts`, not in this file. Status rules. Supersede proposals use `ProposalStatus` = `pending`, `accepted`, `rejected`, `stale`, `undone` (`decide-proposals.ts:21`). The table has no CHECK on status, and review proposals take any text status. The rate SQL also counts `accepted_no_action` (`decide-proposals.ts:216-228`). Every transition is compare-and-set, an `UPDATE ... WHERE id = $1 AND status = $from RETURNING id` that answers true only when exactly one row moved. A caller that lost the race gets false and nothing changes. A transition stamps `decided_at = now()`. The `lock` flag on the two `get` members only switches the statement text to `... FOR UPDATE`. It is the same text on PGLite and Postgres. The lock holds only inside a transaction, because both engines run the statement as a single autocommit statement otherwise. The interface comment says a store without row locks reads the row plain.

### Decide store (20 methods)

Shared SQL lives in `src/core/engine-sql/decide-store.ts`. The interface lists 20 members from `readDecideState` to `setDecideCalibrationRetired` (`engine.ts:3382-3420`), and every one is in the table. The tables are listed next.

- `decide_state` (`key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, value, updated_at`, `src/schema.sql:3153`).
- `decide_spend`, the third-party spend ledger (`request_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY`, an index on `created_at`, `src/schema.sql:3139`).
- `decision_receipts`, one row per decision (`id BIGSERIAL`, indexes on `(slot, created_at)`, `(model_resolved, slot)` and `decision_id`, `src/schema.sql:3103`).
- `decide_calibrations` (`id BIGSERIAL`, index on `(slot, provider, model_resolved, created_at DESC)`, `src/schema.sql:3174`).

Time windows are `now() - ($1::int * interval '1 hour')` (receipts) or `'1 day'` (pruning), with the window bound as an integer cast. The 60 second daily-spend cache, the buffered writers and the salt cache stay in `src/core/ai/decide/store.ts`. On Postgres, the reads `readDecideState`, `dailyDecideSpend`, `listDecideReceiptStats`, `decideSlotUsage`, `listDecideResolvedModels`, `listDecideMixedModelDecisions` and `listDecideCalibrations` go through `decideRead` (`postgres-engine.ts:1136`). `decideRead` calls `throwIfRequestAborted()` and then `preparationRead` (`postgres-engine.ts:464`) on an unscoped executor. `preparationRead` runs the read on the autocommit executor when no read deadline is active. Under a doctor read deadline it runs it in a reserved read-only transaction whose `statement_timeout` is the time left (`runDeadlineRead`, `postgres-engine/bounded-statement.ts:93`). The other 13 members pass `this` and run through `executeRaw` with no deadline. PGLite passes `this` for all 20, so it has no deadline or request-abort check here.

| Method (iface line) | Impl PGLite / PG | What it does and SQL | PGLite vs PG; tests |
|---|---|---|---|

Null when absent. | Same SQL on both engines. PG runs it through `decideRead` (bounded by a read deadline). files 12 (engine-parity/decide-store.test.ts, decide-read-request-abort.test.ts, doctor-typed-reads-deadline.test.ts). |
| `upsertDecideState(key, value): void` (`engine.ts:3384`) | `pglite-engine.ts:1480` / `postgres-engine.ts:1139` delegating to shared `decide-store.ts:23-29` | `INSERT INTO decide_state (key, value, updated_at) VALUES ($1, $2, now()) ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE SET value = EXCLUDED.value, updated_at = now()`. | Same SQL on both engines. files 10 (engine-parity/decide-store.test.ts, shadow-follow-producer.test.ts). |

Every column is bound as one text array and cast on the server, because the Postgres driver cannot serialize typed boolean or number arrays for that cast. A missing column becomes null, except the booleans, which become false. A null in a NOT NULL column (`request_id`, `slot`, `provider`, `lane`, `outcome`, and the two numbers, since they are always bound) makes the single statement fail, so no row of the batch is stored. ### Parity risks noted

1. Clock sources differ. Reads and sweeps compare against the DB clock (`now()`, `current_date`), e.g. `getDreamVerdict`, `sweepDreamVerdicts`, `getContradictionCacheEntry`, `sweepContradictionCache`, `getOnThisDay`, `getLastSeen`, `getOntology` default `asof`. Some writes and computations use the JS clock: `putContradictionCacheEntry` expiry (`takes.ts:262-263`), `loadContradictionsTrend` cutoff (`takes.ts:195`), and `finalizeLastSeen`'s `days_ago` (`last-seen.ts:11-21`). Clock skew between app and DB changes results. A Rust rewrite has to choose one clock and keep both sides on it.
2. Transaction-local trust GUC. `ONTOLOGY_SUPERSEDE_GUARD` (`chronicle/ontology.ts:65`) reads `current_setting('gbrain.write_trust_tier', true)` in the same transaction. Ontology supersession depends on that session setting, not on a parameter. An embedded store needs an equivalent per-transaction writer tier.
3. Postgres-specific SQL features with no direct equivalent: `DISTINCT ON`, `json_agg`/`json_build_object`, `jsonb_to_recordset` (batch writes), `jsonb_array_elements_text`, `unnest(int[])`, `ON CONFLICT ... WHERE <partial index>` (`timeline_event_dedup`, facts dedup), `RETURNING (xmax = 0) AS created` (upsertFile created flag), `md5()` as a dedup key (timeline `md5(summary)`), `FOR KEY SHARE` (addTimelineEntry), `LATERAL ... OFFSET 0` planner fence (getTimeline), `make_interval`, `date_trunc('week')`, `EXTRACT(MONTH/DAY)`, `pg_trgm` `<%` and `word_similarity`, `pgvector` `<=>` and `vector_dims`.
4. Calibration bucketing relies on NUMERIC arithmetic (`takes.ts:661-665`). Float math would put boundary weights in a different bucket. The Postgres driver also needs explicit `::float` casts on bound scalars (`takes.ts:644-648`), so scalars are text-bound there. Rust must do exact decimal bucketing.
5. `files` identity mismatch. The DDL is `UNIQUE(storage_path)` (`schema.sql:1149`), while `getFile` and the file comment at `engine-sql/files.ts:41` describe identity as `(source_id, storage_path)`. The `ON CONFLICT (storage_path) DO UPDATE` never updates `source_id`. A second source writing the same storage_path keeps the first source's row, and `getFile(secondSource, path)` returns null.
6. `supersedeTake` allocates `row_num` as `MAX(row_num)+1` without a row lock (`takes.ts:554`). Two concurrent supersedes on one page can compute the same next row number. A Rust rewrite should allocate with a lock or sequence.
7. `addTakesBatch` ON CONFLICT is DO UPDATE (`takes.ts:62-63`). An intra-batch duplicate `(page_id,row_num)` fails the whole statement, and the claim-change reset of embeddings (`takes.ts:81-84`) must be reproduced exactly.
8. `updateTakeEmbeddings` silently drops a vector when the claim changed in flight (`WHERE t.claim = v.claim`, `takes.ts:502`). It returns the updated count only, so the caller cannot tell a dropped vector from a successful one.
9. `listStaleTakes` has a hard `LIMIT 100000` (`takes.ts:453`). Excess rows are dropped with no signal.
10. `loadContradictionsTrend` has no LIMIT (`takes.ts:196-205`). Result size is unbounded.
11. `searchTakesVector` has no dimension check in SQL (`takes.ts:385-401`). Mixed-width vectors fail in pgvector. The Rust version must decide what to do with mixed widths.
12. `getLastSeen` matches wikilinks with `LIKE ... ESCAPE '\\'` on `who` strings (`timeline.ts:243-245`). The escape rule has to be reproduced exactly, or wikilink matches will differ.
13. Reads on master are `LegacyUnscopedRead` (`engine-sql/brands.ts:33`): no RLS scope transaction. Any Rust rewrite that adds scope enforcement will change behavior in this range. The brands doc calls widening a TODO, not a drive-by (`brands.ts:17-21`).
14. Dialect cancellation differs. PGLite cannot cancel a running statement; `pgliteQuery` races a late abort (`dialect-pglite.ts:42-56`). Postgres uses real cancellation. Batch-write abort behavior is not identical.
15. Lock capability differs. `transactionAdvisoryLocks` is `false` on PGLite (`dialect-pglite.ts:27`) and `true` on Postgres (`dialect-postgres.ts:55`). `mutatePageTag` calls `lockPageKeys` on both. I did not trace the lock mechanism itself.
16. Type-only drift: PGLite's `searchTakes`/`searchTakesVector` opts type (`pglite-engine.ts:2648-2658`) omits `sourceId`/`sourceIds`. Runtime behavior is unchanged.
17. `resolveTake` and `updateTake` have no transaction and do a SELECT then an UPDATE (`takes.ts:571-576`). Concurrent resolves can race the immutability check. The engine's error code is the only guard.
18. `putRawData` without `sourceId` matches every same-slug page (`postgres-engine.ts:2101`, `pglite-engine.ts:2284`). Multi-source brains can get a 21000 error. The sourceId path avoids this.


---

## Facts, Segmented storage, Versions, Stats, Sync, Config and Migration support

Scope: `BrainEngine` interface members declared in `src/core/engine.ts` lines 2451-3421 at `116d737413` (164 methods, confirmed by listing every member in that range). Rows for 70 of these methods keep `engine.ts` lines at `8bbfd0b5e8`; rows for the other 94 cite `116d737413`. Interface line numbers below are `engine.ts:N`. Implementations: `PGLiteEngine` = `src/core/pglite-engine.ts`, `PostgresEngine` = `src/core/postgres-engine.ts`, shared SQL = `src/core/engine-sql/*.ts`. "Shared" means one SQL implementation runs on both engines through `SqlExecutor`. "Inline" means each engine writes its own SQL.

Groups in this part: Facts (14), Segmented page storage (12), Derivation watermarks (3), Versions (3), Stats and health (2), Ingest log (2), Sync (14), Config (6), Migration and raw SQL (5), Code edges (6), Eval capture (5), Salience and anomalies (5), Export, segmented-body and persistence reads (19), Belief ledger, search-vector inventory and folder access (4), Cycle locks (6), Queue health reads (8), Trust tier and withdrawal reads (7), Code traversal cache (3), Open loops (4), Decide proposals (16), Decide store (20). Total 164.

### Engine plumbing used by this range

| Item | PGLite | Postgres | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Shared executor | `engineSql` getter: `pgliteExecutor(db or guardedHandle(db))`, `pglite-engine.ts:794-795` | `engineSql` / `engineSqlOn(conn)`, `postgres-engine.ts:317-322` | Executor built per access so transaction clones run on their own handle. |
| Dialect capabilities | `PGLITE_CAPABILITIES` = `maxBindParamsPerStatement 30000`, `transactionAdvisoryLocks false`, `probesEmbeddingCast false`, `engine-sql/dialect-pglite.ts:25-29` | `maxBindParamsPerStatement 65533` (`dialect-postgres.ts:54`), `transactionAdvisoryLocks true`, `probesEmbeddingCast true`, `engine-sql/dialect-postgres.ts:54-56` | These three flags drive most PGLite/PG branches in the shared SQL below. |
| Unscoped read brand | `unscopedExecutor(engineSql, label)` wraps reads; `LegacyUnscopedRead` type, `engine-sql/brands.ts` | same | Every read in this range is on master with no source scope (EO4 inventory). |
| Transactions | `transaction()` = `db.transaction` with composable handle, savepoint on nesting, checkpoint guard `runOutermost`, `pglite-engine.ts:1209-1223`. Single connection, no advisory locks. | `transactionOn()`, `postgres-engine.ts:726-760`: `BEGIN READ WRITE` unless `readOnly` (`postgres-engine/read-write-transactions.ts`), `checkoutGauge 'tx'`, signal abort via `transaction-abort.ts`, commit checks, savepoints on nested clones. | Interface declaration at `engine.ts:849-851` (outside this range; owned by the lifecycle part). |
| Statement timeout | None. PGLite runs `read` on itself and ignores `timeoutMs` | Per-statement `statement_timeout` (`keyword-statement-timeout.ts`, default 8000 ms, range 1000-60000) | See `keywordBoundedRead`. |
| Pool gauge / reserved connections | None (single in-process connection) | `checkoutGauge` acquire/release for `raw`, `direct`, `tx` checkouts (`executeRaw` 2998-3008, `executeRawDirect` 3053-3058, `transactionOn`), real driver queue numbers in `postgres-engine/pool-stats.ts:1-11`; `reserveWithCancellation` (`postgres-engine/cancellation.ts:17`) reserves a dedicated connection for each cancellable statement | Pool diagnostics and reserved-connection behavior exist only on Postgres. |
| WAL checkpoint guard | `PgliteCheckpointGuard`, `pglite-engine/checkpoint-guard.ts:1-25`; `writesWal(sql)` regex decides autocommit writes | n/a | Only in PGLite. |
| Config snapshot | `requestConfigValue` / `requestConfigRows`, `config-snapshot.ts:161-172`, used by `getConfig` / `getAllConfig` on both engines | same | Request-scoped snapshot first, then the engine's own read. |

### Facts / hot memory (14 methods)

Shared SQL: `src/core/engine-sql/facts.ts`. Both engines delegate; PGLite passes `undefined` for the embedding-cast resolver, Postgres passes `() => this.resolveFactsEmbeddingCast()` (`postgres-engine.ts:2271-2304`, which probes `pg_attribute` for `facts.embedding` and returns `::halfvec` or `::vector`, cached on the root engine).

Test-count convention: "eng-parity" = `test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts` (Postgres arm needs `DATABASE_URL`, PGLite arm always runs); "pglite" = `test/pglite-engine.test.ts`; "files" = number of `test/` files that mention the method name anywhere (grep, so an upper bound on real coverage).

### Segmented page storage (12 methods)

Shared SQL: `src/core/engine-sql/page-body.ts`. Limits: `src/core/page-body/limits.ts` (`STAGE_BATCH_BYTES` = 4 MiB for both engines, line 65-68; `SEGMENT_TEXT_GC_GRACE_MS` = 1 h, line 132; `BODY_STAGING_IDLE_MS` = 1 h, line 134; `BODY_STAGING_HARD_MS` = 24 h, line 135). Batches yield the event loop with `setTimeout(0)` (`page-body.ts:50`). Both engines call the shared functions with `this.engineSql` (PGLite `pglite-engine.ts:2539-2562`, PG `postgres-engine.ts:2398-2421`); only `readSegmentTexts`, `readManifest` and `readSegmentDerivations` use the unscoped read brand.

Test note for the whole family: `test/large-pages/segment-gc.test.ts` runs the GC helper cases on PGLite, `test/e2e/segment-gc-parity.test.ts` imports `PostgresEngine` (Postgres side). `test/e2e/segmented-publish-tiling-postgres.test.ts` and `test/large-pages/segmented-publish-tiling.serial.test.ts` cover the flip. No engine-parity or pglite-engine.test.ts hits for any of the other 11 methods (eng-parity 0, pglite 0 for each).

Ids are never reused. | Identical. files 12 (helpers/segment-gc-cases.ts, helpers/segmented-page.ts, helpers/page-body-fixtures.ts). |

### Per-segment derivation watermarks (3 methods)

### Config (6 methods)

Config table is `config (key, value)`. Both engines call `requestConfigValue` / `requestConfigRows` first (`config-snapshot.ts`). PG wraps the SQL in `connRetry` (`postgres-engine.ts:2753-2765`: `withRetry` with the bulk-retry options and `reconnect`), so a dropped pooler connection is retried and the pool rebuilt. PGLite has no retry wrapper.

| Method (iface line) | Impl PGLite / PG | What it does and SQL | PGLite vs PG; tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `getConfig(key): string \| null` (`engine.ts:2657`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2863-2868` / `postgres-engine.ts:2767-2777` | Request snapshot first; else `SELECT value FROM config WHERE key = $`. Returns `null` when the key is unset. | PG only: `connRetry`, and `throwIfRequestAborted()` before the statement (`postgres-engine.ts:3035` at `116d737413`). eng-parity 0; pglite 4; 489 files (ubiquitous). |
| `setConfig(key, value): void` (`engine.ts:2658`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2870-2877` / `postgres-engine.ts:2779-2787` | `INSERT INTO config (key, value) VALUES ($1, $2) ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE SET value = EXCLUDED.value`; then `noteRequestConfigWrite`. Autocommit, no explicit transaction. | PG only: `connRetry` (idempotent upsert). pglite 2; 1387 files. |
| `unsetConfig(key): number` (`engine.ts:2664`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2879-2886` / `postgres-engine.ts:2789-2796` | `DELETE FROM config WHERE key = $`. Returns affected rows (0 or 1). Calls `noteRequestConfigWrite(key, null)`. | Row count from `affectedRows` (PGLite) vs `count` (PG). eng-parity 0; pglite 0; 228 files. |
| `listConfigKeys(prefix): string[]` (`engine.ts:2670`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2888-2896` / `postgres-engine.ts:2797-2808` | The prefix is LIKE-escaped (`\`, `%`, `_`) in TS, then `SELECT key FROM config WHERE key LIKE $pattern ESCAPE '\' ORDER BY key`. Literal prefix match, no glob. | PG wraps in `connRetry`. eng-parity 0; pglite 0; 18 files (config-db-merge.test.ts, search-introspection-ops.test.ts, postgres-engine-config-reconnect.test.ts). |
| `getAllConfig(): Record<string, string>` (`engine.ts:2684`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2898-2905` / `postgres-engine.ts:2810-2821` | Request snapshot if one applies (`requestConfigRows`); else `SELECT key, value FROM config` (whole table, one round trip). Used by `loadConfigWithEngine` (about 44 keys per connect, `engine.ts:2672-2683`). | PG wraps in `connRetry`. eng-parity 0; pglite 0; 20 files (config-request-snapshot.test.ts, read-waves-gbra133.test.ts, requester-scope.test.ts). |
| `getConfigValues(keys: string[]): {key, value}[]` (`engine.ts:2968`) | `pglite-engine.ts:1458` / `postgres-engine.ts:1115`, delegating to `config.ts:8` | `SELECT key, value FROM config WHERE key = ANY($1::text[]) ORDER BY key` (`config.ts:9`). A key with no row is absent from the result. It reads the table itself and never the request config snapshot (unlike `getConfig` and `getAllConfig`). The result is ordered by key, so a Rust engine must sort the same way. | Same SQL. Unscoped read. PG runs it as `preparationRead(exec => ... refuseAborted(unscopedExecutor(exec)))` (`postgres-engine.ts:1115`), so an aborted request is refused before each statement and a read deadline bounds it. PGLite runs it directly with no guard. No `connRetry` on either. Tests by grep (18 files), mainly `engine-parity/import-path.test.ts`, `swept-member-request-abort.test.ts`, `search-entity-resolve-concurrency.test.ts`. |

### Migration support and raw SQL (5 methods)

| Method (iface line) | Impl PGLite / PG | What it does and SQL | PGLite vs PG; tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| `runMigration(version: number, sql: string): void` (`engine.ts:2687`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2908-2910` / `postgres-engine.ts:2824-2827` | Runs the migration text unmodified: PGLite `this.db.exec(sql)` (multi-statement), PG `this.sql.unsafe(sqlStr)` with no parameters. The `version` argument is ignored by both (`_version`). The runner that chooses versions is `src/core/migrate.ts` (not read in this part). | Different APIs: PGLite `exec` vs PG `unsafe`; multi-statement behavior must match on both (not verified here). eng-parity 0; pglite 0; 36 files (migrate.test.ts, v273-validate-timeouts.test.ts, function-search-path-pglite.test.ts, oauth-client-registered-via-migration.test.ts). |
| `getChunksWithEmbeddings(slug, opts?: {sourceId?, includeUnsealed?}): Chunk[]` (`engine.ts:2692`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2912-2914` / `postgres-engine.ts:2829-2831` -> shared `chunks.ts:1193-1209` (unscoped read) | `SELECT cc.* FROM content_chunks cc JOIN pages p ON p.id = cc.page_id WHERE <chunkVisibleFilter('p','cc') or chunkGenerationFilter when includeUnsealed> AND p.slug = $ [AND p.source_id = $] ORDER BY cc.chunk_index`. Raw preservation tools may include unverified chunks (`engine.ts:2691`). | Identical. eng-parity 0; pglite-engine.test.ts 2; 14 files (e.g. incremental-chunking.test.ts). |
| `executeRaw<T>(sql, params?, opts?: {signal?, timeoutMs?, deadlineAt?, jitOff?, prepare?}): T[]` (`engine.ts:2714`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2916-2942` / `postgres-engine.ts:2990-3018` (+ `runUnsafe` 2934-2992) | **PGLite:** pre-check signal (`AbortError`), then `db.query(sql, params)`. Autocommit WAL-writing statements (per `writesWal`) go through `PgliteCheckpointGuard.runStatement` (`pglite-engine/checkpoint-guard.ts`). An abort rejects the caller but the statement keeps running in WASM. `timeoutMs`, `deadlineAt`, `jitOff`, `prepare` are ignored. **Postgres:** `throwIfRequestAborted()`, `checkoutGauge` 'raw'. If `jitOff` or `timeoutMs` (outside a transaction): `holdSearchConnection`, then JIT-off envelope (`jit-off-statement.ts`) or `runBoundedStatement` (`BEGIN; SET LOCAL statement_timeout; <sql>; COMMIT` as one pipelined round trip, `bounded-statement.ts`, 57014 on timeout). Else `retryReadOnlyAutocommit(this.sql, runUnsafe)`. `runUnsafe` reserves a connection when a signal is present, sets `prepare` (default true, #5984), and on abort sends `CancelRequest`, then discards the connection after `cancelSettleMs`. No automatic retry of mutations on connection death (comment `postgres-engine.ts:3011-3017`). | PGLite has no server-side cancel, no timeout, no JIT control. PG retries only a read-only-transaction refusal (SQLSTATE 25006) by reopening `BEGIN READ WRITE` (`read-write-transactions.ts:65-76`). eng-parity 46; pglite 1; 3285 files (ubiquitous). |
| `executeRawDirect<T>(sql, params?, opts?: {signal?}): T[]` (`engine.ts:2732`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2955-2961` (delegates to `executeRaw`) / `postgres-engine.ts:3034-3059` | **PG:** signal pre-check before pool wait; if inside an open transaction (`_sql` is the reserved tx connection), stays on it; else if dual pool is active, uses `connectionManager.ddl()` (session-mode direct pool, port 5432 per `engine.ts:2720-2729`); else `this.sql`. `checkoutGauge` 'direct', then `retryReadOnlyAutocommit`. Single-statement UPDATEs only (Minion lock hot path). | PGLite is identical to `executeRaw` (no pooler). eng-parity 0; pglite 0; 30 files (persistence-diagnostics.test.ts, pool-gauge.test.ts, engine-control-routing.test.ts, sync-run-ordering.serial.test.ts). |
| `keywordBoundedRead<T>(read: (db: Pick<BrainEngine,'executeRaw'>) => Promise<T>): T` (`engine.ts:2745`) | `pglite-engine.ts:2945-2947` / `postgres-engine.ts:1507-1510` | **PG:** resolve `keyword_statement_timeout_ms` once per connection (`keyword-statement-timeout.ts`, default 8000, range 1000-60000), then `withScopedReadTransaction(undefined, undefined, tx => read(engineSqlOn(tx)), {alwaysTransaction: true, settings: {statement_timeout}})` so every statement in `read` is bounded and transaction-local. Used for the segmented-region read with its chunk-manifest probe (`engine.ts:2740-2744`). **PGLite:** `read(this)`, no timeout. | PG-only bound (57014 on overrun); PGLite unbounded. eng-parity 0; pglite 0; 9 files (read-transaction-early-result.test.ts, postgres-rls-scope-inventory.test.ts, engine-surface-coverage.test.ts). |

### Parity risks noted

1. `searchKeywordChunks` filter drift: Postgres honors `type`, `types`, `where` (frontmatter filter) and `exclude_slugs`; PGLite (`pglite-engine.ts:1638-1724`) ignores all four. The `stale` flag also differs (PG constant `false` at `postgres-engine.ts:1398`; PGLite computed from `timeline_entries` at `pglite-engine.ts:1713`). Any caller that narrows anchors by type gets different results per engine. Rust needs one filter contract tested on both.
2. Fact insert concurrency: the per-entity advisory lock (`facts.ts:120-123`, `149-152`) exists only on Postgres. PGLite inserts do not serialize the dedup window; a Rust embedded engine that is single-writer is fine, but the dedup contract must not rely on the lock.
3. `createVersion` default source: when `sourceId` is omitted, `createPageVersion` uses `'default'` (`pglite-engine.ts:2710`, `postgres-engine.ts:2566`). The interface doc (`engine.ts:2459-2463`) says the bare-slug lookup picks whichever row Postgres returns first. The code does not do that. Rust should follow the code, or the doc should be fixed first.
4. `insertFacts` return alignment: `ids` drops ON CONFLICT-skipped rows (`facts.ts:318`), so callers cannot map index to id, contrary to the doc at `engine.ts:2236`. Keep the contract explicit (return `null` per input, or document the filter).
5. Embedding cast: Postgres probes `facts.embedding` for `::halfvec` vs `::vector` (`postgres-engine.ts:2271-2304`); PGLite always casts `::vector`. A Rust store with one fixed vector type must not depend on the probe; the dimension and type must be identical to what both engines read back.
6. `getEdgesByChunk` has two implementations (`pglite-engine/code-edges.ts` and `engine-sql/code-edges.ts:141-172`). The `engine-sql` header (`code-edges.ts:6-10`) still says PGLite runs "one UNION with a shared LIMIT", but the PGLite file now applies a per-table LIMIT. Comment and code have drifted; the Rust version needs one behavior.
7. `executeRaw` semantics: PGLite ignores `timeoutMs`, `deadlineAt`, `jitOff`, `prepare` and cannot cancel a running statement. Postgres cancels with `CancelRequest` and bounds with `statement_timeout`. Callers that rely on a timeout (search, Minion locks) see no bound on PGLite. The interface doc (`engine.ts:2696-2713`) describes Postgres behavior.
8. `setPageAliases` replaces only `origin = 'frontmatter'` rows (`pages.ts:1188`). The interface doc (`engine.ts:2572-2579`) says it deletes "the page's existing page_aliases rows". Declared and subject aliases survive a frontmatter rewrite on purpose; the doc is out of date.
9. `updatePageContextualRetrievalState` mode `'none'` also clears chunk vectors and `embedding_signature` when the old mode was `title` or `per_chunk_synopsis` (`pages.ts:431-...`). The interface doc (`engine.ts:2612-2628`) says it stamps only the two columns plus `updated_at`. A Rust port that follows the doc would keep stale vectors.
10. Segmented flip and staging: `flipBodyGeneration` relies on `FOR UPDATE` on the page row and staging row (`page-body.ts:294`, `309`), and `stageSegmentChunks` on advisory lock plus `FOR KEY SHARE` (`page-body.ts:193-197`, Postgres only). PGLite has neither. A Rust embedded store needs its own serialization (a per-page mutex or single writer) and must reproduce the generation checks (`revision_changed`, `body_staging_expired`, `generation_incomplete`).
11. `upsertSegmentDerivations` is not atomic across batches: earlier batches commit before `DerivationWriteRefused` is thrown (`page-body.ts:521-542`). `sweepSegments` runs six statements without one transaction (`page-body.ts:425-487`); its correctness depends on the ordering and grace windows (1 h texts, 1 h idle and 24 h hard staging deadlines, `page-body/limits.ts:132-135`).
12. Chunk indexes: `stageSegmentChunks` derives `chunk_index` from `MAX(chunk_index) + 1` per page and never reuses an index (`page-body.ts:219-221`). Deleted chunks leave gaps, so a Rust store must not compact indexes.
13. `runMigration` runs raw SQL text: PGLite `db.exec` vs PG `sql.unsafe` (`pglite-engine.ts:2908`, `postgres-engine.ts:2824`). Multi-statement and transaction-control behavior of migration files must be checked on the Rust side. The `version` argument is ignored.
14. `getStats` and `getHealth` run their own statements on each engine (`getStats` is inline in both; `getHealth` shares `health.ts` but is wrapped differently: PG `jitOffRead` per statement, PGLite `plannerRead`). Results must match across engines; the planner-robustness notes at `health.ts:20-27` mean a Rust rewrite cannot rely on query plans for correctness.
15. `resolveAliases` throws on pre-v110 brains (`engine.ts:2563-2566`), while `resolveSlugWithAliasDetailed` returns null on pre-v105 brains (`pages.ts:1194-1223`). Callers depend on these two different fail modes.
16. Config: Postgres retries connection-class failures on every config accessor (`postgres-engine.ts:2753-2765`); PGLite has no equivalent. The request-scoped config snapshot (`config-snapshot.ts`) is used by both, so reads can be served from a snapshot taken before a write in the same request.
17. Facts health and recall reads use `now()` for validity (`valid_until > now()`) in SQL on both engines, so results depend on the wall clock at read time (TTL honesty, `facts.ts:419-427`). A Rust engine has to evaluate the same predicate at read time, not at write time.


---

## What a Rust engine must do to pass engine-parity

Scope: `test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts` (2762 lines, commit 8bbfd0b5e8), its helpers, and `docs/TESTING.md`. The file compares the Postgres engine against the in-process PGLite engine. No engine list is parametrized in this file. A Rust engine has to be added by hand to every describe block. A second suite, `test/engine-parity/`, runs a Rust arm (section 2b). The rest of this section calls `test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts` the legacy file. Five things block a pass of the legacy file as it stands:

1. The file is gated by `DATABASE_URL`. Without it, 16 of its 17 describe blocks skip, including the PGLite half. Only the `getRawData` block runs (1 test).
2. About 46 `executeRaw` calls and 17 `installFixtureChunks` calls rely on Postgres SQL and on the page-projection protocol. A Rust engine must either run that SQL or the test must be rewritten to typed methods.
3. Test 1592 reads `EXPLAIN (FORMAT JSON)` plans and asserts index names. This is Postgres-only.
4. The file never compares scores (no `toBeCloseTo`). Ordering and exact nullability are what matter.
5. `undefined` vs `null` matters in several places (see section 4).

Counts: 17 `describe` blocks (16 `describeBoth` + 1 ungated `describe`). 74 static `test(` calls. The `for (const q of QUERIES)` loop at line 137 turns one `test` into 3 runs. With `DATABASE_URL` set, 76 tests run. Without it, 1 runs.

### 1. How the parity test is structured

**Gating.** `SKIP_PG = !hasDatabase()` and `describeBoth = SKIP_PG ? describe.skip : describe` (`test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts:32-33`). `hasDatabase()` is `!!process.env.DATABASE_URL` (`test/e2e/helpers.ts`, `hasDatabase`). The header comment at lines 4-14 says "Always runs the PGLite half". That is wrong: the whole block is inside `describeBoth`, so PGLite is skipped too. The only always-on block is the `getRawData` describe at lines 2714-2762. Its arms array (`arms`, 2716-2723) pushes `pglite` always and `postgres` only when `!SKIP_PG`. That array is the closest pattern inside this file for a multi-engine list. The full version is `parityArms` in `test/engine-parity/harness.ts:298` (section 2b).

**Engine construction.** Postgres arm: `setupDB()` or `setupLegacyEmbeddingDB()` from `test/e2e/helpers.ts`. Both go through `db.ts` and `PostgresEngine`. `setupDB` TRUNCATEs every table in `ALL_TABLES` and re-seeds config (`test/e2e/helpers.ts`, `setupDB`). `setupLegacyEmbeddingDB` also configures the gateway to `LEGACY_EMBEDDING_CONFIG` (`test/helpers/legacy-embedding-config.ts`: `openai:text-embedding-3-large`, 1536 dims) and alters the vector column widths. Isolated variant: `isolatedPersistencePostgres()` in `test/helpers/persistence-postgres.ts` creates a fresh database per describe (used at 2386-2404 for open_loops only).

PGLite arm: `new PGLiteEngine(); await e.connect({}); await e.initSchema();` (for example 126-128, 1368-1370, 1457-1459). Each describe creates both and seeds them with the same function, usually `seedX(eng)` run twice.

Teardown: `afterAll` calls `pgliteEngine.disconnect()` and `teardownDB()` (30s timeout). beforeAll budgets are 90s, or 180s for the dense-hub block (2201).

**Fixtures.**
- Main corpus: `SEED_PAGES` (49-89) with 6 pages, each with `embeddingDim` and a one-hot embedding from `basisEmbedding(idx, 1536)` (35-39, `Float32Array` of length 1536).
- `QUERIES` (112-116): `'fat code thin harness'`, `'fat code thin harness part 3'`, `'fat code production'`.
- `seedEngine` (91-110): `putPage` + `installFixtureChunks` for each page, one `compiled_truth` chunk with `token_count = body.split(/\s+/).length`.
- Other seeds: `seedRelational` (1338-1359), `seedFederated` (1428-1447), `seedTraversal` (2023-2039), `seedDenseHub` (`test/helpers/dense-hub.ts`, 200 inbound + 200 outbound spokes, ring offsets, 5200 edges), `seedAliasOwners` (2241-2256), `seedKeyset` (1624-1639), CJK corpus (1952-1969), `seed` in the timeline plan block (1561-1568).
- Helper `installFixtureChunks` (`test/helpers/page-projection.ts`) calls `readProjectionSnapshot`, then `installPageProjection(..., {seal: true})`. These are in `src/core/page-state/projections.ts`. They use `engine.transaction`, `engine.lockPageKeys`, `engine.readPageSnapshot`, `engine.writeTextProjectionSeal` for the text seal (`src/core/page-state/text-seal.ts:18`), and `engine.executeRaw` for `SELECT archived FROM sources` (`projections.ts:321`), `UPDATE pages SET chunker_version` (`projections.ts:350`) and `DELETE FROM page_projection_jobs` (`projections.ts:353`). So a Rust engine must implement the projection revision model for the fixture helper to work at all.

**Comparison style.**
- Most tests run the same call on both engines and `expect(pgX).toEqual(pgliteX)`, or compare `new Set(...)`, or compare a derived shape string.
- Exact expectations are then pinned against absolute values, so both engines cannot be wrong in the same way.
- Ordering: `toEqual` on arrays is order-sensitive. Many tests `.sort()` first, which means they do not check order (see table).
- Scores: the only score asserted is `DreamVerdict.score` exact `0.31` (line 417). Search scores are never compared.
- Timestamps: checked with `instanceof Date`, `Number.isFinite(getTime())`, `toMatch(/\.\d{6}Z$/)`, or exact ISO strings (for example `'2026-09-01T23:59:59.000Z'` at 2477).

**Plugging in a third engine.** The harness suite (section 2b) has the Rust arm, so steps 1 to 4 are done there. Its cases call typed methods only (`test/engine-parity/harness.ts:18-19`), so steps 5 and 6 do not arise for it. Concrete steps for the legacy file, based on the current code:
1. The engine kind plumbing names `rust`. `readonly kind: 'postgres' | 'pglite' | 'rust'` is at `src/core/engine.ts:922`, and `EngineConfig.engine?: 'postgres' | 'pglite' | 'rust'` is at `src/core/types.ts:2374`. The `switch` in `src/core/engine-construct.ts:12-45` has a `rust` case at line 21 and a `default` at line 40 that throws. The `rust` case needs an absolute `database_path` and a loadable addon (`src/core/engine-construct.ts:21-39`).
2. Add an engine list to the test. Two existing helpers show the pattern. `test/helpers/two-engine.ts` has `type EngineKind = 'pglite' | 'postgres'` and `openTestBrain(kind, settings)` through `createEngine({engine: kind})`. `test/helpers/test-backends.ts` has `TestBackend = 'pglite' | 'postgres'`, `testBackends()`, and env `GBRAIN_TEST_BACKEND` (values `postgres`, `postgres-direct`, `pgbouncer`; anything else throws). The legacy file reads neither. The harness reads `testBackends()` and adds `rust` when `GBRAIN_TEST_RUST_ARM=1` (`test/engine-parity/harness.ts:298-300`, `test/engine-parity/rust-arm.ts:25-29`). Its matrix runs (`scripts/e2e-backend-matrix.txt:33`, `scripts/e2e-matrix.ts:36`) only rerun the same file.
3. Replace `pgEngine` / `pgliteEngine` pairs with `arms` (as in 2714-2729), or add a third variable to each of the 16 `beforeAll` blocks.
4. Gate the Rust arm on its own env var, and make the PGLite arm run without `DATABASE_URL`. Otherwise the Rust comparison can only run where Postgres exists. `.github/workflows/e2e.yml:423` runs the file as `tier1 engine-parity`. The Rust arm of the harness suite runs in `.github/workflows/rust-engine.yml:291-303`, one Bun process per file.
5. Replace or skip the EXPLAIN-based block (1557-1605) and the `jsonb_typeof` probes (1744-1752) for Rust. They inspect Postgres catalogs.
6. Replace the `executeRaw` seeding with typed calls, or provide a Postgres-SQL subset executor (see section 4, SQL surface).

### 2. Per-method table: methods covered by engine-parity

Covered means a direct `.method(` call in `test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts`. "Indirect" methods are covered only by helper code (listed in section 3). Line numbers are for the parity file. "Rust must" lists what a passing engine must return or do.

| `disconnect` (836) | afterAll blocks, e.g. 133, 1375, 2400 | none (cleanup) | disconnect without error, release files |
| `initSchema` (846) | every describe; 128, 1370, 2395 | none (must succeed) | create schema idempotently; a second call is a no-op |

### 2b. The harness parity suite

A second parity suite lives in `test/engine-parity/`. It has 53 case files. A case is a `parityTest(name, { methods: [...] }, scenario)` call (`test/engine-parity/harness.ts:322`). The `methods` array names the `BrainEngine` members the case covers. The harness runs the scenario on every arm from a reset brain. It fails when a declared member was not really called (`harness.ts:367-368`). It also fails when two arms return different values (`harness.ts:371-374`). A case can add `requires: 'raw_sql'` or `requires: 'schema_migration_lock'` (`harness.ts:80`, `test/engine-parity/arm-capabilities.ts:29`).

The arms are PGLite always, Postgres when `DATABASE_URL` is set, and Rust when `GBRAIN_TEST_RUST_ARM=1` (`harness.ts:298-300`, `test/engine-parity/rust-arm.ts:25-29`). With the flag set and no built addon, the run fails instead of skipping (`rust-arm.ts:57`). On the Rust arm a case must do one of three things. It returns what PGLite returns. It ends on the typed `engine_capability_missing` refusal. Or it differs only in the way a row in `test/rust-expected-divergence/` names (`harness.ts:402-412`). `coverage.test.ts:45-56` requires every case file to select its arms with `parityArms(testBackends())` and to have a row in `test/postgres-unit-arms.txt`. CI runs the Rust arm in `.github/workflows/rust-engine.yml:291-303`.

`test/engine-parity/coverage.tsv` is generated by `bun test/engine-parity/coverage.ts --write` (`coverage.ts:5`). It has one row per callable member. The columns are `method`, `family`, `covered`, `parity_cases`, `legacy_e2e`, `pglite`, `postgres` and `rust` (`coverage.tsv:53`). The three arm columns say what each arm must do for the member.
- `yes` means the case runs on the arm and its answer is compared.
- `serves` appears only in the Rust column. The Rust engine serves the member, so its answer must match PGLite, end on a typed refusal of an option it declines, or differ only as a divergence row names.
- `refuses:<capability>` means the arm must throw `engine_capability_missing` for that capability.
- `n/a:<why>` means the member does not exist on that arm.
- `-` means no case covers the member.

Counts from the 358 rows (`coverage.tsv:9` says 326 of 358 covered).
- Covered `yes` 326, `no` 32.
- PGLite column: 325 `yes`, 1 `n/a:postgres-only` (`holdSchemaMigrationLock`), 32 `-`.
- Postgres column: 326 `yes`, 32 `-`.
- Rust column: 254 `serves`, 72 `refuses`, 32 `-`. The 72 refusals name `raw_sql` (32), `pages` (7), `minion_queue` (6), `transactions`, `chunks` and `persistence_journal` (5 each), `code_edges` (3), `timeline`, `takes` and `links` (2 each), and `page_body`, `health` and `access_control` (1 each).
- The `legacy_e2e` column marks 65 members that the `test/e2e/engine-parity*.test.ts` files call. All 65 are also covered here.

The 32 members with no harness case are `listFiles`, `listSegmentedPages`, `invalidateWithdrawnPages`, `hasWithdrawnFenceClaim`, `readExportWithdrawalLedgerBytes`, `listExportWithdrawals`, `readBodyManifestOutline`, `listVersionTrustTiers`, `listVersionReasonWriters`, `listCoveredSources`, `countSearchVectorRows`, `readSourceState`, `readAdmissionSource`, `sharedSkillPackTablePresent`, `listSharedSkillPackRoots`, `getWorktreeBinding`, `hasExtractedEvents`, `listActiveAccessMembers`, `hasLabelledOnlyMember`, `listAccessTokens`, `notifierCoverageRecorded`, `readOpCheckpointKeys`, `readHealthMemoClock`, `listSynopsisSourceIds`, `expireMemoryProposals`, `listAwaitingMemoryProposals`, `readMemoryProposal`, `purgeMemoryProposals`, `readPendingProposalSummary`, `listMemoryProposalRows`, `listOwnerProposalRows` and `attachSourcePath`.

`covered` means a case declares the member and the harness saw a direct call. It does not measure how strong the assertions are. `coverage.test.ts` fails when the checked-in list differs from the generator output (`coverage.test.ts:27-30`). It also fails when the family table and the interface disagree (`coverage.test.ts:32-37`), and the generator throws when a member has no family (`coverage.ts:241`).
