### 4. Cross-cutting requirements a Rust engine must meet

**Error classes and messages.**
- The parity file asserts one error message: `refusing to overwrite non-empty page` (`/refusing to overwrite non-empty page/`, line 1860). The Rust engine must throw an error whose message contains that text, from `putPage`.
- `PageMissingError` (`src/core/engine-errors.ts:18-35`, messages `addLink failed: <endpoint> page "<slug>" (source=<id>) not found` and `addTimelineEntry failed: page ...`) is not asserted in this file. Other tests check it, so the Rust engine still needs it.
- This file expects a rejection only for blank overwrites. Other operations return `null`, `[]`, `false` or `0` for misses.
- `purgeDeletedPages(olderThanHours)` throws `PurgeAgeError` (`code` is `invalid_argument`) for NaN, a negative number or an infinite age. It throws before it reads or deletes anything. The check is `assertPurgeAge` (`src/core/engine-errors.ts:48`, class at `:39`), called from `src/core/engine-sql/pages.ts:349` and from the Rust store at `src/core/rust-engine/store-members/page-ops.ts:119`. The legacy file does not assert it. The harness case at `test/engine-parity/pages.test.ts:312` does.

**Null vs undefined vs missing.**
- `toBeUndefined()` for absent email fields: `result?.message_id`, `thread_id`, `source_subject` must be `undefined` (505-507, 541-543). Returning `null` fails the test. Omit these keys when not applicable. `thread_id` is set when frontmatter has it, even for non-email pages (506).
- `toBeNull()` for absent dream fields: `score` and `triage_version` must be `null`, while `segments` and `entities` must be `[]` (433-436).
- `getPage` miss: `toBeNull()` (785, 899, 1862 etc.). `getChunks` embedding: `toBeNull()` when not requested (979). `deleted_at`: `null` or Date; the test uses `deleted_at ?? null` (934) so either `null` or `undefined` for live rows would pass there, but `getPage(... includeDeleted)` requires a Date (2343).
- `resolveSlugsByPaths` miss: `Map.get()` returns `undefined` (1029-1030), not `null`.
- `resolveSlugWithAliasDetailed` no match: `toBeNull()` (2284-2285).
- `restorePage` / `softDeletePage`: booleans and `{slug}` or `null` (2335).

**Timestamps.**
- `Date` objects (must be `instanceof Date`): `listAllPageRefs.updated_at` (319-320), `listStalePagesForExtraction.updated_at` (1127), `getPage.ingested_at` (723-724), `getPage.deleted_at` (2343), `listPages` rows `updated_at` (1662 calls `.toISOString()` on them).
- ISO strings: `EntityCard.last_touched.last_timeline_date` (298, 304) and `open_threads[].date` must be `string | null` (299-301). `listStalePagesForExtraction.updated_at_iso` is a string (1140). `session_context_state.last_wake_at` must match `/\.\d{6}Z$/` (1735) and equal the input instant `2026-08-05T12:00:00.000Z` after `new Date()` (1734), so microsecond precision is required there. Loops: `due_at` equals `'2026-09-01T23:59:59.000Z'` (2477), i.e. millisecond precision; `opened_at` is a string (2455). Parity compares strings across engines exactly (1736), so both engines must format identically.
- Timeline `date` is a string (`src/core/types.ts:1769`). `created_at` is a Date (`types.ts:1773`).
- Keyset cursor compares `updated_at` as a timestamp plus slug. The keyset fixtures use whole-millisecond values (1616-1618), while `session_context_state.last_wake_at` is pinned at microsecond precision (1730-1735).

**IDs.**
- Numeric. `chunk.id` is passed back as `from_chunk_id` (2683-2684), so it must round-trip as a number.
- `loopId: 2 ** 40` (2511) must work without error and return zero rows (beyond int4). Use 64-bit ids for `open_loops` and any table with a serial id in this file.
- `canonical_chunk_id` is compared only for presence (1380-1385), because its absolute value depends on sequence state. `page_id` is normalized to 0 in the chain test (1416), so exact values are not compared.
- `slug_aliases`, `pages.id`, `facts.id` are used as opaque numbers (`newRow.id`, 2592-2599).

**JSON shapes.**
- `dream_verdicts.segments` and `entities` must be JSON arrays, not JSON strings (419-421). The Postgres path had a double-encode regression (#2339). A Rust engine must store native JSON arrays and return them as arrays.
- `session_context_state.standing_entities` and `surfaced_slugs` must be JSON arrays in storage: `jsonb_typeof = 'array'` (1744-1752). A Rust engine cannot run that SQL probe; it needs an equivalent typed check.
- `loops.evidence` must come back as an array with `message_id` and `quote` (2448-2452).
- `pages.frontmatter` keys drive search result metadata (`message_id`, `thread_id`, `subject` gated by an email identity, 440-545). `subject` alone must not set `source_subject` (499-508).
- Tag/link/timeline keys, `context` text, and `fact` text must round-trip byte-for-byte (for example `'a verbatim line with "quotes" and unicode'`, line 400).

**Source scoping.**
- Scalar `sourceId`, federated `sourceIds[]`, and unset (default floor `'default'`) must behave as in 951-981 and 2104-2116.
- Out-of-grant `sourceIds` returns empty and never falls back to `'default'` (973-974).
- Unscoped link reads must expose exact `from_source_id`/`to_source_id` for every row (1521-1526).
- Origin filter (F1): an edge whose `origin_source_id` is outside the grant is hidden under `sourceIds` (1442-1443, 1498-1501).
- Unscoped `getPage` and `getTimeline` pick the default source first (1819-1830, 1589).

**Soft delete and projection visibility.**
- Soft delete sets `deleted_at` and does not cascade (889-907). Links and tags stay in storage but are hidden from reads (797-821, 823-847).
- Re-adding a soft-deleted slug revives the same row (909-949): `deleted_at` clears, content updates, chunk and link sets are replaced, not duplicated (942-948).
- Unsealed chunks are hidden from default `getChunks` until sealed (942-945).

**Vector search.**
- Input: `Float32Array` of length 1536 (`basisEmbedding`, line 35). Stored vectors must accept 1536 dims (and legacy 1536 via `setupLegacyEmbeddingDB`).
- Test vectors are one-hot basis vectors, so many scores are exactly `0` or `1` and ties are common. The test asserts only top-1 (256), a `Set` of 6 pages (386), and the ordered top-5 on the escalation fixture, whose scores are distinct: `0.99 - i*0.0005` (367) and `0.6 - p*0.001` (375). A tie-break rule is therefore not pinned by the parity file.
- Float tolerance: none asserted. Dream score is compared exactly (`0.31`, 417), so store scores as f64.
- Escalation (347-387): a dense page with 120 chunks must not starve the result. Expect 6 distinct pages with `limit: 6`. The pre-fix engine returned 1 page. The inner candidate pool is 100 chunks (comment 349-351).

**Keyword ranking.**
- Strict AND by default. OR fallback only when `orFallback: true`, and then every row carries `keyword_relaxed: true` (205-233, 673-681).
- Top-1 slug must equal across engines for the three QUERIES (139-146). Top-5 must be the same set (150). Ordering beyond top-1 is not required, except where the test compares ordered arrays (CJK 1990, 1997; typed 168).
- `detail: 'high'` must change ordering on at least one engine (593-621), and must still include chat pages (612-614).
- CJK queries (`東京 会議`, `미팅 김대리`, `予算`) must return the matching page as top-1 with identical ordered slugs on both engines (1987-2006). The corpus includes an ASCII decoy page (1955), but the assertions check only CJK top-1 and ordered equality, not the decoy's absence.
- Title arm: exact title tokens must hit a page whose body lacks them (630-668).

**Traversal and caps.**
- `traversePaths` order is `depth, from_slug, to_slug` (2065). `traverseGraph` node order is not compared (sorted in tests).
- Cycle guard: A to B to A must terminate at depth 25 with 5 nodes and max depth 3 (2089-2097). Each edge appears once in `'both'` (2137-2142).
- Row cap: `TRAVERSE_PATH_ROW_CAP = 5000` (`src/core/engine-constants.ts:37`), one probe row beyond it, and `truncated` flag (2212-2232). Shallowest edges survive the cut (2218-2219).

**Time-based fixtures.**
- Facts TTL uses `Date.now()`-relative `valid_until` (2551-2568). The Rust engine must compare against its own clock consistently with `now()` used in raw SQL seeds (for example 1164, 1629, 2596). Clock skew between client and DB broke a test once (1131-1135), so pick one clock source.
- Entity card counts `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` as a timeline date (289).

**Chronicle, relational, and other helpers.** `buildEntityCard` (`src/core/verbs/entity-card.ts`) expects exact `active_fact_count` 105 for remote and 106 for local (297, 309-310). This needs `listFactsByEntity` (world-only for remote), `getTimeline`, `getLinks`, `resolveAliases`, and `searchKeyword`, which `entity-card.ts` calls directly (see the indirect list in section 2).

### Parity risks noted

- **Harness is Postgres-bound.** `test/e2e/helpers.ts` (`setupDB`, `setupLegacyEmbeddingDB`, `teardownDB`) uses `src/core/db.ts` and `PostgresEngine` directly, and TRUNCATEs tables. A Rust engine needs its own setup helper for this file; there is no engine-level switch to reuse here. The harness has one in `openArm` and `resetArm` (`test/engine-parity/harness.ts:118`, `:165`), with a Rust case in each.
- **Gating hides PGLite.** Everything except the `getRawData` block is inside `describeBoth`. Without `DATABASE_URL`, the file runs 1 test. The header comment (lines 12-14) and `docs/TESTING.md:509` ("each E2E file keeps a PGLite arm in the unit lane") both say otherwise. A Rust arm would be hidden the same way in this file unless the gating changed. The harness suite always runs its PGLite arm and gates the Rust arm on `GBRAIN_TEST_RUST_ARM=1` (`test/engine-parity/harness.ts:298-300`).
- **Raw SQL surface.** 46 `executeRaw` sites plus `installFixtureChunks` rely on Postgres SQL (`now()`, `interval`, `generate_series`, `ON CONFLICT`, `::jsonb`, `::timestamptz`, `ANALYZE`) and on the projection tables (`page_projection_jobs`, `text_projection_revision`, `knowledge_revision`, `search_vector`). An embedded Rust engine that does not run this SQL cannot pass without test rewrites.
- **Postgres-only introspection.** `test/helpers/timeline-plan.ts` runs `EXPLAIN (FORMAT JSON)` and asserts `Index Name` / `Index Cond` (1592-1604). Replace it with a Rust query-plan assertion or drop it for Rust.
- **Catalog probe.** `jsonb_typeof` checks (1744-1752) inspect the stored type in Postgres. Rust needs an equivalent typed check or to rely on the read-back shape.
- **Null vs undefined.** `toBeUndefined` (505-507, 541-543) vs `toBeNull` (433-435) on different fields. A generic "null for missing" serializer fails one set of tests.
- **Weak assertions hide drift.** No score comparison; `detail=high` only asserts "differs on at least one engine" (620); `listPages`/`listAllPageRefs` compare parity but not absolute order; `getTimeline` federated results are sorted. A Rust engine can pass these tests while ordering differs. Add stronger Rust-only checks where ordering matters.
- **Fixture protocol coupling.** `installFixtureChunks` (17 uses) depends on the projection revision protocol in `src/core/page-state/projections.ts`. Any Rust storage model must implement that protocol, or the fixture must be rewritten to call a public seal API. The seal step goes through the typed member `writeTextProjectionSeal`, which the Rust arm refuses with the `chunks` capability (`test/engine-parity/coverage.tsv:129`).
- **Unsealed chunk visibility.** `upsertChunks` writes are invisible to default `getChunks` until sealed (942-945). A Rust engine that exposes chunks immediately fails 942.
- **Uncovered surface.** 298 of 358 callable members are not called by the legacy file (section 3). The harness suite covers 326 of 358, and 32 have no cross-arm case (section 2b).
- **Clock coupling.** Facts TTL and stale-extraction tests mix `Date.now()` (client) and `now()` (server) clocks (1131-1135, 2551-2568, 2596). A Rust engine with one clock may disagree with the test's expectations at boundaries.
- **Engine-kind plumbing names Rust.** `BrainEngine.kind`, `EngineConfig.engine`, and `constructEngine` accept `postgres`, `pglite` and `rust` (`src/core/engine.ts:922`, `src/core/types.ts:2374`, `src/core/engine-construct.ts:12-45`).
- **Matrix runs do not test a third engine.** `scripts/e2e-backend-matrix.txt:33` and `.github/workflows/e2e.yml:423` only rerun this file against direct Postgres and PgBouncer. `GBRAIN_TEST_BACKEND` is not read by this file, so the matrix never exercises a new backend unless it is wired into `testBackends()` and this file. The Rust arm of the harness suite is not wired that way. It is switched on by `GBRAIN_TEST_RUST_ARM=1` (`test/engine-parity/rust-arm.ts:25-29`) and runs in `.github/workflows/rust-engine.yml:291-303`.


---

## Adding a third engine and binding options

Base path for all repo citations: the gbrain repo root (commit 8bbfd0b5e8, read-only). Paths below are relative to it.

### Engine selection today

**How an engine is chosen.** `GBrainConfig.engine` is the three-value union `'postgres' | 'pglite' | 'rust'` (`src/core/config.ts:37`). It is loaded from `~/.gbrain/config.json` (`config.ts:196`; the directory honors `GBRAIN_HOME`, `config.ts:1977`). `loadConfig` infers the engine: a `DATABASE_URL` env var forces `postgres`; otherwise the file's `engine` wins (`'rust'` is accepted as written), and a file with only `database_path` is `pglite` (`config.ts:742-750`). The value `rust` is a hand edit of `config.json`; no command writes it (`docs/ENGINES.md:1723`). `toEngineConfig` copies `engine`, `database_url`, `database_path` into the runtime `EngineConfig` (`config.ts:1968-1975`).

**The discriminator.** `EngineConfig.engine?: 'postgres' | 'pglite' | 'rust'` is at `src/core/types.ts:2374`. `BrainEngine.kind: 'postgres' | 'pglite' | 'rust'` is at `src/core/engine.ts:922`, beside `BrainEngine.capabilities` (`engine.ts:923`), the named set of what an engine can do (`src/core/engine-capabilities.ts`). `EngineKind` in `src/core/sql-engine-kind.ts:11` is the same three-value union for SQL-only paths. `DegradedEngineOptions.kind` is at `src/core/degraded-engine.ts:63` and stays `'postgres' | 'pglite'`. `EngineSettings.engine` (embedding host) is at `src/core/embedded-engine.ts:14` and stays `'postgres' | 'pglite'`. `EngineStatusReport.effective_engine` and `config_file_engine` are at `src/commands/engine-status.ts:138-139` and take all three values.

**Construction chain.**

| File | Role | Lines |
|---|---|---|
| `src/core/engine-construct.ts` | `constructEngine` switch: `case 'pglite'` dynamic-imports `pglite-engine.ts`, `case 'postgres'` imports `postgres-engine.ts`, `case 'rust'` loads the verified addon and calls `openRustEngine` (it needs an absolute `database_path`, else `config_error`); default throws `Unknown engine type ... Supported engines: postgres, pglite, rust.` | 12-45 (`rust` case 21-39, default 40-44) |
| `src/core/engine-factory.ts` | `createEngine` = `constructEngine` + chat-usage sink registration + wraps `disconnect`. When `gbrain engine shadow start` has turned shadow mode on for the brain, it also wraps the engine with `attachShadow` (`src/shadow/attach.ts`) | whole file (40 lines; shadow at 31-37) |
| `src/core/engine-connect.ts` | `connectEngineForServe`: `loadConfig`, `createEngine(toEngineConfig(config))`, then `connectWithRetry`; PGLite one-shot reads set `pglite_shared_buffers`, Postgres one-shot sets `pool_max: 2` | 76-110 (one-shot constants 34-42) |
| `src/core/engine-open.ts` | Experimental `openEngine(settings)` for embedding hosts. `checkSettings` refuses any engine other than the two (`:94`); `SETTINGS_FIELDS` names `database: ['url','directUrl','path']`; `constructEngine` at `:166`; PGLite only accepts `database.path`, Postgres only `database.url` (`:92-106`) | 94-106 |
| `src/core/embedded-engine.ts` | Settings types and `markEmbeddedEngine` registry; engine-kind-agnostic except the `engine` union | 11-20 |
| `src/core/degraded-engine.ts` | Lazy-reconnect proxy for `gbrain serve` when Postgres is down at startup. Scope is Postgres only (header comment, lines 17-24); `kind` is an explicit getter (`:234-235`) that defaults to `postgres` while dead | 17-24, 63, 116-240 |
| `src/core/engine-key.ts`, `src/core/engine-root.ts` | `engineKey` and `ENGINE_ROOT` symbol; kind-agnostic (no change needed) | whole files |
| `src/core/rust-engine.ts` | `openRustEngine({ addon, databasePath })` returns the Rust `BrainEngine` (`kind` is `'rust'`, `:94`). Members the store serves run on it. Every other member rejects `engine_capability_missing` naming its capability (`rust-engine/members.ts`). `connect` takes the owner lock or becomes a client of the owner process | 1-17, 93-94 |
| `src/core/rust-engine/addon-loader.ts` | Finds and loads the native addon. It takes the `GBRAIN_RUST_ENGINE_ADDON` development override, or a cached addon whose SHA-256 equals the cached release manifest of this gbrain version. It never reaches the network. Failures are `rust_engine_addon_missing` or `rust_engine_addon_mismatch`, raised before the brain is touched | 1-19, 115-149, 163-175 |
| `src/core/rust-engine/owner-ipc/` | Owner protocol for a Rust brain. The first process takes `<database_path>.gbrain-owner.lock` through `tryAcquireNativeLock` and serves the engine on a socket. Other processes are clients | `election.ts:1-10, 16, 60` |
| `src/shadow/` | Shadow mode, off unless `gbrain engine shadow start`. A supervised child loads the addon and replays sampled reads on a Rust store seeded from the brain. The serving process never loads the addon | `src/shadow/child.ts:1-14` |

**Config and CLI surfaces.**

- `gbrain init --pglite` sets `isPGLite` (`src/commands/init.ts:56`). An existing Postgres config is kept by `init --force` and by `init --non-interactive` unless an engine flag is passed (`init.ts:170-181` at `8bbfd0b5e8`, `init.ts:182-186` and `:193` at `116d737413`). Bare `init` with an existing `rust` or `pglite` config also keeps it unless an engine flag is passed. A kept Rust brain is reported and its config and store are left alone (`init.ts:124-127`, `src/commands/init-existing-engine.ts:15-27`). There is no `--rust` flag. PGLite init creates `createEngine({engine:'pglite'})` at `init.ts:1144`. Init hints and error text name `gbrain init --pglite --no-embedding` (`src/core/engine-connect.ts:59-64`, `init.ts:598-609`).
- `--pglite` appears 60 times in 20 files, including `src/commands/init.ts`, `setup.ts`, `bootstrap.ts`, `reinit-pglite.ts`, `doctor/checks/db-connection.ts`, `doctor/checks/pglite-worker.ts`, `src/mcp/status-mode.ts`, `src/cli/top-help.ts`, `src/core/error-registry.ts`, `src/core/cli-flag-registry.generated.ts` (generated; regenerate, not hand-edit). Exact generator not verified.
- `gbrain engine status [--json] [--probe] [--brain <id>]` (`src/commands/engine-status.ts:1-80`) is engine-free. It dispatches before `connectEngine`. PGLite lock state is reported via `inspectLockHolder` (`pglite_lock`). For a Rust brain the probe checks `records.redb` and `FORMAT` in the store directory before it opens anything, then proves the store with a typed `getConfig('version')` read instead of `SELECT 1` (`engine-status.ts:251-264`). It reports addon refusals as typed notes (`engine-status.ts:422`). It reports no lock state for a Rust brain.
- `gbrain reinit-pglite` refuses unless `cfg.engine === 'pglite'` (`src/commands/reinit-pglite.ts:42`).
- `gbrain migrate --to postgres|pglite` and graduation: `src/commands/migrate-engine.ts`, `migrate-graduation.ts`.
- `gbrain engine shadow start|status|stop|report|seed|reconcile` (`src/commands/engine-shadow.ts:55-64`) compares the Rust engine against a brain's reads. `gbrain engine swap --to rust --plan` prints a read-only swap plan, and the run and failback forms are refused with `invalid_params` (`src/commands/engine-swap.ts:1-8`). Both dispatch from `src/cli/commands/engine.ts`.

**Size of the branch surface.** Counts are regex-based, so treat them as approximate.

| Pattern | Count | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `'pglite'` string literal (all of `src/`) | ~364 occurrences in ~195 files | broad |
| `'postgres'` string literal (all of `src/`) | ~343 occurrences in ~178 files | broad |
| Strict `kind ===/!== 'pglite'|'postgres'` | ~262 sites | by dir: `core/schema-migrations` 29 files, `core/persistence` 27, `commands/doctor` 24, `core/facts` 5, `core/minions` 4, `commands/jobs` 4, `core/page-body` 3, `core/notifier` 3, `core/search` 2, `core/cycle` 2 (plus 1-count files) |
| `sqlFor` per-engine SQL | 42 uses in 34 files | `schema-migrations/types.ts:29` (`sqlFor?: { postgres?: string; pglite?: string }`); picked at `src/core/migrate.ts:548` (`m.sqlFor?.[engine.kind] ?? m.sql`) |
| Transaction branch on kind | 1 | `src/core/migrate.ts:221` (`useTransaction || engine.kind === 'pglite'`) |
| Doctor checks mentioning PGLite | 40 of 216 check files | `src/commands/doctor/checks/` (e.g. `pglite-worker.ts`) |

Main categories of branch:

1. **Migrations.** `sqlFor` dialect picks, `pglite`-only repair and lock migrations. A non-SQL engine can use the engine-agnostic `sql` fallback only if its migrations are dialect-free, which current raw-SQL migrations are not.
2. **Persistence and locking.** The PGLite single-writer lock (`src/core/pglite-lock.ts`, `PgliteBusyError` at lines 19-25) and the persistence owner socket (`src/core/persistence/local-client.ts`, "Engine-free CLI routing to the process that already owns a PGLite brain"). The owner socket is the existing single-writer routing for CLI, MCP and cron.
3. **Doctor and repair.** `commands/doctor/checks/*`, `pglite-repair.ts`, `pglite-resetwal.ts`, `pglite-leftovers-check.ts`, `db-repair.ts`.
4. **Ops and searches.** Postgres-only features (pg_trgm, statement timeouts, pool) are gated on `kind === 'postgres'`. Engine-sql domain modules (`src/core/engine-sql/`) have two dialect adapters, `dialect-pglite.ts` and `dialect-postgres.ts`.

**Docs.** `docs/ENGINES.md` says "Two engines ship today" (line 7). A separate section, "The Rust engine shell" (`docs/ENGINES.md:1716`), describes the opt-in Rust engine. The "Adding a new engine" guide is at `docs/ENGINES.md:2138-2170`. Its step 3 says a non-SQL engine implements typed methods instead of a `SqlExecutor`, declares no `raw_sql` capability, and refuses each member it cannot serve with `engine_capability_missing`. Its closing section (line 2170) still says every method in `BrainEngine`. Its step 4 says to store `{"engine":"myengine"}` in config (line 2154). That is not accurate. `GBrainConfig.engine` and `EngineConfig.engine` are closed unions (`config.ts:37`, `types.ts:2374`), and `constructEngine` throws on unknown values (`engine-construct.ts:41`). Adding a further kind needs type changes too. The capability matrix at `ENGINES.md:2172-2185` covers only the two SQL engines.

### What adding a third engine kind would touch

Minimum set for a non-SQL engine. Kind `'rust'` is the Rust engine, so each row says what is done for it at H and where. Rows marked open apply to any further kind:

| # | Change | File(s) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Widen `BrainEngine.kind` union. Done for `'rust'` | `src/core/engine.ts:922` |
| 2 | Widen `EngineConfig.engine` and `GBrainConfig.engine` unions; decide config key for a storage path (e.g. `database_path` reuse vs a new key). Done for `'rust'`, which reuses `database_path` as the store directory | `src/core/types.ts:2374`, `src/core/config.ts:37`, `config.ts:742-750` (inference), `config.ts:1968-1975` (`toEngineConfig`), `engine-construct.ts:26-30` |
| 3 | New `case` in constructEngine (dynamic import of the Rust binding module). Done for `'rust'` | `src/core/engine-construct.ts:21-39` |
| 4 | Engine class implementing all of `BrainEngine` (the interface is `engine.ts:920-3421`, 360 members, of which 358 are callable methods and 2 are properties, `kind` and `capabilities`). Non-SQL engines implement each method directly; SQL-dialect adapters are optional. Done in part for `'rust'`. The class has every member, the store serves some, and the rest reject `engine_capability_missing` | `src/core/rust-engine.ts:93`, `src/core/rust-engine/members.ts`, `src/core/rust-engine/store-members/` |

There is no lock field for a Rust brain | `src/commands/engine-status.ts:138-139` (probe at `:251-264`, and the `mount?.engine` mapping at `:314-315`) |
| 8 | `init` flag (e.g. `--rust`) and `isPGLite`-style branches; `init.ts:56`, `init.ts:170-181`, `init.ts:1144`. Done in part for `'rust'`. There is no `--rust` flag, and bare `init` keeps an existing Rust config (`init.ts:124-127`, `init-existing-engine.ts:15-17`) | `src/commands/init.ts`, `src/commands/init-existing-engine.ts`, `src/core/cli-flag-registry.generated.ts` |

Check each of the ~29 strict kind branches in `schema-migrations/`. Open. The Rust engine has no SQL dialect, so its `runMigration` refuses with `engine_capability_missing` (reason `raw_sql`, `src/core/rust-engine/members.ts:26`), and its schema is created when the store opens (`src/core/rust-engine/store-members/index.ts:48-49`). `applyOneMigration` still picks `sqlFor?.[engine.kind] ?? sql` (`migrate.ts:550`). ### Native module precedent: `native/locks/`

**Contents.** `native/locks/locks.c` is a C Node-API v3 addon (`NAPI_MODULE_INIT` at line 301, `napi_define_properties` at line 333) exposing `openLock`, `tryLock`, `close`, plus Windows IPC, plus export publication (`export-publication.h`). POSIX uses `flock`; Windows uses `LockFileEx`.

- `native/locks/README.md`: design and CI notes. Says all eight addons are checked in so source installs work with `bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts`.
- `native/locks/manifest.json`: `version 1`, `napi 3`, `zig 0.14.1`, `input_sha256`, and per-artifact `sha256` and `bytes` for eight targets (`linux-x64-glibc`, `linux-arm64-glibc`, `linux-x64-musl`, `linux-arm64-musl`, `darwin-x64`, `darwin-arm64`, `win32-x64`, `win32-arm64`).
- `native/locks/package.json`: private package boundary, not a package. Its README note says this prevents plugin hosts from treating the whole checkout as one native package (#6026).
- `native/locks/prebuilds/*.node`: exactly the eight binaries, checked into git.
- `native/locks/vendor/node-v22.15.0/`: `node_api.h`, `node_api_types.h`, `js_native_api.h`, `js_native_api_types.h`, `LICENSE`, vendored from Node v22.15.0.
- `native/locks/abi-check.c`: compile-time layout checks (`_Static_assert` for Darwin `stat`, open flags, lock constants). Compiled only on macOS against the real SDK in `native-locks.yml`.
- `native/locks/darwin-abi.h`, `windows-napi.h` (resolves used Node-API symbols from the running executable, so it does not load a separate `node.exe`), `windows-ipc.h`, `export-publication.h`.

**Build.** `scripts/native/build.ts` compiles with pinned Zig 0.14.1 via `zig cc -target <triple> -shared -fPIC -O2 -s -DNAPI_VERSION=3`. Targets are glibc 2.17 baseline, musl, `x86_64-macos.13.0`, and `x86_64-windows-gnu`. It sets `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` and `-Wl,--build-id=none` for reproducible bytes (`build.ts:39-50`). `scripts/native/toolchain.json` pins archive URLs and SHA-256 (zig for x86_64/aarch64 linux, macOS, Windows). `scripts/native/verify.ts` recomputes `input_sha256` and compares artifact bytes against a rebuild; `--write-manifest` regenerates the manifest. The README says the rebuild must be byte-identical across CI runners.

**Load from Bun.** `src/core/persistence/native-lock.ts`:

- `loadBindingSync()` (lines 94-140) picks a literal `require('../../../native/locks/prebuilds/<target>.node')` by `process.platform`/`process.arch`/detect-libc (`familySync`, `GLIBC`, `MUSL`). Literal requires are deliberate: "Bun embeds each .node asset in its compiled executables. Dynamic prebuild discovery does not embed them." (comment at lines 105-107).
- It validates `binding.target === target` and that required functions exist, then caches. Errors become `NativeLockUnavailableError` with code `writer_lock_unavailable` (lines 27-33).
- Lazy: loaded only by callers that need a lock. `nativeLockCapability()` and `nativeExportPublisher()` expose capability checks. The Rust engine's owner election also calls `tryAcquireNativeLock` from this file (`src/core/rust-engine/owner-ipc/election.ts:16, 60`).
- This file has no `process.dlopen` and no `createRequire` for `.node`. A second loader, `src/core/rust-engine/addon-loader.ts`, calls `process.dlopen` on a computed path for the Rust engine addon (`addon-loader.ts:58-60`), on purpose, so the addon is not bundled into compiled executables (`addon-loader.ts:13-16`). `src/core/cli-socket/peer-cred.ts:20`, `:38` and `src/core/pid1-reaper.ts:91-94` use `bun:ffi` `dlopen` against system libs (`libSystem.B.dylib`, libc), not gbrain-owned addons.

**CI.**

- `.github/workflows/native-locks.yml` (reusable): `native` job matrix over `linux-x64-glibc`, `linux-arm64-glibc`, `darwin-arm64`, `darwin-x64`, `win32-x64`, `win32-arm64` on Bun 1.4.4. Each cell runs `scripts/native/build.ts --target <t>`, `verify.ts --rebuilt`, the native lock tests, `compiled-smoke.ts`, and the data-safety suites. A separate `musl` job (line 336) runs `linux-x64-musl` and `linux-arm64-musl` in musl userspace. Scope is narrowed on PRs (`scope=smoke|primary|full`), and a pull request runs no darwin cell.
- `.github/workflows/test.yml`, `heavy-tests.yml`, `persistence-validation.yml` also reference native paths.
- `.github/workflows/rust-engine.yml` is a separate workflow for the Rust engine. Its `addon` job builds the addon with `bun run rust:build`, runs the Bun smoke test (`rust/smoke/run.ts`, which includes a `bun build --compile` executable) and the Rust engine tests on Linux x64 and macOS arm64, and uploads the `.node` file (`rust-engine.yml:7-17`, `:343-348`).
- `.github/workflows/release.yml:121-131` builds release binaries for only two targets: `bun-darwin-arm64` (macos-26) and `bun-linux-x64`. Line 170 runs `bun build --compile --no-compile-autoload-bunfig ... --target=<t> --outfile bin/<artifact> src/cli.ts src/core/skills/resolver/`. Line 175 runs `bun scripts/native/verify.ts && bun scripts/native/compiled-smoke.ts --binary bin/<artifact>`, which asserts the executable embeds the matching lock addon. No step in `release.yml` builds or publishes the Rust engine addon. Its release targets are `linux-x64-glibc` and `darwin-arm64` (`src/core/rust-engine/addon-manifest.ts:30`).
- Tests: `test/native-lock.test.ts`, `test/scripts/native-lock-prebuilds.test.ts` (tamper and matrix-coverage checks), `test/native-locks-package-boundary.test.ts`.

**Compiled binaries.** `bun build --compile` embeds `.node` files reached by a literal `require`. Computed paths are not embedded. The compiled binary does not use the repo's `import ... with { type: 'file' }` pattern for addons; that pattern is used for PGLite WASM and tree-sitter WASM (`src/core/pglite-embedded-assets.ts:1-40`, `src/core/pglite-embedded-asset-paths.ts`). Verified in source only; the packaging assertion is in `release.yml:175`. The Rust engine addon is the other case. It loads from a verified file under the gbrain home and is not embedded (`addon-loader.ts:13-16`), and `rust/smoke/run.ts:121-130` checks that a compiled executable can load it.

### Binding options

**What the repo ships.** The binding is not open. The Rust engine is the in-process napi-rs option (Option 1 below). `rust/crates/gbrain-napi` is a `cdylib` built on `napi` 3 (`rust/crates/gbrain-napi/Cargo.toml:11, 14`). It is built with `bun run rust:build` (`package.json:52`) and released with `panic = "unwind"`, so a Rust panic becomes a JavaScript error (`rust/Cargo.toml:15`). `constructEngine` selects it for `engine: 'rust'` (`engine-construct.ts:21-39`). The loader takes the `GBRAIN_RUST_ENGINE_ADDON` development override, or a cached addon whose SHA-256 equals the cached manifest of this gbrain version, and never reaches the network (`addon-loader.ts:1-19`). Nothing in gbrain writes that cache yet (`addon-loader.ts:10-11`), so an install without the override refuses a Rust brain with `rust_engine_addon_missing`. The design rejects a sidecar process (`docs/designs/RUST_ENGINE.md:124`). The repo has no WASM or `bun:ffi` engine code.

Current Bun facts used below (sources at the end):

- Bun supports Node-API and `require()` / `process.dlopen()` of `.node` files. Compatibility is "best effort" for the exact addon, Bun version, OS and arch. Bun 1.4 aligned N-API status and error behavior with Node 26 and added `napi_create_async_work` and threadsafe functions with teardown fixes.
- napi-rs treats Bun as best effort; its Bun test job is non-blocking upstream, so each addon must be tested on Bun before support is claimed.
- `bun:ffi` is still experimental; Bun's docs say not to rely on it in production. `dlopen`, `JSCallback` are engine-native in current Bun. `threadsafe: true` callbacks marshal to the JS thread with a per-call cost.
- `node:wasi` in Bun is partial: `wasiImport` and `start()` work, `getImportObject()` and `sock_accept` are missing, and WASI preview1 has no `mmap`.

#### Option 1: napi-rs native addon (in-process)

| Aspect | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Bun fit | Good for the `.node` precedent already in the repo. Bun 1.4.4 is the repo floor (`package.json:261-262`, `>=1.4.4`). Bun 1.4 covers async work and threadsafe functions. napi-rs says Bun is best effort, so the repo must own a Bun test job. `.github/workflows/rust-engine.yml` runs one on Linux x64 and macOS arm64. |
| Async and threads | `napi_create_async_work` and threadsafe functions are supported. Use them for blocking Rust I/O so the JS thread is not blocked. Teardown rules still apply: join native threads before the environment is torn down. |
| Buffers and embeddings | Input `Buffer`/`TypedArray` can be read in place with `napi_get_buffer_info` (already used in `export-publication.h:509`). Copy-in is safe. Zero-copy out to JS (external ArrayBuffer with a finalizer) is a napi feature whose behavior under Bun must be verified before depending on it; not verified here. |
| Prebuild matrix | The release addon targets are `linux-x64-glibc` and `darwin-arm64` (`addon-manifest.ts:30`), the two targets `release.yml` builds binaries for. The addon is built with cargo through `rust/scripts/build.ts`, not with zig. napi-rs cross-build: musl via `cargo-zigbuild` (adds `-crt-static` off); Windows MSVC via `cargo-xwin` (GNU Windows not supported in that mode). The repo's zig-cc pipeline does not build Rust. Use `napi build -x` or native CI runners per target, and keep the byte-compare or rebuild-verify step if the repo wants the `verify.ts` guarantee. Windows arm64 and musl are the least-tested on Bun per the napi-rs note. |
| `bun build --compile` | Works if the `.node` is reached by a literal `require`, as `native-lock.ts:108-122` does. The repo's Rust loader does not do this. It loads a verified file with `process.dlopen` and keeps the addon out of compiled executables (`addon-loader.ts:13-16`). Bun may extract the addon to a temp file at runtime; `TMPDIR` must be absolute (Bun issue 45054). Verify with the existing `release.yml:175` style assertion. |
| Crash isolation | None. A segfault, abort or uncaught Rust panic kills the gbrain process (CLI, `serve`, cron). Every exported function needs `catch_unwind`. |
| Multi-process | Each gbrain process would open the store in-process, so the store needs a single-writer story. The repo already has `native/locks` `flock` and the owner-socket routing used for PGLite. The Rust engine takes its own owner lock with the `native/locks` addon and serves other processes on its own owner socket (`rust-engine/owner-ipc/election.ts:1-10`). Two addons would load in one process (lock addon plus engine addon). |
| Per-call overhead | Lowest of the four: a napi call and marshalling, no syscalls or serialization. |
| Startup | One `dlopen` per process. No extra process. |
| Effort | Medium: Rust crate, napi bindings for the `BrainEngine` surface (or a narrow C-ABI-like surface wrapped in TS), prebuild matrix, Bun CI lane, compile smoke. |
| Risk | Medium-high. Crash blast radius; Bun napi gaps for newer APIs; prebuild matrix cost (8 binaries, reproducibility vs cargo output). |
| Parity tests | Run `test/e2e/engine-parity.test.ts` with the third engine factory, same as PGLite and Postgres; add a `native-lock-prebuilds`-style test (binary target check, manifest hashes) and a compiled-binary smoke (`scripts/native/compiled-smoke.ts` pattern). |

#### Option 2: Rust sidecar process (stdio or unix socket, JSON-RPC or binary framing)

| Aspect | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Bun fit | Pure TS client; no Node-API. Bun `child_process` / `Bun.spawn` plus a unix socket. Matches the repo's existing owner-socket and `cli-socket` design (`src/core/cli-socket/server.ts` header: "The serve side of the warm CLI socket"; `src/core/persistence/local-client.ts`). |
| Crash isolation | Full. A Rust crash kills the sidecar, not gbrain; the TS side can restart and reconnect (the repo already has reconnect machinery for Postgres in `degraded-engine.ts`). |
| Multi-process | Natural single writer: the sidecar owns the store and serializes CLI, MCP and cron requests. This is the main architectural advantage given the repo's current PGLite single-writer lock and owner-socket routing. Requires the sidecar's own lifecycle (start, lock, shutdown, stale socket cleanup, peer-uid checks as in `cli-socket/peer-cred.ts`). |
| Per-call overhead | Highest. A syscall or socket round trip plus framing per call. JSON is expensive for embeddings (`f32` vectors); use length-prefixed binary frames for vectors and bulk page bodies. Not measured. |
| Startup | Per-CLI-invocation cost: spawn the sidecar and open the store, unless the sidecar stays resident (the owner socket pattern). The repo already tunes one-shot reads (`engine-connect.ts:34-42`), so this matters. Measure before committing. |
| `bun build --compile` | The sidecar binary must be embedded as a file asset (`import ... with { type: 'file' }`, the `pglite-embedded-assets.ts` pattern), extracted or read from `/$bunfs`, and run with the executable bit preserved. The exec-bit and extraction behavior in compiled binaries is not verified here. |
| Prebuild matrix | Standard Rust cross-compile per target (no Node-API ABI matrix); the output is one executable per platform rather than a `.node`. Windows and musl still need runners. |
| Effort | Medium-large: protocol design, supervisor, lifecycle, error mapping into `opError` shapes, reconnect, embed-and-extract for compiled binaries, plus the same CI matrix. |
| Risk | Medium. Latency and marshalling; two processes to keep in sync on version; a second lock domain; sidecar orphaning on crash. |
| Parity tests | Same engine-parity suite via the TS client. Add process-kill and restart tests (the persistence-chaos suites are the model), stale-socket cleanup, and a concurrency test for CLI + MCP + cron against one sidecar. |

#### Option 3: WASM (wasm32-wasi / wasm-bindgen)

| Aspect | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Bun fit | Mirrors PGLite's model: WASM loaded in-process, assets embedded via `with { type: 'file' }` (`pglite-embedded-assets.ts`). No native packaging per platform: one artifact runs everywhere. |
| Filesystem | WASI preview1 `preopens` work in Bun but are partial and not a security sandbox (Node and Bun docs both say so). No `mmap`: a storage engine must use linear memory and ordinary file I/O, which constrains page-cache and index design. |
| Locks | WASI has no `flock`. The single-writer lock in `native/locks` is a native addon. A WASM engine would need a host import (napi or ffi) for locking or accept single-process use. This is a real gap for the CLI + MCP + cron model. |
| Threads | WASM shared memory and atomics work with workers; WASI itself has no thread creation. Bun supports the shared-memory path, but this is more complex than a native build. |
| Performance | Unknown for this workload; usually slower than native for vector and index work, and no native SIMD guarantees. Not measured. |
| Crash isolation | In-process trap, so a Rust panic is a WASM trap that can still kill the gbrain process unless caught at the boundary. |
| Effort | Large for a storage engine: WASI file semantics, locking, memory limits (wasm32 is 4 GB addressable), and performance work. |
| Risk | High for a storage engine; low risk for pure compute such as a chunker or ranker. |
| Parity tests | Same engine-parity suite; plus WASI file-semantics tests (fsync, rename, lock failure) and a memory-growth test. |

#### Option 4: `bun:ffi` with a C ABI

| Aspect | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Bun fit | Bun-only, experimental per Bun's docs. gbrain already uses `bun:ffi` for small syscalls (`src/core/cli-socket/peer-cred.ts:20-40`, `src/core/pid1-reaper.ts:89-94`), so the team has the pattern. |
| Surface | Rust `cdylib` with a C header (cbindgen) and `dlopen(path, { symbols })`. Scalar and pointer calls are straightforward; a storage API with owned buffers and handles needs manual free functions. |
| Callbacks | `JSCallback` is not thread-safe by default; `threadsafe: true` adds a per-call marshalling cost. Avoid callbacks from Rust threads in the design. |
| Async | No Node-API async-work equivalent; async must be built with Rust threads and polling or a `threadsafe` callback. |
| `bun build --compile` | Same embedding question as napi: the `.dlopen` path must be a file that exists at runtime; extraction needed. Not verified for ffi specifically. |
| Crash isolation | None (same as napi). |
| Effort | Medium for scalars, large for a full `BrainEngine`. |
| Risk | High: experimental API; Bun may change it; no Node-API stability. |
| Parity tests | Same as napi; add ABI and free-function leak tests. |

### Comparison summary

| | napi-rs in-process | Sidecar process | WASM | bun:ffi |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bun maturity | Stable N-API, best effort for napi-rs | Not Bun-specific (plain IPC) | WASI partial in Bun | Experimental |
| Crash isolation | None | Full | None (trap) | None |
| Single writer across CLI+MCP+cron | Needs lock addon and owner socket | Natural | Needs host lock (no flock in WASI) | Same as napi |
| Per-call cost | Lowest | Highest | Medium | Low |
| Startup | Lowest | Spawn + open per CLI call unless resident | Compile WASM per process (cache?) | Lowest |
| Prebuild matrix | 8 (or 6+musl) `.node` per target | One executable per target | One `.wasm` | 8 shared libs |
| `bun build --compile` | Literal `require` embeds; may extract | File asset + extract + exec bit | `with {type:'file'}` (PGLite pattern) | Like napi |
| Effort | M | M-L | L | M (scalar) to L |
| Risk | Medium-high | Medium | High for storage | High |

### Recommendation (open question for the repo owner)

Open question: does the first Rust engine need crash isolation and a single writer across CLI, MCP and cron, or is in-process speed the priority?

- If speed and simplicity matter most: napi-rs in-process. It uses a Node-API `.node` as `native-lock.ts` does, has the lowest per-call cost, and fits the Bun-only floor. Cost: a Rust panic or segfault takes the whole gbrain process, so every export must catch panics, and the store needs a single-writer answer (reuse `native/locks` flock and the owner socket).
- If crash isolation and one writer matter most: sidecar. It maps onto the existing owner-socket design, but it adds per-call marshalling and startup cost that must be measured against the one-shot read paths in `engine-connect.ts`.
- WASM is a poor fit for a storage engine given no mmap and no flock in WASI. bun:ffi is experimental and better kept for small syscalls.

My suggestion, not decided: start with napi-rs in-process, keep the engine surface behind `constructEngine`, and keep a sidecar as the fallback if crash isolation becomes a requirement. Both need the same engine-parity suite, and both need a Bun CI job because napi-rs does not block on Bun. The repo ships the napi-rs in-process option behind `constructEngine`, with a Bun CI job on Linux x64 and macOS arm64 (`rust-engine.yml:7-17`). No sidecar engine process exists in the repo. The shadow child is a read-comparison process that loads the addon itself (`src/shadow/child.ts:1-14`).

- Bun Node-API docs: https://bun.com/docs/runtime/node-api (summary read via search; full page not fetched)
- Bun 1.4 release: https://bun.com/blog/bun-v1.4 ; Bun 1.4.1: https://bun.com/blog/bun-v1.4.1
- Bun FFI docs: https://bun.com/docs/runtime/ffi ; https://bun.com/docs/api/ffi.md
- Bun executables / embedding `.node`: https://bun.com/docs/compiler/executables ; compile extraction issue: https://github.com/oven-sh/bun/issues/45054
- Bun WASI reference: https://bun.com/reference/node/wasi
- Bun napi async work and threadsafe function teardown: https://github.com/oven-sh/bun/pull/37075 (via search summary)
- napi-rs Bun support: https://napi.rs/docs/more/support-compatibility
- napi-rs cross-build: https://napi.rs/docs/cross-build ; CLI: https://napi.rs/docs/cli/build

Caveat: Bun and napi-rs facts above come from web search summaries of those pages, not from fetching and reading each page in full. The Bun ffi and WASI details were partly summarized from secondary result text. Items marked "not verified" were not tested in this repo or on Bun.

### Parity risks noted

- The kind discriminator is a closed union in at least six places (`engine.ts:922`, `types.ts:2374`, `config.ts:37`, `embedded-engine.ts:14`, `degraded-engine.ts:63`, plus `engine-status.ts:138`). The first three and `engine-status.ts` include `'rust'`. `embedded-engine.ts:14` and `degraded-engine.ts:63` do not. Missing one will type-check or silently fall through.
- About 29 kind branches sit in `schema-migrations/`, and `sqlFor` only knows `postgres|pglite`. A non-SQL engine must not silently get the Postgres or PGLite SQL.
- `migrate.ts:221` treats `pglite` as the transactional-migration special case; a third engine's transaction semantics must match.
- PGLite single-writer lock and owner-socket routing are the multi-process coordination for PGLite, and the Rust engine has its own owner lock and socket (`rust-engine/owner-ipc/election.ts:1-10`). A further engine needs an equivalent or CLI+MCP+cron can double-write.
- `degraded-engine.ts` is Postgres-only; a third engine has no degraded-serve story unless added.
- `engine-open.ts` `checkSettings` refuses every engine but `postgres` and `pglite` (`:94`), `'rust'` included, and `openEngine` is the embedding surface; an engine must be added there or embedders cannot open it.
- The `--pglite` init flag and `init.ts:170-181` "preserve existing postgres engine" logic need a parallel rule for each new engine, or `init` can flip an existing brain. The Rust engine has one in `src/commands/init-existing-engine.ts:15-17`.
- `docs/ENGINES.md:2138-2170` "Adding a new engine" step 4 (line 2154) implies a free-form engine string that the types reject; the doc is out of date with the code.
- Compiled binaries: lock addon embedding is verified in source (`native-lock.ts:105-107`, `release.yml:175`). The Rust engine addon is not embedded. It loads from a verified file with `process.dlopen` (`addon-loader.ts:13-16`), and `rust/smoke/run.ts:121-130` checks a compiled executable can load it. A file-asset sidecar is not built.
- Release binaries build only `linux-x64` and `darwin-arm64` (`release.yml:121-131`), while the lock addons cover 8 targets. The Rust addon's release targets are `linux-x64-glibc` and `darwin-arm64` (`addon-manifest.ts:30`), and no step in `release.yml` builds or publishes it.
- napi-rs Bun support is best effort upstream; without a repo-owned Bun CI lane on each target, a Bun regression can ship unseen. `rust-engine.yml` runs the Bun smoke on Linux x64 and macOS arm64 only (`:7-17`).
